madam prime minister, good luck in the presidency for the next six months! i would like to make two specific proposals to supplement or improve the commission's existing policy. in the energy sector, your presidency could take the initiative of listing all existing sources of energy in europe. we must know which energy sources are available in the european union and which of the investments we are making in the energy sector are not realistic, for example, we have photovoltaics in countries that do not have solar. the existing sources of energy must therefore be recorded. the second proposal is to take an initiative in the field of employment. the wall street journal yesterday reported on the great exodus of workers from the european union. we must look at this change in the fabric of the european union's labour market. i propose these two initiatives in the fields of energy and employment.
madam prime minister, i wish you a successful presidency. the six-month rotating presidency is a major symbol of the democratic functioning of the european union. but it leaves little room for any real influence on things. the danish presidency will be in office for six months, and this is a crucial time for the future of europe and its citizens. it is clear that the institutions of the union have been replaced by an informal directorate, which has not succeeded. we hope that the new president, mr schulz, whom we elected yesterday, will try to reverse this situation. greece, madam prime minister, is proof of the failure of europe's economic policy, followed by italy and all the other countries of the south. the european parliament has been a very active participant in the debate on the future of the european union, and i am sure that the european parliament will be a very active participant in the debate on the future of the union. take steps to improve the situation.
in writing. i would like to wish denmark good luck in assuming the presidency of the council of the eu at a crucial turning point in its history and to welcome the commitment shown by the danish government to the european vision. the presidency will undoubtedly face many challenges in its six-month term, notably the push for negotiations on the next multiannual financial framework (mff) 2014-2020, so that an agreement can be reached by the end of 2012. in the context of the mff negotiations, an important element is the adoption of the legislative package on cohesion policy for the period after 2013 which will contribute to ensuring a more competitive eu, through more targeted investment, and based on solidarity between member states. we are hopeful that under the danish presidency there will be as much progress as possible in these negotiations. finally, i would like to express my satisfaction at the fact that the presidency has made protection policy one of the main parameters in achieving its priority for a secure eu. in this regard, i welcome the presidency's intention to hold talks aimed at strengthening the eu's disaster resilience and protection of vital infrastructure.
in writing. the promoted international agreement on stability, coordination and governance of the emu consists of measures that continue and intensify the same failed neoliberal policies and in no way address the root causes of the crisis. in today's vote on this, i voted against the joint resolution by the political groups (epp, s-d, alde, greens). the resolution, while expressing doubts about the necessity of the agreement, avoids highlighting its main negative features and criticising the policies being implemented at european level. the 'international agreement' effectively institutionalizes austerity policies, degrades the quality of life of the majority of european citizens, increases inequalities between member states and creates a multi-speed europe. the main concern is the need to maintain fiscal discipline at all costs (e.g. cuts in wages, pensions, social benefits), while the destructive speculative activity of financial markets and rating agencies is not curtailed at all. moreover, the provisions of the 'international agreement' undermine the democratic rights of europeans by minimizing the possibility for elected governments to pursue alternative economic and fiscal policies. the left group, in its alternative motion for a resolution, raised the above issues and also the need for extensive public consultation (e.g. referendums) in the event of changes in the treaties.
mr president, it is true that in durban there was a change. your strategy worked out very well, commissioner: the idea of a roadmap that you were proposing gathered alliances and the eu was put back at the centre of the negotiations. i am proud of what you did and i would like to congratulate you on it. but it is also true that there are things which make me, as an eu citizen, ashamed. we told our allies and other people who gathered around our proposal that we would deliver kyoto ii if they delivered the roadmap. they delivered their part, the roadmap, and we did not deliver kyoto ii. we did not deliver something legally binding, and while i know that this is not your fault – it is because we could not reach a common decision on the set-asides and other issues – the bare fact, in terms of continuing to gather allies, is that we have not delivered kyoto ii.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. ladies and gentlemen, we are one step closer to an important agreement on the improvement of the management of electrical and electronic waste. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is not a radical approach to the problem but it is certainly an important step. as the green group we have in particular called for the collection targets to be increased and calculated on the basis of the waste generated, for distributors to be obliged to collect electrical and electronic waste without any obligation to buy from the consumer, and for stricter controls to be introduced on the reuse and export of such waste to non-oecd countries. we were the only group that made any suggestions about nanomaterials. we have not succeeded in getting the producers to commit themselves to report the presence of nanomaterials in electrical and electronic waste, but we have succeeded in getting the commission to assess whether selective treatment of nanomaterials in such waste is necessary. i hope that parliament will vote in favour of this and of course tackle the low collection rates, the improvement of management and treatment and the control of illegal exports of electrical and electronic waste to third countries.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on thursday 19 january 2012 at 12 noon. written declarations (rule 149) the president of the court of justice shall be informed of the decision of the court of first instance.
the next item is the recommendation for second reading by the committee on the environment, public health and food safety on the council's position at first reading on the adoption of a regulation of the european parliament and of the council concerning the placing of biocidal products on the market and their use (05032/2/2011 - c7-0251/2011 - 2009/0076 ((cod)) (rapporteur: christa klaß) (a7-0336/2011).
member of the commission. − mr president, even if it is difficult to catch the eye, i will try to do my part. i would like to thank everybody for their support. many members made quite substantial comments with which i fully agree, and i will not repeat them. on the question of sustainable use: for the record, we are in favour but we still need to work on the review of active substances used in biocides. we will have a lot of work to do to implement this regulation – delegated acts, guidance documents and so on. therefore, let us focus firstly on the regulation and look at sustainable use on the basis of the report provided for in article 17a. secondly, a short comment on confidentiality – an issue addressed many times in relation to this proposal over the past two-and-a-half years. in my opinion, we have the right balance in the proposal between transparency and the legitimate protection of commercial interests. transparency was already a central principle of the commission’s initial proposal and, on the other hand, changes were introduced into the text limiting the amount of information which should be made public. finally, with regard to small and medium-sized enterprises, we in the commission are very sensitive to the needs of smes, and the regulation does include a number of provisions that will help smaller companies: the possibility of waiving information data requirements thereby reducing costs; compulsory sharing of data; the provision on tests on vertebrate animals; a simplified and much less burdensome authorisation procedure for biocidal products based on safer ingredients, and so on. finally, i would again like to thank the rapporteur, christa klass, for her excellent work and also for her cooperation with us, and i would certainly like to thank all of you who have stayed till the end of the evening with us.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on thursday 19 january 2012 at 12 noon. written declarations (rule 149) the president of the court of justice shall be informed of the decision of the court of first instance.
vice-president of the commission. - madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i should first like to thank commissioner cioloş, the rapporteur mr bové and the members of the agri committee for their report and also the authors of the oral questions on imbalances in the food chain. the prices of agricultural incomes and inputs have reached record levels in recent years, but they have not been accompanied by a corresponding increase in the price of agricultural products. although real input prices increased by almost 18% between 2005 and 2011, agricultural prices have been less rapid. agricultural incomes also fell by more than 10% in 2009 as a result of the economic crisis and only in 2011 returned to pre-crisis levels. the volatility of prices of agricultural commodities is also increasingly reflected in farm incomes and is exacerbating the decline in the bargaining power of farmers in the food supply chain. farmers are therefore facing a growing number of difficulties linked to the increase in the cost of inputs, but also to improving environmental and agronomic efficiency. what is the commission doing to help farmers overcome these difficulties? our work has focused on these problems, which are being tackled, on the one hand, in the context of the proposals for reform of the cap presented in october and, on the other, in the context of the high-level forum for a better functioning of the food chain which we have been launching for a year now together with mr cioloş, mr dalli and mr barnier. the recent proposal for the post-2013 common agricultural policy introduces innovations to strengthen cooperation between farmers and to clarify the scope of the competition rules. the proposals also aim to facilitate the creation of producer organizations and associations as well as inter-branch organizations. the compulsory recognition of producer organisations is a fundamental element in the european union. the commission's proposals provide for co-financing for their establishment in all member states, irrespective of the type of sector, through the second pillar dedicated to rural development. moreover, economies of scale will favour marketing through the presence of more distribution channels and the reduction of risks and this will also favour a better distribution of the same added value within the supply chain. in addition, our proposal on contractual relations in the milk sector aims to strengthen the position of milk producers. the subject of crop diversification, which is raised in the report, is also dealt with in the proposals for reform of the cap as part of the commitment to the environment. the european innovation partnership for agricultural productivity and sustainability and awards for innovative local cooperation in rural areas can help to promote innovative measures throughout the food supply chain. the partnership could include projects on the rational use of resources, precision farming and so on. the cap reform will also strengthen the agricultural advisory system, allowing for better exchanges of good practice and information campaigns for farmers and consumers. another important issue is seed, for which an action plan is being developed as a follow-up to the evaluation of the legislation on the marketing of seeds and propagating material carried out in 2010-2011. a commission proposal is expected to be adopted in the third quarter of this year. the report also refers to the purchase and rental price of land, as mr bové mentioned in his speech. the authors of a 2010 study seek to clarify the relationship between land prices and direct payments. the commission is ready to carry out further studies to better define the impact of renting and leasing of land as input costs in agriculture. the last point i would like to make, also in response to the point raised by the rapporteur, mr bové, before moving on to the other aspects more closely linked to imbalances in the food chain, concerns energy and water. improving energy use in farms is clearly a win-win solution for all concerned. in fact, this solution not only allows farmers to reduce costs but also has a positive impact on the climate. the second pillar of the cap provides for mechanisms to promote renewable energy production inside and outside farms in rural regions, the processing of agricultural and forestry biomass and investments to rationalise the use of resources as well as mechanisms for energy saving. the measures under this second pillar also offer many opportunities to improve water management. as regards imbalances and abuses in the agri-food chain, honourable members, they are currently aggravated by price volatility, a problem raised by the three oral questions which are subjects to which the commission pays particular attention. unfair business-to-business trading practices can occur at all levels of the food chain, and they pose a threat to farmers, small and medium-sized enterprises as well as their employees and often consumers. what measures must we take then? until now, unfair trading practices have been tackled at european level mainly in the context of business-to-consumer relations. as regards business-to-business relations, the union has adopted measures against manifestly unfair contractual terms and commercial practices due to late payments. the directive is due to come into force by march 2013, but i have recently written to all the ministers responsible to ask them to bring forward the entry into force of this legislation which is a concrete implementation of the small business act. we estimate that this directive will help to bring about eur 180 billion into the market. there is also a directive on misleading and comparative advertising, which aims to ensure fair practices and lays down rules for authorising comparative advertising. together with the communication on the single market act, the report on the monitoring of the retail market and following parliament's own-initiative report on more efficient and fairer retail trade adopted last july, the commission has planned to adopt a communication on unfair trading practices this year. this text should clearly define the scope and extent of the problem in the retail sector and emphasise the fragmented nature of national rules and their application. mr president, i apologize if my speech is longer than i had intended, but there are so many questions and so many questions, i shall be much shorter in my reply. the commission also consulted interested parties on their perception of unfair trading practices in the european business test panel and is currently analysing the responses received. once we have assessed the data collected, we will have to consider possible fields of action to remedy the various problems we have found. we also plan to adopt a communication this year on the directive on misleading and comparative advertising. at national level, some member states have already adopted important instruments to facilitate the management of business-to-business relations, contract law, policies for small and medium-sized enterprises, and legislation and codes of conduct against unfair practices. the commission has mandated the high level forum for a better functioning of the agri-food chain to work, inter alia, on contractual relations between undertakings and price transparency. representatives of agriculture, industry, trade and distribution recently agreed on a document on vertical relationships within the agri-food chain and principles of good conduct, which proposes a common definition of fairness in business-to-business relations. the document also proposes guiding principles for business-to-business relations, together with concrete examples of unfair and unfair practices. however, the measures to implement these principles have not yet been evaluated and therefore further, delicate work remains to be done. on the basis of progress in the coming months, the commission will take a decision: the commission will either continue to encourage such a consensual approach or, in the absence of satisfactory agreement between the parties, reserve the right to consider possible legislative action. at present, the commission does not favour one option over the other, all the alternatives are considered valid including the one suggested by the bové report. finally, as already announced in the communication 'better functioning of the food chain in europe', the commission has put forward legislative proposals to improve the transparency of the derivatives markets for raw materials, including agricultural raw materials. in particular, these proposals concern over-the-counter derivatives, markets in financial instruments and market abuse. these proposals, as you know, are currently being discussed in parliament and in the council. i thank you for your attention and i apologize again to the president if my speech was longer than expected.
the s&d group is calling for a new approach to the problem of the environment. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's agricultural policy is not distorted by the excessive use of the agricultural sector. first of all, i would like to stress that the proper functioning of the food market is inextricably linked to the proper functioning of the input market used by an agricultural holding and vice versa. regulatory arrangements are therefore required to ensure transparency in the formation of input prices and to ensure the application of, and compliance with, competition rules both in the input supply chain and in the agri-food chain. secondly, i would like to emphasize that, in line with the policy on the quality of agricultural products, the quality of traditional seeds should be linked to the cultural heritage of the member states. i therefore think it would be important to set up a european seed bank to store and preserve the genetic diversity of traditional plants and also to combat the loss of biodiversity in traditional varieties. thirdly, i would like to stress that the rational use of inputs and resources requires much more than the application of rules. it requires a change in farmers' mindsets, knowledge and skills so that they adopt the most environmentally efficient, sustainable and innovative farming systems each time. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i hope that the new common agricultural policy will take all of this into account.
the commission's proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use and the development of a european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use is a welcome step towards the creation of a european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use. i would like to thank from the bottom of my heart our good colleagues, maria da graça carvalho, giommaria uggias, mrs black, alyn smith, and all of you who have co-signed our declaration, as well as our colleagues inge and alessandro.
in writing. i voted for the motion. the amendment to the regulation establishing a community code on visas aims to clarify the rules governing transit through the international zones of airports in order to achieve legal certainty and transparency. this clarification, although technical and not changing the practice followed by the member states, has practical value for the facilitation of travellers by providing that third-country nationals holding a valid visa of a member state or a valid residence permit of a member state which does not (or does not yet) fully apply the provisions of the schengen acquis (united kingdom, ireland, cyprus, bulgaria, romania) are exempted from the requirement to have a transit visa at an airport. the proposal is part of the wider debate on the revision of the functioning of schengen. the european parliament will shortly be taking a position on the amendment and updating of the schengen code.
in writing. i voted against mr michel's report on the amendment of regulation 810/2009 because the correction of the provisions of the regulation, while not changing the practice already applied by the member states, reduces their bureaucratic burden and facilitates the movement of individual travellers of third country nationals who hold a valid visa or residence permit in an eu member state. it also simplifies the procedures followed by airlines.
in writing. the global forum on harmonisation of vehicle regulations is a permanent working group within the un institutional framework that promotes open debate on regulations relating to motor vehicles. all un member countries and all regional economic integration organizations created by un member countries may participate fully in the activities of this global forum and become parties to the agreements administered by this forum. europe, for its part, has an interest in ratifying, in addition to the other agreements to which it has acceded, regulation no 29 of the united nations economic commission for europe concerning uniform provisions on the approval of vehicles with regard to the protection of occupants of the cab of a commercial vehicle. these regulations will allow the same standards to be applied in many countries, thereby reducing administrative costs and thus giving a little help to european industry to compete more widely and more effectively in an economically critical period for the time being. that is why i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr moreira's report because i believe that the proposals of the un working group wp 29 will make a positive contribution to road safety, the environment and trade, which are the responsibility of all countries. moreover, unece regulations will facilitate the implementation of own standards in many countries, thus reducing administrative costs and making european industry more competitive.
in writing. i voted against mr simpson's report because i believe that non-discrimination between eu air carriers and ensuring their right of equal access to fly to indonesia are important issues. in addition, the report's approval will help to bring the competition rules into line with eu law and ensure that aviation fuel is taxed when international flights are operated.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr simpson's report because most standards (such as environmental protection) and icao requirements are compatible with european legislation. issues such as flight safety, air traffic management and environmental protection are of particular importance and require priority regulation. in addition, the adoption of the memorandum of cooperation between the eu and icao is a decision in the eu's interest, as it strengthens its position within icao and ensures the competitiveness of its own civil aviation sector.
in writing. biocides represent the third largest group of substances that are hazardous to human health and the environment, let alone products that people come into contact with on a daily basis. they are often non-biodegradable, in some cases carcinogenic, and highly toxic substances, and may be harmful to humans, animals and the environment when used and subsequently disposed of as waste. they enter the food chain, the soil and the water. although the current legislation is the product of compromise, significant progress has been made since it was first introduced in 1998. the following shall be regulated by law: - the drastic limitation of carcinogens and substances classified as toxic to reproduction; - the possibility for a member state to impose prohibitions or restrictions where specific circumstances so require; - stricter authorization; - improved labelling and the prohibition of misinformation; - a reduction in animal testing; - the replacement of dangerous substances by safer alternatives; - the implementation of separate safety checks and the labelling of products containing nanomaterials. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a regulation on the protection of the environment and the protection of the environment in the event of a serious accident.
in writing. despite the plethora of european parliament proposals adopted by the council, important aspects concerning the placing on the market and use of biocides remain unsatisfied. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of personal data, in particular by ensuring that the data subject is able to access the data in a secure manner. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work. this system has the potential to be an important factor in improving the availability of these products and protecting human health and the environment. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
in writing. the report confines itself to describing the problem of the fall in farm incomes, because it wants to hide the very causes that are causing the problem, which is the fall in farm incomes and the robbing of the income of the working-class family by means of fire increases in food prices. it is striking that it does not even mention that energy, fertilisers, pesticides, water, seeds, animal feed etc. are owned and controlled by international monopolies that set prices and take the labor of farmers and working consumers to increase their profits. subsidised community programmes promote the concentration of land on large farms, in the framework of the cap and its revision beyond 2013. water privatization makes it more expensive to use, as do rising fees and energy prices. the report supports the disastrous eu cap for poor and middle-class farmers and promotes the 'competitiveness' of agriculture in the eu, which means the concentration of land in large capitalist enterprises and the mass uprooting of poor farmers from their fields. the only way forward for the poor and middle peasants is the struggle against the cap and the whole of the eu's anti-people policy, its alliance with the working class and the self-employed of the city for radical changes in power and the economy.
in writing. josé bove's report highlights the acute and chronic problems in the supply chain of agricultural holdings faced by european farmers at a time when farmers' farm income is shrinking particularly as a result of the global economic crisis. the package of proposals referred to in the resolution contains general solutions to problems affecting the whole range of the supply chain on farms but also includes specific references to issues such as energy, soil improvers and plant protection products, animal feed, seeds for sowing. the content of the report's proposals to tackle supply chain problems is moving in a positive progressive direction to the benefit of producers and consumers of agri-foodstuffs with the aim of maintaining food security at european level. for all these reasons, i voted in favour of the report by mr josé bové.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr bove's report because i believe that his proposals are important and will contribute to improving the agricultural and environmental performance of farmers, which will lead to a more efficient use of productive resources. in this way, farmers will be freed from the centralized input industry and the cost of production will be reduced, resulting in an increase in their income.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because it is moving in the direction of supporting small and mediumsized farmers, even though it is not as strong as the original resolution we had tabled as the left european group. the resolution fails to get out of the logic of the 'necessity of regulating the competition rules' and does not include the amendments tabled by the left on the establishment of maximum commercial profit margins, in particular for large supermarkets, the immediate adoption of measures to stop dumping between member states and the maintenance of production quotas in the milk and sugar sectors. however, it is positive that it recognises the problem of unfair profit sharing in the food chain, in particular with regard to farmers' adequate incomes, and insists on the definition of abusive and unfair practices by dominant wholesalers, their supervision and the imposition of sanctions. finally, it focuses on small and medium-sized farmers, including family units, and the need to earn a decent income, produce food of adequate quality and quantity at affordable prices for consumers, create jobs, promote rural development and ensure the protection and sustainability of the environment.
in writing. i voted in favour of the joint motion for a resolution because the proposed amendments are designed to improve bargaining power, reduce the income problems faced by farmers and tackle the problem of imbalances in the food supply chain. i believe that the achievement of the objectives set out in this resolution of the european parliament will make a significant contribution to solving this problem.
in writing. the european union needs the development of a space industry to promote scientific and technical progress and industrial competitiveness. the galileo and gmes initiatives are a good start in this direction, but in view of the progress in this area of competing countries such as china and japan, support for these initiatives is essential. the economic crisis in the eu should not be a hindrance to funding for relevant research programmes at a time when, as the report points out, markets for space services are growing rapidly and can be profitable. it is an opportunity for the eu to become a leading force in this field, and that is why i voted in favour of the patriciello report.
in writing. the lack of food to meet the nutritional needs of the population is a serious global problem affecting the eu, where 110 million people live below the poverty line and over 15% of its workers earn less than 60% of the average income in their country of residence. worldwide, according to fao figures, 925 million people are at risk of malnutrition. in our view, food waste is not the main cause of the world food problem, but that does not mean that it does not exist. the report conceals the cause of the problem, which is the capitalist mode of production, which is not concerned with meeting the needs of the people for food but only with the profit of the monopolies, the support of the big producers at the expense of the poor peasants. what food is produced and in what quantities depends on whether the multinationals will make a profit without taking into account the objective of tackling world hunger. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. a final solution to the world food problem can only come by overthrowing the capitalist mode of production and replacing it with a socialist system, where food production is not for profit but for the needs of the people.
in writing. in the last 35 years, the world's food waste has increased by 50%. at the same time, the rates of deprivation in the eu, and particularly in countries affected by the financial crisis, are rising significantly. europe must manage its resources more efficiently. it is important to note that according to a recent survey, the average person in europe and north america wastes between 200 and 400 pounds [95 and 115 kg] of food a year, compared with the same figure in sub-saharan africa. it turns out that in developed countries, most waste is found in the final stages of distribution and consumption - mainly because of the overabundance of food production - while in developing countries it is concentrated in the initial stages, due to the lack of advanced agricultural techniques, efficient transport systems and infrastructure, etc. more efficient food management is a guarantee for more efficient food distribution and for feeding those who lack it. i therefore agree with the european parliament's call on the council, the commission and the member states, particularly in the midst of the economic crisis, to adopt strategies in this direction and with the proposal to declare 2013 the european year against food waste and that is why i supported this report.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr caronna's report because i believe that food waste is a problem which has not only european but also global dimensions. the european action programme also proposes correct and realistic ways of tackling the problem. i think that the vote in favour of the report is important because it gives the european parliament the opportunity to serve as a means of informing and raising awareness among the citizens.
in writing. food waste is an issue that, particularly in a time of global economic crisis, requires special attention and i am pleased that the european parliament is taking such an initiative to raise awareness among europeans. according to the report, people in europe and north america waste between 200 and 400 pounds [95 and 115 kg] of food each year, the highest number in the world at a time when malnutrition is rampant in third world countries. i agree that the fight against food waste must be a priority in the european political action programme and that is why i voted in favour of the report.
mr president, the latest council decisions contribute to the completion of the single market and economic governance. i ask you whether what we have seen here today shows us that we are actually moving towards a united europe. is our aim to become a europe of the united states on the model of the united states? have you ever seen a michigan official attack a debt-ridden california like a number of prominent european politicians? i believe that we are all doing everything possible to deconstruct the european structure by criticism, by comments, by silly proposals and by the rigidity with which it imposes policies on countries and therefore on people, on their citizens. we are watching procedures to circumvent democracy and democratic procedures, we are watching procedures to circumvent the laws of the international labour office. as a greek, i accept all constructive criticism of what my country has done and is doing, but i believe that all of europe is hiding behind little greece, as if greece alone has all the problems of the crisis, as if we have built the euro on a very good basis, as if no mistakes have been made by anyone. as long as there is a problem in greece, there is a problem in europe. we call on all of you to stop dealing with greece because we will do our job and we call on our german colleagues because a gap is already being created that has a name and a face; germany's. already the media in southern europe are calling germany a "young hegemon". i'm very troubled and scared about everything that's going on. we're all gonna have to join forces to move forward.
mr president, the summit could be seen as positive for growth and job creation, but it remains at a theoretical level. four years after the crisis began, there is still no firm decision to leave the european union. the stability pact, as we all know, was not really necessary on a legal level but it was obviously necessary as a lesson in discipline or even a threat to those who deviate from the rules. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the recession will not solve the problem in any country, especially in my own. the budgetary discipline and the austerity programme are absolutely necessary, but without growth they are a dead-end policy which will have disastrous results not only for the countries in which it is applied but for europe as a whole.
mr president, mr president of the european council, ladies and gentlemen, i know that you do not share some of the extreme and offensive remarks made by some of the loudmouths in this chamber about greece. i think it is pointless to address them or comment on their statements. but i would like to ask the other members to consider with us where the stereotypes, the flattening perceptions and the references against a country and an entire people who are being tested by the crisis are leading. i would like to point out, although my dear colleague mr brok has left, that i do not doubt the intentions of the vast majority of colleagues here, but i do believe that if we are sincere about wanting to help a country to complete its effort, then we should not offend it because, as another german, sigmund gabriel, said, when you put a people to choose between national dignity and aid it is as if you ignore basic lessons of history.
vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − i do not believe that we have been marginalised at all. i think that we have been extraordinarily active in the most appropriate way we possibly can. this is the country of iraq. we have to respect that. we have to work with the un and martin kobler, who i feel has been the subject of some terrible things in terms of what has been said to him and who is doing his real best to find a way through this. it is going to keep these people safe. we have heard lots of different reports about missile attacks over the period. the question for me is how to get them to safety and how to get them to their future. it is clear that they have to move – that seems to be accepted by everybody – and to do so in a way that is as safe as possible. i have made many offers to the un and to iraq to be as engaged as we possibly can in that and i will continue to do so. but i really do think we now have to work through what is inevitably a bit of a compromise, frankly, to try to get these people to safety. that is what we will continue to do.
vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − i am very happy to look at where people have been and this is part of trying to review the designations for sanctions, for the reasons you rightly say – to keep the pressure on and to make sure that they are targeted. but one of the big challenges for us is to make sure that ordinary people are not unduly affected, and that means having the discussions which we are now engaged in in minsk with opposition groups, with civil society, to try and make sure that we are reaching out to ordinary people. somehow we have to develop this in a way that helps them. that is why, for me, it is so important that the discussions in the february foreign affairs council reflect what we know and what we learn from our discussions on the ground with civil society, opposition groups, and so on, so that we target it appropriately. i do not want to hurt the relationship between european countries and people in belarus. i want to find ways we can strengthen this while, at the same time, continuing to ratchet up our sanctions and concern with the government. that is really the challenge we have to face and that means we have to think about the economic issues alongside the political ones.
madam president, madam vice-president, it has been almost a year since commissioner malmström announced to our parliament that the negotiations with turkey on the eu-turkey agreement on the readmission of third-country nationals who have entered european territory illegally from turkey have been concluded. i would point out that today, as we speak and have the opportunity to ask you questions, as happens every day, more than 320 third country nationals are coming from turkey to european territory, crossing the border into northern greece, specifically the evros region. despite the conclusion of the agreement and despite its approval by the council about a year ago, turkey has not yet proceeded to approve the agreement and certainly not to implement it because it is asking in return, at least according to the statements of turkish officials that we read, for visa liberalization for its citizens travelling to europe. i would ask you, madam vice-president, whether, in addition to what commissioner malmström is doing, you intend to take the initiative on this matter and whether you intend to do more.
vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − the first thing to say is that cecilia malmström will be following this up herself. she has not raised this directly with me, but now that you have asked me i will make sure that i talk to her about it. it is a general problem. we have to find ways in which these agreements can be properly implemented. certainly she is tireless in her work to try to make sure not only that she gets agreements but that they are properly dealt with. so, if i may, i will ask her perhaps to send you a fuller answer from her perspective as the commissioner responsible.

vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − we keep ourselves involved with these issues. you are right to raise the importance of the european union being engaged in what are important matters for the security and safety of our citizens but also, as you rightly point out, the reciprocal work being done with russia. within the external action service we have the capacity to look at these issues from a disarmament perspective. as you know, i attend meetings at nato of foreign ministers and defence ministers, and i will be at nato tomorrow. so we are engaged in this. our concern is that the work we do, as i was describing earlier – for example on a middle east free of weapons of mass destruction – is geared to our commitment to try and support all these initiatives that will reduce the numbers.
vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − thank you for all the support that you have been giving in the work that we are doing in india and in pakistan. as you know, i will be in pakistan in a few weeks’ time. the free trade agreement has a whole range of key sectors within it. i hesitate to speak too much on this because this is karel de gucht’s territory and i stray onto it at my peril – quite rightly too. what we try to do is to make sure that in each of the discussions there is a real dialogue with the industries who are going to be directly affected. a free trade agreement in principle is designed to support and enhance the opportunities of all countries and of all sectors of industry, but one has to be very mindful of the implications that there can be for individual sectors. so, if i may, i will find out more about where those particular discussions might be. for my part, my role was to try to ensure that we were moving forward in the bilateral relationships with india on security issues, on issues of human rights and on economic matters, in order that we have the strongest possible relationship with a country that we will be debating later in the context of what we now call the brics.

mr president, mrs ashton, i urge you to make further diplomatic efforts. i'm not sure western diplomacy is following the proper approach to iran. they deserve respect and have a 4,000-year history to consider. i would like to make three points. the first is the delicate balance that is required when we impose sanctions, as to who is affected more by them, the ruling elite or the people. i am afraid that it is the people who will be hurt in this case and that we will have the opposite result of what we want. the second concerns energy sanctions. energy includes multi-year contracts, 40 and 50 years, large infrastructure, rising consumer prices all over the planet. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. please continue your diplomatic efforts.
mr president, baroness ashton, today's debate in the european parliament is of great importance. the imposition of new sanctions against tehran was absolutely necessary, but it is equally necessary to try to exhaust all possibilities to bring iran back to the negotiating table. the decision by the european union to impose sanctions, although necessary, is not simple because it will have the effect of raising oil prices and because it will also create a problem for the energy reserves of many countries. the economic cost, especially for the countries of the south, will be high, taking into account the economic crisis. on the other hand, we must not forget that iran has the ability to channel its oil to markets other than the european ones. but there is something more important, because we have to make sure that the sanctions are strictly targeted and that they hit the iranian people as little as possible. i think, baroness ashton, that you should exhaust all means to persuade iran to cooperate constructively on the nuclear issue. but that should not be the only issue. the international community and europe seem to have forgotten the appalling human rights situation in that country. i think you should remind tehran of the matter and bring it to their attention again. in any case, armed conflict must be avoided at all costs. it is in no one's interest to have a conflict in the region of the already burdened arab world. i am afraid that the red line drawn by the united states on the closure of the strait of hormuz is dangerously escalating the situation. the european union is not only a member of the international community, but also a member of the international community. we all know that is not self-evident.
mr president, the embargo on iran's oil exports and the other sanctions that the council unanimously decided on are aggressive actions and an escalation of measures against the people of iran. the measures will boost fuel prices and corporate profits, while further exacerbating the capitalist crisis in the european union. the european union is once again becoming a world policeman, as dictated by its imperialist values and interests. the european union has a duty to help the countries of the gulf to develop their economic and social cohesion. the blackmail by the nuclear powers under the guise of iran's nuclear programme is a challenge. these same forces cover israel's aggressive policy and nuclear weapons, the countless nuclear weapons in europe and the proliferation of nuclear weapons in india and pakistan. i am sure that the workers will condemn these provocative actions. the joint exercises of certain countries with israel and the war against the people of iran must be stopped. the european union is a democratic country, and it is not just a democratic country, but a democratic country, and it is not just a democratic country, but a democratic country. on behalf of the efd group. mr president, tonight is a night of congratulations. i congratulate you on your election, i congratulate the rapporteur on the excellent report he has produced and i would like to say that mrs ashton has also given me a very satisfactory response to the points she has made. it is certain that the brics countries are the driving force behind global economic growth. the european union must take seriously the new political and economic weight that the brics countries are acquiring by exploiting the political prestige they are developing. i also agree with the rapporteur that there is a need for a differentiated approach for each country. the structures of the four economies are different, as are their experiences of the global recession. but it should not be forgotten that, despite the recent impressive economic growth in most of these countries, they still have the highest concentration of poverty in the world. moreover, although they represent 42% of the world's population, they produce only 17.4% of global gdp. the comparisons are currently in favour of the european union. the question is what happens tomorrow.

on behalf of the efd group. mr president, first of all i would like to congratulate mr iturgaiz angulo on his excellent report, which we discussed in committee and on which we congratulated him on its first reading. european citizens expect the public authorities to provide adequate and effective services, especially in the field of waste, which has a huge impact and harmful effects on public health and the environment. according to the un, the problems facing humanity by 2030 are: combating social exclusion, migration and environmental issues. i do not know if the hierarchy is correct, but this is a matter to which we must give great attention. the reports received by the committee on petitions on this subject are now commonplace. i therefore recommend, as the report rightly points out, that the 2008 waste directive be implemented, which lays down rules for the protection of the environment and human health with the aim of preventing or reducing the negative effects of waste generation and management. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we need transparency in waste management because waste management is a big economic event. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect.

mr president, while the report describes the environmental crimes committed in waste management and the practices that are harmful to the environment, it nevertheless covers all the factors that cause them. instead of proposing measures against the unrecoverable pollution caused by big business, the report openly advertises the destructive action of the industry's monopolies. parliament welcomes the 1998 directive 2008, which upgrades energy combustion that damages the environment and endangers public health in order to multiply capital gains. the same directive legitimizes the pollution immunity of industrial capital by allowing the mixing of hazardous and non-hazardous waste. the report refers to the role of organised crime in creating environmental risks in order to conceal the real class crime against the environment perpetrated by monopolies, the institutions of the european union and the governments of the member states. in greece, argolis, crete and many other areas, the health of workers is constantly endangered by the landfills that contain even toxic waste and operate next to their homes. the solution for us, the communist party of greece, is to reverse the anti-environmental policy of the european union to ensure the health of workers and the protection of the environment.
in writing. our role and duty as the european parliament is to respect the principle of sound financial management of the financial support granted by the european union through the community budget, that is, through the money of european taxpayers. respect for this principle should apply to aid to member states of the union as well as to third countries, in particular those facing the specter of a humanitarian crisis. in this spirit, i support the call to the european commission to further improve the efficiency and effectiveness of echo's control, monitoring and surveillance system. as regards the efficiency and effectiveness of the humanitarian aid managed by echo, i share the recommendations made in the report so that the commission can continue its efforts to establish a better coordinated and more coherent system of needs assessment. finally, i agree that the european parliament should have access to the final financial reports of echo's partners on the implementation of humanitarian operations. only through transparency and accountability can we ensure that the money of the community budget is spent correctly and efficiently.
the vote will be taken at the next voting time.
in writing. the economic growth of the brics (brazil, russia, india, china and south africa) and other emerging economies is expected to shift the balance in the field of foreign policy. the economic crisis has also shown that the eu must take account of the new special weight of these countries, both for economic reasons (facilitating trade, facilitating investment, etc.) and for humanitarian reasons (disseminating european values and experience and democracy). the present own-initiative report, which i voted in favour of, is intended to define the areas of action of the eu and to formulate a first set of ideas on the institutional role that europe can play in the new global political and economic circumstances. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to improve the quality of the information provided by the member states. it also has comparative advantages such as culture, geography and geography, which it must exploit in its efforts to reach out to emerging economies. it is important to state at every opportunity that the crisis must not distract and isolate the country from what is happening at the global level.
in writing. i voted in favour of parliament's proposal as it serves the long-term interests of the eu's foreign policy. these demand that the brics countries join the current multipolar system of global governance so that they do not act in competition with the eu. these countries and the other emerging economies, because by 2050 they are expected to have larger economies than all the g-7 countries combined, could gain a great deal of influence over foreign policy at the global level. such a development would have a direct and adverse impact on the eu's ability to do so, which would now face great difficulties in its efforts to uphold and promote the universal values it stands for.
in writing. i voted in favour of parliament's proposal because i believe that the eu should pursue a strict policy towards proven authoritarian regimes and not allow their leaders to use their power to abuse the state wealth of their countries for their own benefit. in this way, not only are democracy and human rights safeguarded in the countries of authoritarian leaders, but eu interests are also served in the sense that authoritarian leaders, their relatives and associates are not allowed to do business within the eu and gain the ability to influence its policy.
in writing. the eu's total contribution to humanitarian aid is around €3 billion (64% from the member states and 36% from the commission), making it the world's largest donor. echo, the european commission's directorate-general responsible for the implementation of eu humanitarian aid, is tasked with the role of distributing a significant share of these resources through ngos, un agencies, international organisations and specialised services of the member states, and in 2010 managed €889 million. this initiative sets out in detail the financial data of the agency and the precise allocation of expenditure, and details the results of the directorate's periodic evaluations and the process of monitoring and implementing the humanitarian programmes for which european funds have been allocated. the report is therefore a good report on the accountability of the directorate-general and the transparency of the procedures, and that is why i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. i voted in favour of the rapporteur's proposal because i believe it is in europe's interest to have a coherent approach to the issue of collective actions. the spirit of european union law requires that victims of unfair practices, whether individuals or companies, are able to effectively exercise their rights under eu law and to be able to claim compensation for the damage suffered in the event of a breach of european law. it is absolutely essential that the eu's collective action system be based on respect for european culture. this will, among other things, increase consumer confidence and smooth the functioning of the internal market.
in writing. nutrition and health claims made on foods should not be misleading as to the characteristics, nature and identity of the food. the new nutrition claim proposed by the european commission, claiming to be for the benefit of consumers, is vague and raises reasonable doubts. this is a misleading claim which does not ensure a high level of consumer protection. it is an unacceptable proposal of expediency by the commission which confuses consumers and sets a dangerous precedent. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the food industry is able to compete with the consumer. it is not the first time that the european commission has been seen as a market-maker for food businesses. i voted against the european parliament's rejection of the motion for a resolution because i believe that the european commission's proposal opens up the way in a scandalous way for more and more unsafe food to end up on our plate. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of consumers' health and safety is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area.
in writing. in today's vote on the recent council conclusions and on the international agreement on a fiscal stability union, i voted against the joint resolution of the political groups (epp, s-d, alde, greens). the measures contained in the agreement fail to address the root causes of the economic crisis. on the contrary, they institutionalize the austerity policies that are driving europe into recession and its citizens into economic and social misery. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the budgetary authority is able to exercise its powers in a way that is compatible with the treaty. the joint resolution not only does not reject the international agreement but supports recent changes to the architecture of emu (economic governance, european semester, euro pact). these changes are the most reactionary, undemocratic and extreme neoliberal response to the current crisis. the european parliament, in its resolution of 15 june on the situation in the former yugoslavia, calls on the council to adopt a common position on the situation in the former yugoslavia and to adopt a common position on the situation in the former yugoslavia.
in writing. the own-initiative report, which i voted in favour of, also raises the important problem of match-fixing (articles 55-57). it is noted that, following my presentation, there will be a public hearing at the end of the year in the committee on culture and education on this matter. europe, especially in the last two years, has been particularly affected by corruption in sport. the match-fixing scandal in germany, on which the commission consulted uefa, and similar phenomena in other european countries, such as italy and greece, are just a few examples. in the context of the increased community powers in the field of sport resulting from article 165 of the treaty, the commission has previously stated that it intends to support cooperation between the private and public sectors in order to find ways of combating 'fixed' games. it is important that the commission informs us of the results of these efforts and whether there has been any progress by the member states in this direction.
in writing. i voted in favour of the rapporteur's report because i believe that sport is an important activity and that it benefits society, health and the economy. i therefore believe that the promotion of sport is of paramount importance and that the eu's action can contribute to achieving this objective and to developing its european dimension. i also believe that the commission's communication and the proposed actions are intended, among other things, to promote sport and to help it develop. finally, i believe that these proposals will make a positive contribution to bringing more attention to issues that threaten sport, such as doping and violence (e.g. the various incidents taking place in the stadiums).
in writing. the report is dangerous and disorienting for working class and popular women, as it calls them to become victims and perpetrators of the imperialist barbarity of war. while the report acknowledges that the largest number of casualties in wars are women, it proposes as a solution to increase the number of female soldiers in imperialist armies and interventions, as it claims that this will reduce aggression in armed conflicts. this is a daring challenge! the aggressiveness of imperialist armies is not a question of racial composition of the army, but an inherent characteristic of imperialism. their wars are born of the same capitalist system that is born and reproduced through them. it is the eu itself and the governments of the member states that are actively involved in the imperialist division of the world to ensure the profits of the plutocracy that murders women, men and children in imperialist wars across the planet from africa, asia and the middle east to the balkans. the kke is fighting so that the anti-imperialist and militant women's movement can create the conditions for the overthrow of the capitalist system that gives birth to imperialist barbarity.
in writing. i voted in favour of the own-initiative report, which gives a comprehensive picture of the problems and challenges facing populations, particularly in developing countries, in accessing electricity. the report rightly notes that access to modern energy services is an absolute prerequisite for poverty reduction, sustainable human development and the achievement of the un millennium development goals. it is significant that over the next two decades, significant global investments in the energy sector are forecast to reach $26 trillion, when just 3% of this amount would be sufficient to achieve universal access to energy for the human population by the year 2030. however, many developing countries have failed to improve access to electricity for the poor, mainly because this investment is not a profitable business if there are subsidies. meanwhile, as the financial aid provided by the eu, the world's third largest donor, has been too small to provide access to energy for the poor, the report proposes some targeted measures to make europe's aid more effective for citizens in developing countries who lack access to energy.
member of the commission. - madam president, i would like to thank the committee on economic and financial affairs, its chairman, mrs bowles, and i would ask you, corien wortmann-kool, to convey to mrs sari essayah, very warmly, my thanks for the work she has done in close consultation with your fictitious rapporteurs, sophie in't veld, sven giegold and ivari padar. payment services are simply vital to our economies. without a fully functioning payments market, we cannot have a fully functioning internal market for trade and growth. the regulation under discussion is intended to speed up the implementation of sepa, as corien wortmann-kool has said, the single area for payments in euro. the sepa project aims to develop and is developing euro payment services which are no longer fragmented from one country to another but are common to the whole of the union, and it replaces domestic payment services. this is the very example, ladies and gentlemen, of financial regulation for the benefit of those in the real economy: sepa will make crossborder payments as easy as domestic payments and will bring greater reliability and security to european citizens and businesses. the latter will benefit from more competition in this area and - i think so - from lower costs of payments. this should also stimulate innovation and give the internal market an additional concrete dimension. i welcome the fact that this compromise is a step forward from the commission's original proposal. this is further proof of the real usefulness of the work we are doing - as you said, mrs wortmann-kool - in good faith, especially at the time of the trilogues. i am pleased to note that many of the commission's texts have been improved and clarified by the work of your assembly. the report of the committee on economic and monetary affairs, prepared by mrs essayah, has therefore contributed greatly to improving our proposal. the new text is more favourable to consumers. i will give two examples: firstly, the planned disappearance of the bic code from 2014, i.e. less information to be provided by the consumer to make a transfer, whether it be a domestic or international transfer. secondly, the elimination of surcharges for all crossborder payments, and this as soon as the regulation comes into force. of course, the commission regretted the abandonment of the principle of two separate migration dates for transfers and withdrawals. in our view, this would have helped to speed up the migration process and rewarded the most advanced member states, providers and companies. however, a single deadline should also facilitate communication on the migration to sepa, including the use of the new iban account numbers. the commission is therefore, ladies and gentlemen, generally happy with the final compromise reached. we are willing to look at the governance of sepa by the end of this year - there is progress to be made - and also at the issue of consumers' right to unconditional reimbursement for withdrawals, under the revised payment services directive. these two points were each the subject of a statement of principle, accompanied by a third on the exercise of the powers delegated by the commission. as agreed, these three statements are included in the annex to the resolution before you and they reflect the compromise reached in december, word for word. in conclusion, mr president, with this regulation we are providing the payment market with the legal framework it needs. we are contributing to the emergence of a genuinely integrated market for payments in euro in the union. finally, we are offering european citizens and businesses a market where payments will be made throughout the union as quickly, efficiently and reliably as at home, and this, i think, at a lower cost, thanks to increased competition. this is the third point we have discussed since the beginning of this afternoon and it represents real progress for the single market, both for citizens and for businesses.

member of the commission. − mr president, honourable members, the posting of workers directive is indeed very high on our agenda and we have work in progress despite the fact that originally the idea was to deliver this at the end of last year. i have to admit there is a little delay with this package, but this is also about quality and we believe that it is very important to get it right after such a long period of conflict and consultation. we invested a lot in this consultation. last june, at a conference we organised in brussels, i outlined the structure of the legal instruments the commission would like to use to address the question of posting. this consists of two elements: an enforcement directive in order to ensure that the implementation of the original directive improves in terms of its quality, and a monti ii regulation on social rights, following the pattern of the previous monti i regulation in the context of cross-border trade, only this time in the context of cross-border services. the objective of the latter is to ensure that fundamental social rights are not subordinated to economic freedoms and vice-versa, and that there are further clarifications about the powers of various courts if industrial action takes place. but i think it is very important to stress that by reopening the original directive we would not go very far; in fact we would undermine the key objective of the original legal instrument which is to facilitate cross-border services and at the same time fight social dumping. the enforcement directive will facilitate further cross-border service provision for companies through better administrative cooperation, more transparent information and limits to national control measures. it will prevent abuses by clarifying the elements of the notion of posting, limit the opportunities to impose national control measures by codifying the case law of the court, and contribute to fairer competition and a more level playing field, for example, for smes. the key is better administrative cooperation. we have had many consultations with stakeholders and the various participants in this debate with a view to taking every possible aspect into account. there is still fine tuning to do, and the proposed date for adoption is march.

in writing. any decision on copyright must take into account that problems of different kinds cannot be dealt with by a general rule, as defined by the term 'intellectual property'. the falsification of products and works covered by intellectual property rights is related, firstly, to the division of labour at world level, and, secondly, to creativity as shaped by the diffusion of new technologies with radically different conditions of production and consumption. the inability to identify and manage issues related to finite material and infinite immaterial resources negates the synergy that the term 'intellectual property' attempts to achieve. the creation and strengthening of a monitoring body for these rights in the run-up to the acta trade agreement, which also faces heterogeneous problems, going beyond basic democratic rights and functions, turns the observatory into a police body for monitoring citizens' privacy and communications and undermines creativity and innovation in the union. the possible cooperation of this body with private companies prescribes the policing of the public and private lives of citizens by private individuals, a perspective incompatible with democratic principles and values. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the financial interests of the european communities and the european investment bank is based on the principle of subsidiarity.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. support for innovation and the efficient use of resources for research and technology require the safeguarding of copyright and the protection of intellectual property rights. in this context, the european commission's european monitoring centre for counterfeiting and piracy is being set up, which aims, among other things, to develop technical tools to prevent counterfeiting, illegal copying and piracy. the commission's proposal for a regulation entrusting the european anti-counterfeiting and piracy observatory to the eu office for harmonisation is therefore a step in the right direction, as this merger will allow the observatory to access the necessary funding and infrastructure to carry out its tasks and activities, while benefiting from the expertise of the office for harmonisation and thus saving around eur 40 000 in the eu budget.
