answer "blue card". i would like to begin by expressing the thanks of the working class, the popular strata and the youth of greece to the germans, the french, the danes and all the workers in the eu member states for the unprecedented solidarity they are expressing in a number of ways. the greek workers have given and received solidarity. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done.
the spring council, mr president, is an institution which deals with competitiveness, growth and employment and is of enormous importance. but if we do a little self-criticism, we will see that our success to date has not been significant. the unemployed have reached twenty-three million in europe and fifty years of prosperity and development in europe do not seem to be matched by the corresponding benefits today. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. mr president, we as greeks thank our partners for helping us in a very difficult time. i would like to point out, however, that the rhetoric of europe's leaders must not be offensive to the people. i am particularly concerned about the german side, which has the history and the capacity to understand what i am talking about. we cannot be different, we must act as partners. we are partners, and that means that in a time of trouble, we must help those who suffer. greece will implement the programme, but such a programme also needs growth rates. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
mr president, i would like to make a suggestion. we are all talking about greece all the time here and we are doing very well, but we must not forget that behind greece are portugal, spain and france. we all know the downgrades that are made by the rating agencies. we therefore know that a greek default would be a default on european ideals, a default on the european social model. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i therefore propose, since so many voices are being heard that have solidarity and social cohesion in europe in mind, that first a 'troika' be set up by the whole of parliament and then that we, all members and all political groups who believe in europe, all of us who want the european union to live, make an effort to draw up a resolution to show that the european parliament, which represents the european citizens, exists, acts and wants the european union.
mr president, i would like to make three points. the first concerns the troika of the european union, the european central bank and the international monetary fund. the proposals of this troika lead to austerity, recession and unemployment. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is a proud troika of the european parliament making proposals that lead to growth, hope and employment. we must take it seriously, commissioner. the second point concerns employment. i have here before me a letter from the president of the general confederation of greek workers to the president of the european parliament and to all the members of the european parliament. do you know what the president of the gsee says in this letter? it says, very simply, that the measures proposed by the troika are destroying collective and sectoral labour agreements in greece. what about this? what will you say and what resistance will you put forward, commissioner? i would like to conclude, mr president, by referring to a programme for young people. there's the youth on the move program. if you don't do something right away, this will be renamed "youth in stillness".
in writing. this recommendation concerns the conclusion of an agreement between the european union and the republic of iceland, the principality of liechtenstein, the kingdom of norway and the swiss confederation on the participation by those states in the work of the committees relating to the entry into force, application and development of the schengen acquis. the enlargement of the schengen area to include non-eu countries has brought significant economic benefits to european countries, as well as immediate practical benefits and facilitated the daily lives and travel of european citizens. the european union has a duty to ensure that the schengen agreement is respected and that the schengen agreement is respected, and that the schengen agreement is respected.
in writing. the treaty on the functioning of the european union provides that the council is to define the broad guidelines of economic policies and to lay down guidelines for employment. these guidelines should serve the objectives of the eu 2020 strategy and be consistent with the broad economic policy guidelines. the eu 2020 strategy, moving in a neoliberal direction, fails to respond to the needs of european citizens, and particularly european workers, even more so since the outbreak of the crisis. the main thrust of the programme is to increase competitiveness at european level by reducing the structural imbalance in industrial relations. moreover, the general orientation of the eu's economic policies today, and in response to the crisis, is to impose harsh austerity programmes and cuts in labour and social rights. at the same time, unemployment is at an all-time high. the employment guidelines within which they are embedded are therefore not an important tool for controlling poverty and unemployment. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to improve the quality of life of the citizens of europe. i therefore voted against the report.
in writing. i voted for the recommendation. the agreement reached in the council between the member states to bring the food distribution programme back to eur 500 million per year (from eur 113 million initially proposed by certain member states) is certainly welcome. in a very difficult economic period, with poverty widening in many member states, and particularly in those affected by the economic crisis, such as greece, governments and national actors should pay attention to the full use of relevant resources. in previous years, there has been a tendency for inefficient absorption due to insufficient information for the parties involved, bureaucracy or other relevant factors. it is a shame for the social fabric of europe that money which exists for such a sensitive issue and for which no national participation is required to be used, is being wasted through negligence.
in writing. i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution. the european parliament hereby formally adopts the view that the issue of bonds with joint and several liability constitutes a process of greater economic integration. the need to strengthen economic governance and economic development in the euro area is also recalled. in this context, the commission is invited to submit proposals in the near future to resolutely address the current public debt crisis, such as the european debt amortisation pact, the finalisation and ratification of an esm and/or eurobond treaty, and the joint management of the issuance of public debt securities.
in writing. i voted against the report because it is governed by a central contradiction in its analysis of the economic and social situation in europe. on the one hand, it rightly recognises that the austerity policies being imposed are leading to a widening of poverty, a deterioration of the welfare state and an increase in unemployment, particularly among young people and women. on the other hand, it proposes the adoption of policies, such as the europe 2020 strategy, which contain neoliberal positions similar to those being implemented today. the administrative proposals for reducing unemployment and protecting work which appear in the text only serve as a framework for rescuing workers and the unemployed from complete impoverishment. if the eu is to really improve the living standards of the economically weak, it needs to ensure the right of citizens to permanent and stable employment, to boldly increase wages, to expand the welfare state, to tax large companies, to have full control over stock exchange capital, to create an educational system geared to the needs of society rather than the needs of the market. unfortunately, the report does not go in that direction. the deep economic crisis cannot be dealt with by a policy of 'carrot and stick'.
in writing. the annual development overview on the eu's priorities for the next 12 months focuses on the areas of tax policy, financial sector stabilisation, growth and competitiveness, employment and the social impact of the crisis and public administration. it is noted, however, that decisions taken in this critical economic environment - with unemployment rising rapidly, with youth reaching 50% in some member states, with recession affecting much of the euro area and with europe's own europe 2020 targets appearing unrealizable - should have greater democratic legitimacy. in this context, i voted in favour of the report which proposes to increase the involvement of the european parliament and points out the insignificant role it plays in the european semester procedure, where, under the treaty, it has only the right to give an opinion.
in writing. once again, the commission's 2012 annual growth survey (ags) does not prioritise the interests of the citizens of the union. on the contrary, they are promoting the same failed neoliberal policies that led to the crisis, while, in the name of fiscal consolidation, they are imposing harsh austerity measures. recovery and sustainable development are incompatible with austerity, recession, unemployment and poverty, which are essentially institutionalized. the ep report is fully in line with these guidelines. it is limited to the request that the ep be involved in the preparation of the esc in the event of a revision of the treaty. unfortunately, however, the problem with the annual development report is not only a problem of democratic legitimacy that can be solved simply by involving the ep in the process, but it is also highly political and has to do with the neoliberal policies imposed by the dominant economic and development model it promotes. the only way out of the crisis is to reverse these policies and implement alternatives aimed at economic efficiency, social and environmental justice and sustainable development. i have voted against the report for all these reasons.
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, italy entered the global financial crisis in a vulnerable position due to its high public debt and low economic growth. but from its first day in office, the government of prime minister monti has shown its determination to address these two challenges and has already adopted several important measures. on the one hand, in december, it passed a new austerity package which is helping to shore up italy’s public finances. it should allow italy to achieve a very sizeable primary surplus by 2013 and put the government debt on a downward path. on the other hand, between last summer and recent weeks, a broad range of structural measures has been adopted on taxation, pensions, competition, the business environment and the efficiency of the public administration. the most recent measures on opening up closed sectors and administrative simplification can contribute to improving competitiveness and a business environment, removing long-standing bottlenecks to growth and ultimately increasing the adjustment capacity of the economy. in conclusion, the commission welcomes the decisive action being taken by the italian government and stands ready to assist and support that action as the government sees fit. a concrete example is the commission’s support in fighting youth unemployment by setting up a dedicated action team for italy and reprogramming structural funds until 2013. mr prime minister, as you have seen, you have strong support in the european parliament, and i am sure that you know you have a lot of friends – i would even say fans – in the commission. i would therefore like to tell you on behalf of the commission that we wish italy, your government and you personally all the best in your efforts.
president of the council, italy. mr president, since i have taken more time than i had intended in my opening speech - and i apologize for it - i shall be very brief in these concluding words, which i wish to devote above all to renewing my thanks to you, mr president, to the honourable members who have shown their support and esteem for my country, for the government i have the honour to preside over and for me personally. these words, these sentiments and these actions are a great comfort to us and stimulate us to try to govern italy ever better, and to try to make italy contribute ever more to a vigorous european policy, particularly in favour of growth. i also thank commissioner šefčovič for his words to me. i think i will answer only two points, but i will reserve the right to examine in the transcribed text, because of the complexity of the philosophical and systemic points which the speech presents, the words which mr speroni wanted to address to me and for which i thank him very much. the two answers i wish to give are one very short and one very short. the very short one concerns the comment by mr gollnisch. (the speaker now speaks to the honourable member in french.) i would like to say that i cannot boast of a democratic electoral legitimacy, and i assure you that, unlike you, monsieur, and others, i did not come to obtain this legitimacy. i was asked to do this job, which i find exciting. there's nothing i would have done spontaneously. and so i am sure that if you had been asked to intervene at a difficult moment as head of your country's government, you would have certainly refused, saying that you would have done so only if you had a democratic legitimacy. (applause from the centre) mr president, i also thank you for the warm welcome you have given me. i would like to turn to mr harms, taking his words as a starting point, in more general terms. many important comments have been made on the greek question: we've all been thinking a lot about greece. but i would like to invite you, as one of the oldest among you, to look at things in a historical perspective. the harsh treatment of greece today may well be considered excessive, and it probably is. but i would suggest that we do not forget that greek politics for many years - i say this with modesty and as a mere outside observer - has been a perfect catalogue of the worst practices of politics in our countries. when we say that this subject is briefly dealt with in the article to which i referred earlier that democracy is working in europe at the national level for the time being, but unfortunately not yet at the european level, the denial is twofold. the first: we are here in the place of democracy at european level. the second: we have to be very careful not to let the commission and the council of ministers, which are the only ones who can do this, get in the way of the european parliament's work. from this point of view, therefore, - even if we can consider the rigors to which the euro leads us with its budgetary discipline excessive and even if i criticize them too - it must be acknowledged that the culture of stability invented in germany, which we all absorbed with the euro and the maastricht treaty, has had the merit of setting the political activity of individual countries in a more serious way, because it was too easy in the past for politicians to comply with any need, accumulating public spending and public debt behind the backs of future generations and thus ruining their own countries. i would therefore like to look at europe as a moment of balance, which perhaps now has a pendulum that has been pushed too far in the direction of rigor, but as an italian i think it is a good thing that pendulum is there, because a country like italy, many years ago to join the euro, benefited from having to undergo better discipline.
the next item is the statement by the vice-president of the european commission/high representative of the european union for foreign affairs and security policy on the situation in syria. i would like to ask, on behalf of the vice-president of the european commission/high representative of the european union for foreign affairs and security policy, mr villy søvndal to speak.
president of the council. - mr president, first of all, thank you for a good, informed and very engaged debate. it's easy to understand, because the situation is terrible. i think that everyone in this room shares the feeling that it is intolerable to see people being shot at by mortars in residential areas, people going out to demonstrate peacefully being shot at, people being tortured, people disappearing, people having to flee. i think that everyone shares the same sense of disgust at a regime that reacts in this way to its own people. it is also one of the reasons why we have spent virtually every foreign minister meeting i can remember at the european council discussing syria as one of the major issues. it is important to emphasize that we have a strategy in the eu. we have a strategy that we are pursuing and that we will continue to pursue on how europe can best help the syrian people in the current situation. our first order of business is: stop the violence! don't shoot your own people. we shall pursue this view in all international forums wherever we have the opportunity. we are doing so at the un general assembly, where a resolution will be voted on tomorrow which will probably condemn in the strongest terms what is happening in syria. we do this in relation to the un security council. for, as several speakers have also said, it is essential to involve the un security council and to criticise the positions of russia and china on this issue whenever we have the opportunity. we cannot sit as a responsible member of the security council, as one of the permanent representatives, and just watch things happen. we have a responsibility to protect, as some speakers have said. we intend to stick to that for both china and russia. i think it is impressive, not only when the european union says this, but also when the arab league does it and when turkey does it. i saw today that china is going on a tour of the arab countries. they're probably just saying that because they think they have something to explain. we will also use this opportunity to make our message known at the meeting in tunisia on 24 march, to stop the violence and find a political solution. because there is only one political solution to the crisis we face. finally, we will promote our position elsewhere, in all other forums where we have the opportunity. that is what we do as foreign ministers - that is what mrs ashton, the high representative, is doing. what are the options? i can understand your impatience. there are four options that we want to pursue. the first is the idea of a peacekeeping force from the arab countries, which they would like to implement in cooperation with the un. it is still a little unclear what the concrete shape of this will be, but this is one of the subjects we will continue to discuss when we meet in tunisia. then there is a proposal for international corridors. i would just like to say that we certainly do not exclude this, but it requires a very strong international community to be behind it. because with international corridors, we are talking about making our way forward. then we are talking about combat action. there have also been some strong statements about avoiding a devastating civil war. i do not necessarily think it is wise to bring more weapons into an area where there is a risk of civil war, a risk of the situation getting out of hand. in fact, today's debate has confirmed several of the things that are the eu's position in this conflict. firstly, we will continue to tighten the sanctions. we will do the same at the next meeting on the 27th. we are moving on to the number of areas and the number of people covered by the sanctions. we are continuing to provide humanitarian aid where we can and we are not ruling anything out in this context either. we are working more and more closely with the opposition and are also calling on the opposition to work closely together so that we do not have a quarrelling opposition. it is absolutely crucial if a new syria is to emerge. finally, i would like to comment on another debate, namely the alternative to assad. we have heard some say that this is ethnic extremism. i don't believe that shit. i believe that the syrians have the same desire as all other people to determine their own future in a constellation where we give them the opportunity and push for it. i do not believe that any country is doomed to have only the choice between dictatorship and religious extremism. i think that is also the lesson of some of the elections we have seen in north africa, for example in tunisia, where we are going to vote on 24 june. thank you for the debate. i will pass on the conclusions and the speeches that have been made to the high representative, mrs ashton, and they will of course help to shape the views that will be the views of the european union on the conflict and our contribution to finding a solution to it.

mr president, i too would like to thank and congratulate our rapporteur on his report and his excellent work. for another year, the debate on the guidelines for the administrative budget of the institutions of the european union has been taking place as the economic and social crisis is putting the budgets of all countries to the test. throughout the european union, budgets are drawn up on the basis of budgetary rationalisation and austerity measures in many sectors of the economy. in this respect i support the rapporteur's position and the need to avoid unnecessary and non-immediately necessary expenditure by clearly defining priorities and, where possible and useful, by redistributing expenditure, so as to achieve rationalization of the administrative budget of these institutions. however, like the rapporteur, i too would like to stress that the rationalization of administrative expenditure must take account of the need to respect the financial commitments of the european union, such as the accession of croatia, and the need to maintain an efficient administration that can respond to the increased responsibilities of the european parliament as derived from the treaty of lisbon.
the s&d group is calling for a new approach to the problem of the environment. mr president, the possible extraterritorial effects of the legislation of third countries, particularly the united states of america, and particularly in the field of data protection, are of course of great importance to the european parliament. it is imperative for us in the european parliament to know clearly, and from you to the commissioner herself, whether there is a breach of the relevant european union legislation on data protection in particular. it directly affects the rights of european citizens, it also has repercussions on the economic world within the territory of the european union. i would like to take this opportunity to repeat something i said in connection with your recent presentation of your proposals for the revision of the 1995 directive on the protection of personal data, which, i repeat, is in our view in the right direction. we are in a period where europe is being accused of lack of leadership. the european union has unfortunately lost its prestige and weight internationally and particularly in the field of foreign policy, but that is of course another story. however, in terms of data protection, europe is at the forefront and is showing the way to other countries outside the european union. i hope and am sure, after many discussions with you, that the commission and you personally will continue with the same dedication to work in this direction.

in writing. the report in question, which i voted for, concerns the compensation of depositors in the event of the bankruptcy of a credit institution. it is recalled that when the global financial crisis broke out, the eu increased this guaranteed limit from eur 20 000 to eur 50 000 and from 1 january 2011 finally to eur 100 000 per depositor. however, this is not sufficient to ensure full protection of deposits. it is clear that mass exits from banking institutions, a major factor in the euro crisis, can destabilise the banking system in a very short time. this directive seeks to establish a deposit guarantee scheme by bringing together the european measures followed by the member states. the commission has also proposed that the greek authorities should be given the possibility of exercising their powers of control over the banks in the event of a failure to comply with the conditions laid down in the treaty.
in writing. the european parliament's joint resolution on the situation in syria comes as a result of the escalating and ongoing violent repression of peaceful demonstrations in the country which has been going on for almost a year. unicef's estimate of 400 children who have fallen victim to the crackdown, the un's estimate that the number of dead in syria has exceeded 5,400 in the last eleven months of the uprising, and the fact that thousands more civilians have been injured and at least 69,000 are in detention, all create the image of a humanitarian crisis in the country. it is clear that the international community has already been too late to take effective action against the syrian government, as different approaches and conflicting pursuits are limiting it. the european parliament has made its position clear with this resolution, which i voted in favour of.
in writing. the kke votes against and condemns the unacceptable joint resolution of the european parliament on the situation in syria, which is a gross interference in the development of an independent country. the european parliament is once again becoming a harbinger of imperialist violence by preparing the ground for intervention in syria. it is a challenge to present as defenders of people's freedoms the political staff of the monopolies which support israel and deny the palestinian people the right to a sovereign and independent state, which crushes the rights of workers in the eu member states. the european union has been unable to reach a satisfactory agreement on the terms of the agreement, and the council has not been able to reach a satisfactory agreement on the terms of the agreement. they are cultivating a war climate to justify open imperialist intervention in the region by the eu, nato and the usa. they are using the same method as in libya, where the european parliament, by demanding the imposition of a no-fly zone, opened the way for imperialist intervention and war against the libyan people. the syrian people and the peoples of the region are the only ones who have the right to solve the internal political problems of their country and decide their own destinies, by their struggle against capitalist exploitation, overturning imperialist machinations and interventions.
in writing. i voted for the motion. 95% of all published works, books and publications are never converted into 'accessible formats' such as braille, large type or audio. this fact alone constitutes a distinction. this distinction is contrary to the un convention on the rights of persons with disabilities, in particular articles 21 and 30, but also to the european charter of fundamental rights. and it's even more sad, our fellow citizens not being able to access valuable sources of knowledge for intellectual property reasons. this resolution seeks to support the convention on the rights of the author in books and printed works for the blind and visually impaired in order to facilitate access to these works as quickly as possible.
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, one year has passed since the terrible natural disaster in japan, which was one of the worst accidents in the history of civil nuclear power. this anniversary is an opportunity for us to convey our condolences to those who are suffering and to ask ourselves what lessons we have learned and what we have done about nuclear safety. long before the fukushima tragedy, the commission initiated a process of improving nuclear safety with the aim of developing a consistent european dimension based on high standards. the first milestone was the adoption of the nuclear safety directive in 2009, followed by the waste management directive in 2011. i would like to thank parliament for the constructive role it has played in the adoption of these two key elements. after the fukushima accident, the commission initiated a comprehensive risk and safety assessment of all nuclear power plants operating in the eu. this was endorsed by the european council and by parliament. as you know, the stress tests have been under way since june 2011. they are based on a common eu methodology. i listened very carefully to what ms harms said and i will convey this message to my colleague, commissioner oettinger. all 14 member states that operate nuclear power plants – and lithuania, which is decommissioning its nuclear power producing unit – are participating in the voluntary process and have provided national reports which are now subject to peer reviews. as you know, our neighbouring countries are also committed to the eu stress test methodology. switzerland and ukraine are participating. the results of the peer reviews, which are an essential part of the process, will be known by the end of april. the final report will be presented by my colleague, commissioner oettinger, to the european council at the end of june 2012. he has asked me to tell you that he will also be available for discussions on it with parliament. the outcome of the stress tests is expected to provide an objective overview of the nuclear safety situation in the eu, and will form a basis for legislative or non-legislative proposals that the commission will bring forward to improve the nuclear safety framework. the main areas for improvement could be technical safety requirements, nuclear safety governance and emergency preparedness and response. security threats, including malevolent acts, are an important part of stress tests, and a discussion group on nuclear security has been set up in the council of the european union to address these. the first results of that group will be published in its final report, also in june. this report will be annexed to the commission report, which will be presented to the council and to parliament. the implementation of the resulting recommendations is a national responsibility. however, monitoring and reporting mechanisms should be established to ensure the implementation of the necessary safety improvements. the fact that the results will be made public will help to ensure that all the necessary steps are taken to guarantee the utmost safety and security. the commission will closely and actively follow all developments. last, but not least, it is necessary to strengthen the safety culture and emergency preparedness worldwide. the stress tests are already acting as an international benchmark, and from the very beginning the commission involved the neighbouring countries, in order not to be alone in going ahead with these, but to extend the safety culture beyond our frontiers. international cooperation, particularly in the framework of the international atomic energy agency, is more important than ever. the commission intends to contribute to the updating of the convention on nuclear safety with the aim of increasing its effectiveness, governance and enforceability.
the debate is closed.

vice-president of the commission. − mr president, i do believe that this is very good news for european citizens. there are 450 000 successions per year which have an international background. we are now giving citizens the freedom to choose and we are reducing red tape. that is what we should do in order to allow citizens to exercise their right to free movement. the negotiations on this file, which represent the first cross-border cooperation in this area, have involved a lot of technical work and a lot of time in order for delegations to understand the underlying issues and the differences between the legal systems. an important part of this work went into trying to accommodate the concerns of the uk and ireland. the future regulation, which is now on the table, already constitutes a fair compromise which the uk and ireland should be able to accept because there are substantial benefits in it for their citizens. at any rate, even if we decide on this without the participation of the uk and ireland, they will of course always have the possibility of opting in later. on the information question, i agree absolutely with members of this house that it will be important, once this piece of legislation comes into force, to inform citizens about their rights and their possibilities. the european council of notaries has designed a website, with the financial support of the commission, in 22 languages, in order to inform citizens about what the new rules will bring to them. i think it is very important that notaries in all the participating member states will be informed and will inform their clients about the new possibilities available to them.

the next item is the oral question to the commission on legal training, by klaus-heiner lehne, on behalf of the committee on legal affairs, and juan fernando lópez aguilar, on behalf of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs (o-000059/2012 - b7-0106/2012)
mr president, allow me to give a true account of my personal experience. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the european parliament is able to exercise its right to vote in the european parliament, and that the european parliament is able to exercise its right to vote in the european parliament. the judge was then forced to suspend and even postpone the hearing for several months until he received the interpretation of the european court of justice on a matter which was nevertheless clear. unfortunately, the national court did not have the necessary experience and familiarity to judge the issue at issue directly during the trial. i think we often see examples of this in many member states. the result is more inconvenience for citizens, more bureaucracy and delays in the handling of cases by national courts and of course an excessive workload for the european court of justice which can certainly be avoided. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. if we implement pilot projects for the training of judges, will we also provide for a genuine evaluation mechanism? the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to improve the quality of the information provided by the member states.
the capitalist development promised by the european union and the bourgeois governments serves the profits of capital and not the needs of the people. unemployment is running at 25 million and spreading rapidly in all the member states of the european union. unemployment is a nightmare for women and young people. one in two is unemployed in greece and spain. unemployment in greece has soared to 23% and the unemployed have exceeded one million but in reality the situation is even worse. at the same time, the government in greece and the troika are proceeding with the abolition of sectoral labour contracts and are drastically reducing wages and pensions. the young people's wages are being cut by 32%, and this reduction is now accompanied by a 22% cut in the meagre unemployment benefit. life for the working class, the popular strata and the youth is hell. thousands of young people are taking the path of forced migration, wandering across europe and other continents in search of survival. but there too they face slave-trading offices and flexible forms of work and find starvation wages that do not cover basic needs. the young people, whether they live in their own country or are uprooted from it, must not accept the unemployment, poverty and misery to which capitalist barbarity condemns them. in such circumstances, common organization and struggle of the workers for their protection and relief is required.
mr president, a short while ago we discussed the report on gender equality. i would like to stress, however, that while the debate has been about improving the position of women, in greece things are going backwards. the slogan 'equal pay for equal work' seems far too far removed from reality and unemployment is now reaching unprecedented levels for both sexes, although the impact of the crisis is heavier for women. the social infrastructure, which is already weak in our country, is shrinking all the time, but this is affecting women most of all because the two sexes have different starting points. in the past, unemployment among women was higher than that of men, but today it has doubled. women were working under a labour dismissal scheme long before it was officially introduced. the wage cuts hit women hardest, who were already paid lower wages, with the gap reaching 20%. in order to tackle the problems of the greek crisis, there must be an effort to at least compensate for the loss of income with targeted measures to improve social infrastructure and social cohesion, if we are to still talk about gender equality. this is something that must be taken into account in any measures and policies.
mr president, the restructuring of the greek debt, with the largest ever exchange of government bonds, is now a fact. it is a great breath in a painful marathon that does not end here, with the greek people being the runner who makes dramatic sacrifices and makes great efforts. the greek economy is traversing the desert of recession and greek society the desert of unemployment, while commerce sees nothing but empty shops with locks on them. greece urgently needs to find an oasis of growth. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. but now it is time to apply the growth model. this requires new and practical ideas. the creation of a greek-european development bank is a serious and realistic idea recently proposed by the leader of my party to the german chancellor. i believe that the european parliament should support and promote this idea.
mr president, mr barroso, mr van rompuy, you who are here in front of me: two years ago, the leadership of the european union, together with the greek prime minister, put the people of my country through a huge experiment. greece has become a vehicle for the international monetary fund in the eurozone. the international monetary fund is now an institutional part of the european union. the greek experiment consists of reducing wages, reducing pensions, reducing benefits, reducing spending on schools and hospitals, cutting benefits, abolishing collective agreements. what are the results? the recession reached 7% last year, the unemployed numbered one million officially and one and a half unofficially, shops are closing, workers are not being paid, a people is offended and unhappy. greece has gone bankrupt and its people, its youth, its families are paying the price. the banks reap the benefits. and now, mr barroso, mr van rompuy, what did you decide in the council? what are we talking about today? you have decided to bankrupt the idea of europe. you have decided to bankrupt the idea of solidarity. you have decided to create a europe of iron budgetary discipline, a german europe. you have decided to create a europe in which we are all spitting at each other, a europe that is afraid of its own people, a europe that is afraid of referendums and the voice of the people. but, mr barroso, mr van rompuy, you must know that the people have not gone bankrupt and that they will soon give their answer and throw away such memoranda, such conventions, such treaties.
mr president, i am afraid that in theory we are talking about growth when in reality we are only concerned with budgetary discipline. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the euro against counterfeiting is a very good one. we must not forget that taxes kill taxes. it has also been found that tax evasion at european level is growing and becoming uncontrollable. the insurance problem is the next bombshell in the foundation of the system. unemployment is turning into an avalanche. unemployment in greece for the month of december reached 21%. one in two young people aged 15 to 24 is unemployed. the small and mediumsized enterprises which form the backbone of society are closing one after another. these phenomena are present in almost all the countries of the european union. the social fabric of europe is under threat. mr van rompuy, i congratulate you on your renewal of your mandate, but i would like to remind you and mr baroso that you must not rest on your congratulations, because congratulations are sometimes more painful than condolences. do your job and move on.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. that's encouraging, but it's not enough. we need binding decisions and action because words and wishes are not enough. if we want to be concrete and convincing to the european citizens, then we can and must link specific development measures, such as the financial transaction tax or the eurobond, to the new fiscal pact. but let me also address the various 'pythons' in this chamber and outside, who, invoking democracy and the welfare of the greek people, are talking about greece leaving the euro. it is clear that everyone can have his own opinion - democracy in this parliament is an asset. but for god's sake, do not invoke the interests of the greek people. the vast majority of 70% of the greek people are in favour of the country remaining in the euro and will not allow anyone to exploit the difficulties it is experiencing to promote the interests of the disintegration of the eurozone, as some systematically do.
the council's decisions show that progress has been made and that some of the way has been traveled. of course, there is often bad language. we all wish for a reduction in tensions, but there are no magic and pleasant solutions. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the establishment of a european centre for the development of vocational training. structural changes will help countries like mine in particular. it is the only way to deal with today's crisis and to have hope for growth. the budgetary pact, the european semester and the decisions to promote employment can and must be the guiding principles for the way forward. the european union has a historical conscience and a sense of the interests of the european citizens, so that it does not allow itself to be overwhelmed by the economic crisis for too long. the ideological content of the common struggle for all of us and the efforts for development can give hope to the young people of europe. the point is that the sacrifices have a real impact. i would like to stress that the greek people are willing to make such great sacrifices because they are not prepared to risk their presence in the european union and the eurozone, despite what the various 'cassandras' claim.
in writing. this report endorses the european parliament's agreement under the lisbon treaty to improve judicial cooperation between the eu member states, on the one hand, and norway and iceland, on the other, by implementing almost all the provisions contained in the 2000 convention on mutual legal assistance between member states and its 2001 protocol. the delay in the application of that agreement is due to internal national procedures which were not completed at the time of the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. therefore, and in accordance with the new legal framework of the treaty of lisbon, and in particular article 218 tfeu, the consent of the european parliament is now required. the implementation of the agreement, which i voted in favour of, extends to norway and iceland the rules on mutual legal assistance. these are rules that are already in place and being applied in the eu member states and will help to combat crossborder crime and promote cooperation with these two countries already in the schengen area.
in writing. i voted in favour of the recommendation on the draft council decision on the conclusion of the agreement between the eu and the republic of iceland and the kingdom of norway because i believe that extending to norway and iceland the rules on mutual legal assistance already in force in the other member states will make a significant contribution to the fight against crossborder crime.
in writing. the proposal for a regulation on the establishment of common rules on succession and the establishment of a european inheritance register, which i voted in favour of, is in line with the conclusions of the tampere conference and with subsequent efforts and initiatives to harmonise the rules of law between the member states. this initiative is a useful continuation and complement to a number of regulations such as the rome i and ii regulations and the brussels i regulation. the integration effort makes a significant contribution to the establishment of legal certainty in the common market and the single european area and underlines the constant orientation of the member states towards achieving a high degree of judicial cooperation. the extension of this commitment to areas of law which until now had been left to the legislative initiative of the member states alone, such as the law of succession, demonstrates the firm commitment of the european states to their common course and can only be encouraging.
in writing. the report offers a weak analysis of the causes of the economic and social crisis, its consequences for women and, inevitably, lacks a focus on policy change. i am not saying that the commission should not be involved in the negotiations, but that it should be involved in the negotiations. the report calls for equal representation of women in social, economic and political life and makes important proposals for improvement. specific and binding measures are proposed to protect and promote women's rights, such as pension rights, maternity and paternity leave, access to health care based on a gender perspective, protection from all forms of violence, but also equality in pay, political representation, promotion to positions of political and economic responsibility. the report's key point is the reference to the rights of women regardless of their social status, age, sexual orientation or ethnic origin. the commission is also called upon to implement its commitment to mainstreaming the principle of gender equality in the common european asylum system. finally, it is rightly pointed out that families take different forms (married, unmarried, cohabiting, heterosexual or same-sex, single parent and foster family) and are entitled to equal protection.
in writing. i voted in favour of mrs pietikäinen's report because i believe that women's participation in the decision-making process on an equal footing with men is an important factor in achieving equality, sustainable development, peace and democracy. i also believe that promoting gender balance in politics and foreign relations will contribute to stability, transparency and democracy.
in writing. i voted against this resolution because i am against the continuation and completion of the bologna process, which promotes the application of neoliberalism to higher education and transforms the university into a place where the interests of business and the market are served. the triad 'competitiveness-mobility-employability' aims at creating a flexible scientific workforce without fixed employment and professional rights, while at the same time strengthening class barriers in higher education, as it opposes the public and free character that it should have. the bologna process turns education from a public good into a commodity and homogenizes degrees and curricula, not based on the needs of society, but based on the needs of the market. at the same time, through the ects system for the acquisition of the first degree (bachelor), the envisaged postgraduate (master) course and the lifelong learning programmes as they are proposed, it attempts to alter the global scientific knowledge provided to young scientists by providing them only with fragmented and specialised knowledge with the exclusive focus on the interests of their employers. finally, the text does not mention that all the reforms implemented under the neoliberal bologna approach have the effect of underfunding scientific research in the eu member states.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. in view of the council meeting next month, where developments will be discussed and new objectives will be set again to achieve the bologna process, a number of general findings should be made. twelve years after the bologna declaration, the climate in the eu is very different. firstly, the economic crisis affecting many member states and which has led to the unemployment of millions of young europeans, qualified europeans, young people with a degree, requires a much faster policy on the recognition of their skills in order to encourage, among other things, their mobility. secondly, there is a trend in many member states, as opposed to the previous decade, towards a reduction in expenditure on education of up to 10% compared to 2009 levels. that is not to be overlooked. strong education and strong research in the european area are objectives of the europe 2020 strategy. it requires a robust education, not a substandard one. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr berlinguer's report because i believe that the contribution of the european institutions to the promotion of the bologna process will continue to be a driving force for the reform of the european higher education system. furthermore, i believe that cooperation between member states will contribute to the creation of the european higher education area, which will help in the efforts to achieve better employment, growth and the other objectives of the europe 2020 strategy.
in writing. the report highlights the difficulties of capitalists in moving towards full implementation of the 'bologna process', due to contradictions in capitalist development between states and sectors. in the mid-term evaluation phase and in the midst of the global capitalist crisis, 'mobility' is emphasised in order to serve the profitability objectives of capital. 'mobility' must be ensured from the time of study so that the new student learns to wander and acquires not scientific knowledge but skills that will enable him to change low-paid jobs without rights. capitalists seek a minimum level of knowledge, so that graduates then pursue degrees and certificates. the capitalists are trying to establish schools to strengthen the class barriers in education. they are giving financial support to lifelong learning programmes and cutting funding for social and student welfare. they insist on the equalisation of qualifications and a single qualification system to ensure cheap skilled labour for the greater competitiveness of the euro-western alliance vis-à-vis other imperialist states and associations. the committee's constant position is that a degree should be the only prerequisite for access to the profession because it is based on solid studies and a clear scientific subject. the only way to do this is through genuinely pro-people development, with a break from the eu which only the power of the working class and its allies can build.
in writing. reliable and high-quality statistics are certainly essential for the formulation of economic and fiscal policy by member states and at eu level. i voted against because the scicluna report, while clearly advocating the elimination of political intervention in statistics, takes an approach which leaves the door wide open in some areas to central political intervention by eurostat and the commission. in particular, it endorses the proposal for a systematic approach to statistical quality, i.e. the logic of statistics of agreed outcomes. the agreed results in today's eu can well be translated into policies of austerity and a dramatic reduction in labour and social rights. the commission and the rapporteur's reports are also in line with the agreement on enhanced economic union, which removes the tool of economic and budgetary policy from the member states. finally, the report wrongly concludes that the risks of statistical fraud have come to the fore with the debt crisis, overlooking the fact that in many cases statistics have been manipulated and used at will in the past, with the responsibility of both the member states' authorities and eurostat accepting them.
in writing. european statistics, in addition to being a tool for evaluating an implemented policy, are crucial insofar as their results can influence or revise the economic and fiscal policies implemented. i therefore voted in favour of the relevant own-initiative report on the strict quality management of european statistics. the need for more reliable, valid and free of bias statistics improves their quality, validity and therefore their effectiveness. among other things, this report outlines suggestions, experiences and best practices that greece should take advantage of.

vice-president of the commission. − madam president, in the name of the commission, let me call on the netherlands authorities to take all appropriate measures to fully assess the lawfulness of this website and to draw the necessary consequences. the netherlands authorities have to look into the matter from different angles. first, the framework decision against racism and xenophobia. the national courts have the responsibility to determine according to the concrete circumstances and context whether a concrete situation represents an incitement to violence or hatred on the basis of race, colour, religion, descent, national or ethnic origin. i want to make it clear that the commission cannot replace the assessment of the relevant criminal judge at national level. second, their national laws on hate speech on the internet. third, there are national laws implementing the 1995 data protection directive. here, it is the dutch data protection authority which is responsible for taking action in order to analyse the initiative, to investigate whether an unlawful processing of personal data is taking place and to take the necessary steps to correct this. this debate was about the unacceptable behaviour of a political party, the reputation of a national government, the rights of individuals to exercise their rights and obligations linked to the free movement directive, the rules commonly agreed by all governments to protect all europeans, and the values – everything we believe in and everything on which we are constructing our common future. i am very glad to have heard the political leaders from a wide political spectrum in this house combine words and forces in order to condemn unacceptable behaviour. i think we can be proud of the european parliament today.
president of the council. - madam president, i have to speak. i would like to thank the rapporteur for his report. member of the commission! thank you very much for the debate today. i have to be brief, out of respect for the agenda. many important issues and problems have been raised today and i, as president-in-office of the council, have noted both views and concerns. i would like to repeat that the council has not had the opportunity to discuss the question of the handling of internet sites by the member states. but i would also like to make it very clear today that the council of ministers fully and completely respects the principle that no citizen of the union should be discriminated against on grounds of his or her origin. it is a very fundamental principle. it is part of the eu's dna. it is part of our common values and history. i therefore expect all member states to take the necessary measures to ensure that the fundamental rights of all eu citizens are respected and enforced. this applies both to discrimination and to freedom of expression.
minister wammen, thank you very much, and thank you all, dear colleagues, for this extremely interesting discussion. η συζήτηση έληξε. η ψηφοφορία θα διεξαχθεί την πέμπτη 15 μαρτίου στις 12 το μεσημέρι. γραπτές δηλώσεις (άρθρο 149)
mr president, commissioners, we are aware of all the discussion that is taking place specifically about greece in relation to the oversized public sector and how much it affects its competitiveness, since that is what we are discussing today. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i read on page 36 of the report on public employment: the government has committed to cutting public sector employment by at least 150,000 over the period 2010-2015. to achieve this objective, it is expected to strictly apply the existing numerical rule between inflows and outflows of personnel, reduce its contractual employment and transfer to the labour reserve for over 15,000 personnel before their dismissal. i ask you directly, since the commission also took part in the preparation of this report: is the dismissal of public sector employees this year in greece being called for as one of the measures to improve its competitiveness?
member of the commission. − as you know the greek situation in europe is absolutely extraordinary and in the last two years the commission has made all possible efforts, together with other european institutions – and not just european ones but also international financial institutions like the imf – to find the right approach and to ensure that europe as such, the economic and monetary union, becomes more stable and in the end also more dynamic. we can of course discuss various aspects of this experience in the greek context and also in the wider european context which, in recent months, shows signs of sliding back into a kind of recession, at least in the majority of the member states, but it is definitely a very difficult time for the ‘programme countries’. in this context the key is to restore financial stability, to ensure that after the adjustment period there will be a return to stable financing of the real economy and then to ensure that even in such times there is investment, and this is what the taskforce in greece is working on. recently, just a couple of weeks ago, we had an opportunity to meet mr papademos in brussels together with the president of the commission and some eight commissioners working in the greek context. we are doing our utmost to ensure that structural funds continue to flow and the reforms that are implemented will result in the greek economy regaining its competiveness.
member of the commission. - mr president, i would just like to express our understanding of what mr papanikolaou has just said about the demands, the harshness of this national programme, as recommended by the troika, and the difficulty of its implementation. with the greek question, we are faced with an exceptional situation which has warranted exceptional measures. and both the european union, the european central bank, and the international monetary fund have played their part, as have the greek authorities, in agreeing together to extremely tough measures. i would like to link this question, mr papanikolaou, to the debate we are going to have during question time on the question of growth and competitiveness. these exceptional measures were necessary to get out of an exceptionally serious situation and this requires efforts on the part of everyone and first of all on the part of the greek people. i would like to express my confidence that your country has real assets, both through its tourist economy, which is one of the strongest in europe thanks to its natural, cultural and historical heritage, its extremely powerful maritime economy and its extremely large agricultural capacity. and so if the internal market, which is the area of the greek economy, open, is working well, i think your country should play its part in this internal market and find the basis for the revival of its growth and its employment market. that is why the case of greece is linked, like other countries, to what we need to do to restart growth, employment and competitiveness within the internal market.

mr president, i will speak about childhood diabetes, a subject which i follow very closely and for an additional personal reason, because a year and a half ago my now six-year-old niece - an only child - was diagnosed with type a diabetes. i will always remember the parents' first attempts, i will always remember her father's attempt to negotiate with his insurance company to explain that his fund had to cover the special equipment the child needed to measure sugar, because it had not been included in the arrangements they had agreed upon. i remember that when the child was taken to the public day care centre where he had been admitted, after the diagnosis and in accordance with the advice of the doctors, some tried to convince the parents that the child should go to school later so that he could, as they said, stabilize. i would like to say that even today in modern europe there is a great deal of ignorance about these matters. there are prejudices and misunderstandings which eventually lead to discrimination, which, as we all know very well, is not allowed. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the child with childhood diabetes will grow up like all children, will have a family like all children, will live happily like all children.
member of the commission. − mr president, it is obvious that many of our member states face a lot of difficulties relating to the economic crisis. i do not want to undermine the severity of the problems, and of course i do not expect to solve these problems through today’s proposal. as you perhaps know, however, the commission committed at the euro summit of july 2011 to provide more liquidity to the member states most affected by the crisis by increasing the co-financing rates in eu funds, and this proposal follows on from that. i think the proposal is a positive measure: we are trying to facilitate the absorption of the funds. we are trying to help member states facing difficulties, but of course the crisis, and all the problems deriving from it, are a very important issue that cannot be solved in the margins of this discussion. this proposal is limited in scope, since the purpose of these amendments is specifically to provide more liquidity to member states. parliament and the council have already adopted the necessary amendments for the european regional fund, the cohesion fund, the social fund and the agricultural fund for rural development. now this house will vote on similar amendments to the european fisheries fund. the sustained financial and economic crisis is indeed increasing the pressure on national financial resources as member states reduce their budgets. i share the rapporteur’s concerns about this. ensuring the smooth implementation of national operational programmes adopted under eu funds is, therefore, of particular importance. the implementation of fisheries operational programmes, in particular, injects funds into the fisheries and aquaculture sectors. we want to allow the most affected member states – those that have received financial assistance under the european financial stabilisation mechanism or from the balance of payments mechanism – to continue to implement their european fisheries programmes on the ground and disburse funds to projects. to achieve this purpose, our proposal would allow the commission to reimburse newly declared expenditure by an increased amount for the period in which a member state was under these support mechanisms. this increased amount would be calculated by applying an additional 10 percentage points to the applicable co-financing rates of each priority axis of the operational programmes. the total financial allocation for the period from the eff to the countries and the programmes concerned will not change; the proposal will thus not affect the overall budget. actually the new budget is in front of this house, and you can discuss this greater issue in the framework of this discussion. the cooperation between parliament, council, and the commission on this file has been excellent, since all parties were aware of the importance of adopting the proposal as quickly as possible. i expect that a positive vote in plenary will lead to the proposal’s imminent adoption, which will enable us to come to the assistance of those member states that most need this facilitation as soon as possible.
the s&d group is calling for a new approach to the problem of the environment. mr president, commissioner, i wholeheartedly support this legislation because it is a real act of solidarity. the positive aspect is not only the savings in resources, which in the case of greece amount to 20 million euros, but also the possibility of accessing the european resources that these countries are acquiring. many countries are affected by the crisis. the crisis is affecting the whole of europe, but today it is showing its most severe face in certain countries which are unable to give their own rate of participation. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. these resources represent an opportunity to finance the priorities of the fisheries fund, such as sustainable development, sustainable fisheries and the development of coastal areas, as well as support for young fishermen. this is very important at a time when the most severe aspect of the crisis is precisely youth unemployment, which is approaching 50% in many of the countries hit by the crisis. it is clear that this legislation touches the heart of the problem. the message i would really like to send to the mediterranean countries which will benefit from this regulation, and to the other countries of the european union, is that we must seize the opportunity to implement either the mediterranean regulation or other fisheries regulations so that we can guarantee that there will be fish, guarantee that fisheries resources will be protected so that there will be fishermen and there will be the jobs we so badly need.
on behalf of the efd group. mr president, i congratulate mr ferreira on his report with the proposals and amendments to the regulation and i note that i was particularly pleased to hear the views of commissioner damannakis. the acceleration and facilitation of financing provided by the european fisheries fund certainly contribute to the achievement of the objectives of the common fisheries policy. the importance of such a proposal is great because it is taken at an important juncture. we will soon have to decide on the new common fisheries policy of the union, which will define fisheries policy for the next ten years. the provision of adequate resources and the elimination of absorption failures is crucial to achieve the desired results and to ensure the conservation and sustainable exploitation of marine resources. as we have heard, the absorption of the fisheries fund's resources is at very low levels. i should like to stress that in greece in particular small-scale and coastal fishermen make their living from fishing, which makes it extremely important to increase resources and to facilitate the distribution of resources to fishermen. in addition to the reduced national share of the available resources, we ask you, commissioner, to propose that the new budget under preparation should increase these resources because, as we all know, fishermen are suffering. this report recognises the pressure and the ways in which the ongoing credit and financial crisis is affecting fishermen's income and the formulation of fisheries policy in general. the economic and financial crisis in the union is of course manifesting itself differently in the various member states, but all of europe is affected. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would ask that efforts be made in this direction and that money be directed where it is needed without prejudice to economic growth and job creation.
the proposal for a regulation does not serve the interests of small and poor fishermen but, on the contrary, accelerates their disappearance. the common fisheries policy, which is being implemented by the european fisheries fund, is destroying small-scale coastal fishermen by financing the destruction of small fishing vessels, with painful economic and social consequences for workers and for the inhabitants of the continental and island regions. the concentration and profitability of large business groups active in fisheries, aquaculture, fish processing and marketing is encouraged. the uncontrolled overfishing and destruction of the environment, including protected areas under the natura 2000 programme, which is being carried out with the approval of the governments of the member states of the european union, will not be stopped or stopped because the criterion of the common fisheries policy is to increase the profitability of european monopolies. the reduction in the member states' contribution to the european fisheries fund will not benefit small fishermen because the total amount of eu funding remains the same, which means that the number of projects and actions planned will be reduced and their funding will be even more selective. projects which fall within the strategic objectives of the common fisheries policy and the capitalist restructuring of the european union's europe 2020 strategy and which serve the competitiveness and profitability of european fisheries monopolies, thus accelerating the disappearance and destruction of poor and small fishermen, especially those in small-scale coastal fishing, will be eligible. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the results will be poverty, impoverishment and the persecution of small fishermen to increase the profits of european monopolies.
