member of the commission. − mr president, the commission is well aware of the threat piracy is posing to european – and not only european – ships and crews. it is unacceptable that criminal gangs are allowed to continue to take the international shipping lanes hostage and bring suffering to thousands of seafarers and their families – not to mention the threat to the stability and prosperity of the region and somalia itself. we all agree that only a comprehensive approach can bring a sustainable solution to the problem of piracy. containing maritime piracy at sea and ending impunity are two important elements of the eu’s comprehensive approach to combat piracy. others relate to building regional capacities and the stabilisation of somalia itself. also, we definitely should not forget cooperation with the maritime industry. when it comes to containing piracy at sea, the most visible of all eu counter-piracy action is eunavfor – operation atalanta. the council has just decided to prolong its mandate to december 2014. but how do we make sure justice is served, once suspects have been arrested by the naval forces? one way of addressing impunity is by the prosecution of piracy suspects in the region. the commission directly supports countries in the region which have accepted transfers for prosecution by eunavfor and established a joint programme with the un in support of judicial capacities. this cooperation is an excellent example of how commission programmes and common security and defence policy actions go hand in hand. piracy has to be understood as organised crime that is happening at sea and on land. in the long run, only the establishment of the rule of law, good governance and economic development will bring the solutions we are seeking. the eu is therefore strongly committed to supporting somalia in finding a political solution to the ongoing crisis. at the recent london conference, the international community showed strong determination to support the transition process in somalia. to date, the eu is the biggest donor to somalia, having committed eur 415 million for development aid through the european development fund for the period 2008 to 2013. on top of these bilateral funds come additional budget funds from horizontal lines and eur 77 million during 2011 for humanitarian aid. part of the new development funds will be directed to supporting the fishing industry, building alternative livelihoods and helping coastal communities and rural development more generally, thus tackling some of the root causes of piracy. the european union is also supporting the development of somali security capacities. in addition to the training of police officers – we also provide some financial support if they stay in the service of the somali government – we are the main donor to the african union peacekeeping mission in somalia, which is funded through the african peace facility. since 2007 the european union has provided eur 325 million and the commission is proposing additional support for amisom. as in the area of counter-piracy efforts, commission action is closely linked with council-led csdp actions such as the eu training mission to somalia (eutm). there is also an important role for the maritime industry to play in containing the threat of piracy. it is vital to implement international maritime organisation-supported ‘best management practices’ on board ships under eu member state flags. let me also assure you that the commission is following very closely the case of two italian marines, salvatore girone and massimiliano latorre, who are now in custody in india. we have been in constant touch with the italian government on this issue from the beginning. following italy’s request for assistance, the eu has raised the issue with the indian authorities both in brussels and in new delhi, encouraging a positive outcome as soon as possible. yesterday the high representative, cathy ashton, also discussed these matters with prime minister mario monti, who thanked the high representative for the eu’s efforts. it is clear that we need to address this issue in the broader context of our cooperation on counter-piracy and, more specifically, the regulation of armed protection aboard ships. we need to see what lessons we can learn to prevent this type of incident from happening ever again. the european union and india had already agreed in principle to work on these broader policy issues, and this is what we are currently also taking forward. but we have to differentiate between this case of eu member states’ security forces protecting commercial shipment and using private contractors for armed deterrence on board commercial ships and even distant fishing vessels. due to the immensity of the sea area where somali pirates now operate and the scarce naval resources available to protect this shipping lane, the industry has had no other choice than to turn to the services provided by privately contracted armed security personnel on board ships transiting the high-risk areas. this is an increasing phenomenon that we cannot ignore. the international maritime organisation has already addressed this item and issued interim recommendations but, as no solution to piracy is apparent in the short term, we believe this must be addressed in more depth. we are not confident with the idea of self-regulation by this new business in view of the risks involved in this kind of activity, which can potentially lead to the – sometimes lethal – use of force. in this regard we think that the imo is the international body that should lead to the development of very clear, stringent rules and, in particular, of a mandatory instrument. ensuring prosecution of pirates captured at sea only takes care of one part of the problem. it is therefore vital to increase the ‘risk/reward’ ratio for those who benefit most from piracy, and the eu has decided to step up its efforts to break the underlying business model. law enforcement agencies of eu member states, interpol and europol are currently cooperating, not only in attempting to trace any flows going into eu member states but also in collecting evidence packages against leaders, financiers and instigators. another element of the eu comprehensive approach is the support of the region. it is in the strategic interest of regional countries to engage in the fight against piracy. the eu is definitely keen to assist and enhance local ownership. this is why the european union has been a strong supporter of the eastern and southern africa – indian ocean regional strategy and action plan, which was adopted in october 2010 in mauritius to fight piracy and promote maritime security. support measures are under way, and up to eur 40 million of further support is currently under consideration. we have also been enhancing the surveillance capacities of our partner countries through the eu’s network of fisheries partnership agreements, which strengthen our relations with the countries in the western indian ocean area, and the regional surveillance programme which we have been financing with eur 10 million from 2007 to 2011, and which is going to be renewed. the critical maritime routes programme funded under the instrument for stability has also focused since 2009 on the security and safety of essential maritime routes in the areas affected by piracy. in close coordination with the imo, one of the projects contributes to the implementation of the regional djibouti code of conduct. i would like to thank the european parliament especially for requesting a pilot project on ‘maritime awareness’, which will provide technical tools to help the authorities to be aware of what goes on at sea. these programmes will work very closely with the planned regional maritime capacity building mission.
i am speaking on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, commissioner, no one could have foreseen a few years ago that piracy would today constitute such a serious threat to international trade and shipping, to regional and international security. above all, however, piracy threatens the safety and lives of thousands of seafarers working on ships that ply the dangerous waters of the gulf of aden and the indian ocean. right now, hundreds of sailors are being held hostage. they and their families live through dramatic hours, days, even months. the human dimension of piracy itself obliges us to take more decisive action. piracy has become a highly profitable business for pirates, high-profit, low-risk. their tactics are changing and their operational capabilities are being dramatically strengthened in a region of great strategic value to the international economy. three million barrels of oil a day and about 50 percent of the world's container trade pass through this region. the annual cost of piracy is estimated to be between 7 and 12 billion dollars. piracy is a strategic threat, it is a shared challenge that requires collective action. the causes of piracy are found on land but its effects are found at sea. the european union, with the largest shipping fleet in the world, must act more effectively. today we must take a decisive lead in the international effort against piracy. the next few months are going to be crucial. in coordination with the international maritime organisation, the un and nato, we must finally identify the flow of illegal and black money, resolve legal gaps for the effective prosecution of pirates, prevent the dispersal of pirate bases in a wider area and promote the establishment of maritime exclusive economic zones in the region, in accordance with the international law of the sea. i have listened carefully to what you have said. i would add that another positive step has been taken, namely the appointment of a question of the day: blue card. mr van orden, i listened to you very carefully. you refer once again to the efforts of the european union to combat piracy, to tell us in essence that the european union should not have a common security and defence policy. that's wrong and you know it. the first operation to be undertaken, namely operation atlanta, was the european union's. after a delay of many months, nato followed suit. the first reaction was european and results have been achieved. i think that you should be clear about what you want to do with your intervention and what the message is.
the absolute poverty and misery of millions of people caused by capitalism in africa, asia and latin america is being exploited by powerful international circles, which develop piracy as a highly profitable business activity that exploits busy trade routes and is linked to the trade in arms and drugs and to prostitution circles. the phenomenon of piracy and armed robbery at sea has taken on a great dimension, with thousands of seafarers falling victim. the qualitative diversification of piracy is characterised by the upgrading of the activity of the pirate ring, because the target is no longer limited to robbing the seafarers and taking food or various other items from the ship, but is extended to the capture of ships and crews, the sale of cargo and the seizure of ransoms worth millions of euros. the concentration in the horn of africa and the wider region of powerful naval forces of the usa, nato, the european union and other states, china, russia and india, has nothing to do with the fight against piracy. in the context of their broader geostrategic ambitions, piracy is used as a pretext to consolidate their position, to advance their military plans, to control against iran and the preparation of a new imperialist war find a naval machine in the region ready for war, reinforced under the pretext of piracy. the search for ships and crews to transport military equipment for major nato military operations in the persian gulf region is already underway. the issue is further complicated by the decision of the governments of the member states of the european union to station armed guards on merchant ships. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's fishing fleet is not disrupted by the threat of a catastrophic accident at sea. the solution proposed is hopeless and poses a threat to human life at sea, to seafarers and to the people of the wider region in general.
madam president, we should welcome the recent council decisions on a comprehensive approach to combating piracy, which concern, firstly, the appointment of a special representative for the horn of africa, which strengthens the union's diplomatic action in the region, secondly, the new operational concept which extends the activities of the atalanta mission and, thirdly, the preparation of a new mission, within the framework of the european defence and security policy, to support the development of capabilities at local level. it is important, however, to look at what has not been done properly in recent years. on the operational side, there is a real need to overcome the obstacles to the activation of the operational centre in brussels, because only in this way will central planning and better coordination between the various missions and activities of the european union be ensured. the athens mechanism should also be reviewed in order to secure funding and strengthen participation in missions. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work. but we must also consider the potential risks of operations off the coast of somalia and focus more on the need to tackle the causes that are fuelling piracy. as for the causes, it is well known that instability and poverty in somalia has pushed ordinary citizens into this illegal activity. the solution can in no way be purely military. in parallel with any military action, it is necessary to strengthen institutions and the rule of law and, above all, to make a greater effort to tackle the root causes of piracy, with an emphasis on information, awareness-raising and, of course, development and job creation.

what we have seen from poland. there was another question on shipping. we are now analysing options for shipping, as we were asked to do by the european parliament. last year we had a number of meetings with stakeholders where we were discussing options forward. so that work is being done in the commission. mr ouzký asked what the point of all this is without global cooperation. to make it very clear: there is global cooperation. it is not bringing the world fast enough to where it should be, but i think that durban was proof that it is possible to push international climate negotiations forward a little. one of the outcomes of durban was that all countries of the world now admit that we have to be equally legally bound by 2020. that has been a key ask for europe for many years as well as from this parliament, so it is just one example that things are moving forward – but too slowly. to focus on energy efficiency and renewables will also be in our own industrial, economic and job-wise interests. it is not either-or; it is both the international track and also what we do in our own region. to mention just one fact: even poland is importing energy from outside. so this is also about our economy and our energy security. last year in europe we paid eur 315 billion for our oil (imported oil – just the oil part). that is a lot of money. our trade deficit was usd 115 billion if you take us all together. eur 315 billion is a very, very large burden macroeconomically speaking. it would be good if, through intelligent climate and energy policies, we could reduce that kind of bill and at the same time increase our energy independence. i agree with ms yannakoudakis, who said that we should also look at some of the possibilities here and not just at the challenges. the final two points. ms gardini said: look at the eurobarometer. yes, please do: look at the eurobarometer. a new one on climate was produced last autumn, the first since 2009. never have the europeans been as concerned about climate change, and they asked for intelligent climate policies which also address energy efficiency, energy security and can also lead to growth and jobs in europe. so please look at the eurobarometer. the last point. mr cramer said that it was crazy to plan for the future. i think mr cramer is expressing a view that many would take when discussing 2050, as if we did not have enough challenges here and now. i think that it is totally irresponsible not to plan for the future – especially here, because the kind of investments you are talking about when it comes to energy systems, for instance, lock in your energy choices for the next 40, 50 or 60 years. that is why short-termism will not work there. it is clear, however, that when you are talking about 2050 there is – and should be – a limit as to how detailed the policies you can come up with are. what we are doing here is setting a direction. we are, as mankind, at a crossroads. where do we go? which road do we choose? do we follow business as usual – that is, do nothing and continue to build on fossil fuels, which we know will be more expensive, restricted and limited in the future, knowing that that will only make things worse? or do we use our knowledge, our technologies, man’s ingenuity to pursue a cleaner and greener kind of growth? that is the kind of choice we have to make, and we have to make it now. that is the whole point of the roadmap: the sooner you make that choice, the more cost-efficient the transition will be. neither i nor the commission is in doubt as to which way we should choose, but i am glad to note that a very large majority in this european parliament is also not in doubt. i thank you very much for that.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place today, thursday 15 march, at 12 noon. written declarations (rule 149) the president of the court of justice shall be informed of the decision of the court of first instance.

member of the commission. − mr president, i am really happy to be here today. it has, as the rapporteur has said, been a long journey to where we stand today, and i would like to thank mr tavares for his commitment, his endeavours and his hard work to finally achieve a result in this dossier. i would also like to thank all the presidencies that have been involved, and the danish presidency that has now brought it to a successful conclusion. many people have worked on this and thank you, mr tavares, for mentioning stefania pasquetti. she and many others at the commission did a lot of work and she was very committed. as you said, she very tragically died unexpectedly a few weeks ago. she would have been very happy today to see that we have finally reached an agreement between the three institutions, with overwhelming support from the european parliament. i would like this to be a tribute to her work. when we proposed the establishment of the programme back in 2009, our aim was to make sure that more refugees in situations of extreme vulnerability could be given more stable lives by resettling them in the european union. we proposed not only to increase refugee resettlement to the eu, but also to use and to pool our resources in a more strategic way. in order to achieve this, increased funding was offered to member states new to resettling refugees. we also had the idea of annual selection of resettlement priorities. this was intended to widen the scope of refugees that could be resettled under the european refugee fund. in this way more people can be given the possibility to have a life in dignity and safety, offering them a future in which they can live rather than merely survive. i am so proud and happy that today we have managed to overcome all the differences in a spirit of good compromise and to see our work on this file completed. today’s legislation runs to the end of 2013, but the new asylum and migration fund that we have proposed for the coming financial perspective, 2014 to 2020, includes a reinforced and ambitious resettlement component. furthermore, in line with the suggestions made by the european parliament, we are working this year on the implementation of a pilot project on resettlement in emergency situations which allows for even more resettlement to take place in the european union. as you know, we had to overcome a last-minute snag in the legislative negotiations on the legal basis for the proposal, so i would like to read out a specific declaration on this issue by the commission: ‘the commission, in a spirit of compromise and in order to ensure the immediate adoption of the proposal, supports the final text; however, it notes that this is without prejudice to its right of initiative with regard to the choice of legal bases, in particular in reference to the future use of article 80 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union.’ by establishing this programme, we are sending a very strong signal to the international community that the european union recognises the need for more resettlement of refugees and of our commitment to give shelter and protection and a durable solution for refugees. thank you all for your work on this; i am looking forward to a very strong endorsement in the vote tomorrow.

mr president, a few days ago some people were celebrating the salvation of greece and the eurozone. the troika which monitors greece - yes, it does - when it came to parliament, however, it appeared cautious about the future of the country, it predicted a storm for the salaries and pensions of citizens and hinted that the wage levels should reach 150 to 200 euros a month, "butter on the left's bread". vague reports of growth and no measures to support the grieving small and medium-sized enterprises in greece, resulting in 68,000 lockdowns in 2011 and unemployment of over one million people. obviously, the analytical approach overlooks the breakdown of the social fabric in the country and the poverty that the economic recipe has brought. the turmoil in society in greece and in other countries of the south facing the same problems is of no interest to anyone in this union, unfortunately. the commission has not yet adopted a decision on this matter. we have failed to manage the crisis, mr president. it is important to understand that the crisis is not limited to a few countries and must therefore be treated as a global and european phenomenon.

in writing. first of all, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on her report. the european investment bank is one of the instruments to support the real economy. in the midst of the financial crisis, the bank is playing an important role in helping to revitalise the european economy and support growth and job creation. i would therefore like to highlight the achievements of the european investment bank in 2010, such as the increase in its lending to support european economic support measures. i would also like to express my support for the position expressed in the report that support for small and mediumsized enterprises in the eu should be one of the main priorities of the activities of the european investment bank, since small and mediumsized enterprises represent the 'engine' of the european economy and create jobs. finally, i would like to stress the need to take appropriate measures to ensure that the bank maintains its excellent credit rating (a-a-c) and is subject to the same prudential supervision rules as all credit institutions. the bank's capital needs are therefore not limited to the capital needs of the bank, but also to the capital needs of the bank.
on behalf of the efd group. mr president, i would first of all like to congratulate mrs vălean on her excellent report. the report is based on the principle that every person with the nationality of a member state of the european union is also a european citizen. she's all right. citizenship of the european union is therefore complementary to, but not a substitute for, national citizenship. this is why the rights of citizens are at the heart of the report and the right to apply to the ombudsman, the right to submit legislative proposals on the initiative of citizens, and the right to vote and to stand as a candidate in municipal and european elections are inalienable rights of citizens. as a member of the committee on petitions, i would like to inform you that we receive a great many reports from european citizens about the obstacles they face in exercising their rights. it is therefore important that the rights of european citizens are clearly enshrined and described in the union's legal provisions and implemented in such a way that citizens have confidence in the union. the committee on petitions is trying to remedy these problems by putting citizens at the centre of its activities. the european union is a global community of citizens, citizens of europe, citizens of the world, citizens of the european union. i hope that we will be able to apply this principle.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr taylor's report because i believe that his proposals help to improve the service to passengers and to safeguard their rights. i consider the proposal to provide equal opportunities for persons with reduced mobility (pmo) and the existence of enforcement bodies (eo) responsible for handling complaints from passengers and helping to serve them to be important.
in writing. the financial crisis we are in has shown the dangers of derivatives and hedging contracts, as their use for purely speculative purposes has contributed to its outbreak. i voted against the report on derivatives because, although it provides for some measures which improve market structures to a certain extent and limit some risks, it does not really address the problem. it does not prohibit the use of derivatives or the involvement of so-called 'intermediaries'. no matter how many adjustments we make and how much we tighten up the surveillance, the source of the problem remains because we are talking about causes and not causes. in times of crisis, the role of the banking system becomes particularly crucial, as it can mitigate the consequences of the crisis as long as it prioritises the real needs of society. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european union's financial interests are not undermined by the failure of the member states to implement the acquis communautaire.
in writing. the jrc opposes the report and the commission's proposal to postpone for two more years, until 30 april 2014, the implementation of the directive on minimum health and safety requirements for workers against the risks associated with electromagnetic fields. even these inadequate measures to protect workers from exposure to electromagnetic fields are seen by the capitalists as an obstacle to the unfair exploitation of the workforce. the eu and the european parliament have therefore been quick to meet their demands, to refer the implementation of the health protection measures to the railways. the real character of the eu is revealed here as an imperialist association of monopolies to ensure their profitability and to shield their power. the protection of workers' health and life is a cost to the eu, to the political representatives of capital in the european parliament and to the civil governments of the member states, and has no value whatsoever in the face of the profits, the 'competitiveness' of the enterprises, which are built with the blood and life of the working class. the overthrow of capitalist barbarity, the demolition of the european union's edifice and the power of the monopolies by the working class and its allies is the greatest debt in its life and future.
in writing. the report seeks to strengthen administrative cooperation in the field of excise duties in order to tackle fraud problems. the emcs is already operational and the new regulation merely provides for a permanent obligation on the member states and the commission to ensure the maintenance of the emcs and its associated services. i voted against because although checking the movement of excise goods more rapidly and more completely through the automated system is a step towards tackling tax evasion and tax avoidance, i believe that it is not sufficient and does not solve the problem of tax evasion and tax avoidance. it is also based on the reversal of the union's policy on socially unfair indirect taxes on citizens, at the same time as direct taxes on all sources of wealth are being reduced. i therefore believe that it is necessary to put the problem on its proper footing and to implement a socially fair tax policy, with a development and redistributive role capable of tackling the serious problem of tax competition, effective control, taxation of offshore companies and the abolition of tax havens in the union.
in writing. the report on the european democracy fund constitutes an unprecedented escalation of the eu's war against popular and anti-imperialist movements and movements fighting to overthrow the power of monopolies it is the most vulgar and reactionary of all designs, since the purpose of the edf is to secretly finance, on the model of the notorious american equivalent, eu agents, counter-revolutionary parties and organizations, various agencies for their action in periods of upsurge of movements and popular uprisings, especially in times of great upheaval. the aim of the eu and the bourgeois governments of the member states is to manipulate the movements so that they can install "new" political forces, parties and leaders totally subordinate to the eu and the interests it serves. the edf is established to provide support and funding to a wide group of 'beneficiaries': all kinds of agents, parties and organisations linked to the eu and member state governments, secret and security services, ngos which are the long arm of imperialism, that is to say the whole spectrum of agents of imperialism. the eec has nothing to do with democracy and popular freedoms, but is created precisely to restrict and crush them. it is a tool for imposing the dictatorship of the monopolies, for undermining and manipulating popular movements, for imperialist domination and for imposing the dictatorship of the capitalist class.
in writing. i voted against the report on the establishment of a 'european democracy fund' because it is an attempt to create another intervention mechanism to promote the interests of the european union. its main objective will be to provide the eu with ways to support political parties, civil society and pro-democracy activists in countries under authoritarian regimes or in transition. in my view, as in that of the left group, this is a mechanism designed to influence directly political developments in third countries, but in the interests of interests which are different from the real interests of the people. moreover, this mechanism will be financed not only by the eu and the member states but also by private actors and other states. the european union has a duty to ensure that the european parliament is able to exercise its right of initiative in the matter of the internal market.
in writing. the idea and need for a european democracy fund arose in the wake of the escalation of violence in parts of north africa and the middle east. the european union, which has strong democratic values, must look for ways and practical measures, beyond the political, to strengthen healthy democratic forces in the countries of the southern mediterranean. the establishment and the precise actions of the fund are at an early stage, but the european parliament wants to see it set up as soon as possible, a position which i support by voting in favour of the report. the establishment of a semi-autonomous body such as this fund could support democratic processes around the world and where necessary in a way that is free from bureaucratic obstacles and sufficiently autonomous from eu diplomacy or the european commission.
in writing. it is a fact that the promotion of democracy in third countries is a key commitment of the european union. as the largest provider of humanitarian and development aid in the world, the eu can thus apply pressure on countries which flagrantly violate international human rights conventions and democratic institutions. i am in favour of the creation of such a fund, but i think that the european union should be very careful about who it funds through this fund, because interfering in the internal affairs of third countries requires very delicate handling.
in writing. the kke voted against the european parliament resolution because it is intended as a powerful lever for pressure and blackmail against the serbian people, an important step towards serbia's integration into nato, the eu and other imperialist organisations and the final secession of kosovo with very serious consequences for the borders, minorities and peace in the balkans and the wider region. it is characteristic that serbia, which has no fleet, will participate in the eu fleet off the coast of somalia. the resolution welcomes the savage capitalist restructuring being promoted by the serbian bourgeois government in power thanks to imperialist intervention, criminal war and the brutal eu-us-nato blackmail against the serbian people. it cynically confirms that the conditions for the start of the eu accession process for serbia were the submission of the serbian people, the handing over of the last defendants to the nato "court" and the embellishment of the dirty eurasian war under the cloak of "reconciliation with neighbours". the european union is also calling for the immediate release of all political prisoners and for the immediate release of all political prisoners. the council also reaffirms that the dismemberment of yugoslavia and the secession of kosovo as a eurasian protectorate was the second condition for eu accession.
in writing. after long reforms and painful internal changes, the efforts of the neighbouring country are finally being recognised and the way is being opened up for serbia's european perspective. greece has been an advocate of eu enlargement to the western balkan countries, and particularly to serbia, from the outset. unfortunately, today an amendment was incorporated into the final text with a reference to the former yugoslav republic of macedonia (fyrom) under the term 'macedonia'. we consider it unacceptable that an official text of a european organization contains a name of a candidate country which is not internationally recognised. the european parliament has also expressed its support for the commission's proposal to establish a european union-wide network of national authorities responsible for the protection of the environment. the european union is also committed to the development of the internal market and the fight against fraud. on the joint initiative of the leaders of the new democracy and pasok parliamentary groups, a protest has already been sent to the president of the ep, asking him to have automatic corrections of parliament's texts in cases where there are references to unrecognised country names in ep reports and resolutions.
in writing. the pasok parliamentary group voted in favour of the report on serbia's european path, although the final text of the report incorporated a reference to the former yugoslav republic of macedonia (fyrom) under the term 'macedonia'. greece, as is well known, has been from the beginning and remains in favour of the accession of all the countries of the western balkans, and in particular of serbia, provided that the copenhagen criteria are fully met. after long reforms and painful internal changes, the country's efforts are finally being recognised and the way is being opened for serbia's european perspective, so it would be unfair to vote against the report for that reason alone. greece has historically been and remains a strong supporter of its european path. the european parliament has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we are therefore in favour of the establishment of a european union with a single currency, and we are in favour of the establishment of a european monetary system with a single currency. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover the whole of the eu.
in writing. the eib is today one of the most important european institutions for supporting the real economy and development. it finances major infrastructure projects which could not otherwise be carried out. many of these also concern greece. the approval of this report also brings some important elements for the eib's 2010 activity: total lending this year reached €72 billion (of which €63 billion within the eu), the €30 billion target of the sme loan schedule signed and the €61 billion european recovery support package completed. the european parliament makes it clear that it should be encouraged to continue its strong action and to establish it as a 'european bank' which will play a leading role in the recovery of the european economy and, in particular, in the regions which need it most.
in writing. the european investment bank is one of the european union's major institutions, as its activities have contributed to the achievement of the union's objectives for more than 50 years. eib financing activities, including loans for structural programmes (spps) and support for european smes, are an important factor in supporting the real economy. in addition, the eib's contribution to furthering the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy and its contribution to climate projects is noteworthy. i therefore consider it important to support mrs ivanova's report.
in writing. in the difficult economic climate europe is experiencing, there is a great need for the european investment bank to continue the work for which it was set up. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competition' in the context of the directive. the report rightly points to the worrying divergence between european economies in terms of competitiveness and innovation. i voted in favour of the ivanova report, but i would like to stress that the eib must focus on more favourable lending and economic reconstruction in the member states most affected by the debt crisis, such as my own country, portugal and ireland, and in the countries of the southern mediterranean, such as greece, greece and portugal. these countries need to become competitive again.
in writing. i voted in favour of the valean report because i consider its report on behalf of the committee on petitions to be very important. particularly at a time when european institutions are being challenged and citizens of the european union are showing great distrust of its effectiveness, it is important that such a report shows that the voice and concerns of european citizens are being heard. the eu must prove that the free movement of persons is not a theoretical right and i am very pleased that the report also tackles specific cases of violations (rights of family members in britain, recognition of citizens' qualifications in other countries, etc.) by calling for them to be addressed immediately.
the s&d group is calling for a new approach to the problem of the environment. madam president, i would like to congratulate mr richard howitt on his comprehensive and very detailed report, and the mr president, on the occasion of today's world day for disarmament, we should learn from the mistakes made by greece in these matters. from 1974 to 2011, greece spent about $218 billion on defense and military programs. in fact, in the period 2001-2011, and despite the decrease in its defense equipment spending in 2011 due to budgetary adjustment, greece was the fifth largest importer of military equipment in the world, with $10.3 billion, after china, india, south korea and the united arab emirates. however, in the five-year period 2007-2011 greece was the largest recipient of german and the second largest recipient of french armament programmes. we must certainly learn from our mistakes, because those who have benefited from the armaments programmes are mainly the large arms industries, but also members of the greek administration and greek politicians who were responsible for the selection of the various armaments programmes. the arrest of the former greek defence minister, mr tzohatzopoulos, is the culmination of all this.
member of the commission. − i should briefly respond to the question regarding youth. if the vice-president wants to comment on the broader questions of growth and the reforms, he should do so. of course the action team has to focus on a particular group, namely young people. this is a critical question, especially in the south of italy. there are huge imbalances inside the country, as you probably know very well. i hope this will produce very practical, concrete results in the coming year. i think the vice-president wants to address the issue of growth and the reforms in a broader context.
vice-president of the commission. − indeed, in the past few months, the italian government has shown very strong determination and i must say an impressive commitment to take up the twin challenge of fiscal consolidation and job-boosting reforms. as regards fiscal consolidation, the effort made, in fact since 2010, is significant and has helped restore confidence in financial markets, not least thanks to the recent packages of fiscal consolidation that have been adopted by the government and supported by the parliament in italy. at the same time, the sustainability of italy’s public finances and of course the development of its employment depends on its growth prospects, especially in the context of recession, as is the case today. the policy response to tackling the long-standing structural weaknesses and boosting the growth potential in italy has been determined so far, and it is very comprehensive and wide-ranging. the rigorous implementation of the measures that have been adopted so far, especially to open up competition in product and services markets, to improve the business environment and to make better use of structural funds for investment, will be crucial in delivering their full benefits. the next crucial step is the adoption by parliament of the long-awaited reform of the labour market, which is necessary in the light of the important challenges of the italian labour market. as recommended by the council at its recent meeting, italy should embrace reforms that tackle the segmentation of the labour market by reviewing its employment protection legislation and revising the unemployment benefits system, as well as enhancing the system of active labour market policies. these reforms are crucial, not least because of the unacceptable level of youth unemployment in italy. the reform recently adopted by the government following consultations with the social partners has the ambition to comprehensively address the rigidities and asymmetries of the labour market, while moving towards a more integrated unemployment benefit scheme. this should make it possible to find a better balance between flexibility of entry and exit from the labour market and should give rise to a more dynamic and cohesive labour market.
vice-president of the commission. − i will leave my philosophical and religious beliefs out of this debate, even though i have to say that in my studies of macroeconomics the books of john maynard keynes figured very strongly. they certainly have plenty of lessons for all of us about what went wrong in the 1930s and what we should avoid in the 21st century. as regards spain – which was the specific point you made – you are correct when you say that spain faces formidable challenges, especially concerning youth unemployment following the end of the credit boom and the bursting of the real estate bubble. this adjustment started in the wake of the crisis with a rapid downsizing of the construction sector, a decrease in the external deficit and some improvement in cost competitiveness. unfortunately, i cannot agree with you when you say that last year in spain everything was done perfectly, because there was a major fiscal slippage of over 2.5%, from 6 to 8.5%, which, in more numerical, concrete terms, is equivalent to a slippage of around eur 25 billion. this slippage came mainly from the regional governments. therefore it is essential for spain, as part of its reform efforts, to take concrete action to ensure better sustainability of the autonomous regions’ public finances. i have recommended to the spanish government that they start applying the new fiscal stability law on the autonomous regions without any delay, because that is clearly an area which creates uncertainty and reduces the credibility of public finances in spain.
vice-president of the commission. − firstly, on the subject of spain: it is indeed essential that the fiscal policy stance of spain, as recommended by the commission and the eurogroup, strikes the right balance between the necessary consolidation of public finances and concerns about growth in the country. we are currently reviewing the economic and fiscal policy figures and stance of the whole euro area. we will present our spring forecast on 11 may and on that basis will make proposals for the economic and fiscal policy stance of the whole euro area and individual countries. i can see that we need to have further differentiation among the countries, depending on the business cycle they are in and on the economic and growth conditions they are experiencing, but it is too early to say today because we do not yet have the figures from eurostat upon which we will base the spring forecast that we will present in may. as regards greece, i would suggest that you attend the afternoon session at 3 p.m. when president barroso will speak about greece. we will adopt today, in our college meeting before 3 p.m., a communication on promoting growth and jobs in greece, which is a very important document outlining ways and means in all policy areas and how we can enhance growth and job creation in greece on the basis of the eu-imf programme. i would like to make one point concerning growth which is important because i know, mrs berès, that you are very concerned about this – as all of you and all of us are. i should like, if i may, to use my extra minute in this context because to my mind it is quite important. this point refers to the possibilities of reinforcing the capital base of the european investment bank. with the eib, the eu has a very powerful institution and instrument of its own for supporting growth and employment, not least in troubled times. the eib, with a lending capacity several times the size of the world bank, has been playing a key role in tackling the crisis since 2008, but it is reaching the limits of what it can do with its current capital base. to allow the eib to do more for growth and jobs, its capital needs to be addressed and its capital base needs to be increased. in our view this should be done by its shareholders, in other words by the eu member states. for instance, a capital increase of eur 10 billion would allow the eib to lend approximately eur 60 billion, which in turn would attract other financing sources for a total investment of eur 180 billion for new projects of infrastructure, innovation and so on. in addition to this, the commission and the eib are currently looking at possible options for using the eu budget to leverage the eib group financing capacity through risk-sharing schemes, building on schemes already developed for research and innovation with project bonds for infrastructure as well as for small and medium-sized enterprises with the help of the structural funds. so we are doing our part to look for innovative solutions to reinforce the lending capacity of the european investment bank. however, let me send a clear and constructive message to all eu member states, which i trust parliament can support. for the sake of sustainable growth and job creation we need more european cross-border and community investment in infrastructure, energy and transport and in innovation, research and communications, and i therefore call on the eu member states to provide, without any delay, additional capital to the european investment bank. i ask for your support for this very important objective.
member of the commission. − the enforcement directive recognises the importance of national control measures and inspections in guaranteeing applicable working conditions for posted workers. however, the freedom to provide services must also be respected. the court of justice decided that certain national control measures were proportionate in view of companies’ freedom to provide services, while others were not. in order to provide for legal certainty, the enforcement directive indicates clearly which control measures are proportionate and justified. new european electronic tools will make cooperation between member states’ enforcement bodies more rapid and efficient. this will facilitate and improve controls. i therefore believe that the provision to which you refer is in line with the agreed objective of this exercise, namely improving the interpretation and the implementation of the existing directive without reversing court of justice case law.
member of the commission. − i believe that the joint and several liability does not interfere with the question of collective agreements. this is about how various companies engage in cooperation by means of subcontracting. it is actually a way to guarantee that what is involved in various labour contracts will be delivered, even if the economic activity takes place in the form of posting in a member state other than the home of the given company.
i have listened to you very carefully, mr president, commissioner, and it is indeed very important that we support the mobility of workers which is aimed at improving skills, especially for young people. it is a different reality, however, and a different reality that forces young people in some countries with significant problems to leave their country because their country offers no opportunity to work, to develop their lives. you said that mr barosso would make an announcement at noon. but the president of the commission also made an announcement last december on opportunities for young people. you also announced action groups for young people. but we don't see any added value at this time - the last four, five months that these announcements have been made. what is the added value, commissioners, what is your advice to young people who are looking for prospects and hope? the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full, and i am sure that the commission will take the necessary steps to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. five and a half million are unemployed throughout europe. you know better than i do.
member of the commission. − emigration from the countries which felt the impact of the financial crisis is of course a problem, especially if this migration leads to other continents, because we very often find – in the case of greece, and also ireland and portugal – that people prefer to go other continents, despite the geographical proximity of many vacancies in the countries in the more dynamic part of europe. that is why our efforts, including in the forthcoming employment package, are to make the european labour market more transparent and the jobs and vacancies that exist in europe more accessible. this would also be done by developing the eures programme, which helps with finding and understanding what opportunities exist in the european labour market. there are practical developments. the action teams went to athens as well as to the other seven capitals. they came back with concrete findings. we had an opportunity in brussels to hold discussions with prime minister papademos. i also went to athens at the end of march and held discussions with several members of the government. for example, there are very practical opportunities now to develop not just training and apprenticeships but also a new programme for start-ups, which i discussed with minister diamantopoulou, and also to develop the social economy, which i discussed with minister koutroumanis. these are just examples of how refocusing the use of the structural funds – the esf in particular but not only the esf – can very directly, concretely and urgently help the young people who are now unemployed or who just want to find new opportunities inside greece.
the only point i would like to make, mr president, commissioner, is this, because you have been very specific in what you have said. i would remind you, however, that in greece we are in an election period, we have elections in twenty days, and everyone is asking the question: why have we not applied these tools that you have just described to us in the last two or two and a half years?
member of the commission. − very serious work has been undertaken in the recent period. greece has been lagging behind with the implementation of the structural funds, and a lot of progress has been made over the last year regarding the social fund, for example, not least because of the contribution of the task force which the commission sent to greece. i agree with you that this could have helped earlier. there was only about a 12% or 13% implementation rate up to next june and now it is about double that. so a lot of progress has been made, but of course we cannot change the situation overnight.

we're not going back to that. - before the vote on paragraph 112:
in writing. the technical report on the coordination of social security systems, which i voted in favour of, comes at a time when the social benefits and insurance systems of many member states - and greece in particular - are under pressure. although this report mainly deals with the procedure and practical application of the provisions of the pre-existing 2004 regulation, it nevertheless contains important amendments of greek interest, such as the regulations concerning the safety and work of border workers and civil aviation workers, while at the same time contributing positively to the creation of a more accurate, fairer and more effective insurance plan for accidents at work.
in writing. the negative outcome of the irish referendum on ratification of the lisbon treaty in june 2008 brought to light the concerns of the irish people on a number of issues that were not addressed or adequately enshrined in the new european treaty. as unanimity is undoubtedly required for the entry into force of any new treaty, the european council's decision to examine proposed amendments to subsequent treaties in the form of introducing clarifications to them - to include references requested by the irish side, such as the right to life, family, taxation, security and defence - is a political compromise welcomed by the european parliament. i therefore voted in favour of this resolution.
in writing. the own-initiative report refers to areas which, because of their specific characteristics (physical and structural), constitute a specific group falling under article 349 tfeu and where it is necessary to diversify the applicable eu rules and programmes by following a territorial, rather than strictly technical, approach. in this context, the eu 2020 strategy should take account of these specificities, with a focus on cohesion policy as a key driver for stimulating small-scale economies. at the same time, european policies in the eu's outermost regions need to be adapted to better deliver on the objectives of economic, social and territorial cohesion and to contribute to the objectives of the eu 2020 strategy. the present resolution, which i voted in favour of, is moving in that direction, taking account of the above characteristics.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report, but i differed on certain points. in particular, i was in favour of including phrases which reinforce the text and the eu's commitment to human rights, such as the reference to the need to promote hygiene and health education programmes in third countries and the reinforcement of european humanitarian aid programmes relating to gender equality and child protection. i also supported the reference to the european parliament's disappointment at the behaviour of certain member states and their reluctance to adopt the european convention on human rights.
mr president, for some time the policy promoted by the commission through the troika has been one of unilateral austerity. i am glad that we are discussing employment and growth today, but there has already been a lot of damage done and the commission must admit that it has made very big mistakes and so has the greek government. what are the facts at this point? we know that unemployment at the level of the working population has exceeded 21%, over a million million people are unemployed. two million people, 20% of the total population, are on the verge of survival and over 50% of young people are now unemployed for a very long time. so we must look again at the policies implemented in greece to make them consistent with our european values. we must not just talk, make promises and words, but we must have goals. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. but, specific goals and timelines. we cannot have unilateral austerity and i would say that the problem must be tackled in a fair, balanced way and with a change in the economy and the administration.
mr president, i would like to thank the president of the commission, mr barosso, for the communication in order to get greece back to growth and create jobs. my country has so far made important strides with the help of its partners. the deficit has been drastically reduced and the situation is stabilising. the structural changes are progressing. it is obvious that public sector spending must be reduced even further to curb excessive indirect and direct taxation in order to start some growth. mr president, we greek members of the european people's party, members of the new democracy, have pointed out in good time the danger of unilateral austerity. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. today everyone accepts this truth. some in this room are calling for greece to leave the eurozone. i'm so sorry about that. the greek people want to remain closely linked to europe and the euro. we believe that only together can we tackle a crisis that has become systemic. the same applies to all countries in crisis. mr president, what we have built together must be maintained and secured by our common work. we must all work to combat unemployment and to bring about a new era of prosperity. no country can do it alone. the president of the commission knows this better than any of us. i would point out, mr president, that the comments made by the liberal group chairman, mr verhofstadt, do not exactly correspond to what he said: the financing he spoke about had been done in previous years and parliament simply arranged the repayment to the banks. i do not think that the greek parties are at this difficult moment prepared to spend money while the people suffer from the sacrifices they make.
mr president, mr vice-president of the commission, it is true that the commission has never claimed that the crisis is behind us, but it is also true that we have had three parallel crises in the course of the last few years, a budgetary, an economic and a social crisis. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. this has led to a targeting of europe and a growing distrust of european citizens, and we should be concerned that wherever elections are held - and i am not referring to greece, mr president, i am referring to france - europe is at the centre of the electoral campaign, not in a positive way but in a negative way. greece, mr president, has made great strides in fiscal consolidation in extremely difficult circumstances, with only one party, the socialist party, bearing until a few months ago the full burden and the full costs, political and social, of managing the crisis. but the main burden is on the greek people who will be voting in three weeks' time, and i would ask you, mr vice-president of the european commission, on the basis of the letters sent by mr venizelos and mr papademos to the commission, to tell us what concrete measures and policies you can take to help tackle unemployment, especially youth unemployment, which is approaching 50%, to ensure financing for small and mediumsized enterprises that are suffocating and to unblock the major projects that are crucial for growth and job creation.
mr president, i am focusing on greece. the member states have spent enormous sums. the greeks have made heavy sacrifices. but the objectives were not met. why would you do that? because the adjustment fell mainly, not on the state-sponsored client system, but on productive greece and the weakest: businesses, employees, low-pensioners. overtaxation and a complete lack of financing are now leading to the closure of competitive greek export enterprises. the state is deferring €6 billion in private sector debt. the two ruling parties are making pre-election recruitments and have generously settled, by law, their huge debts to the banks. the commission does not seem to be proposing anything significantly new, such as the 2020 plan recently presented by alde. a plan for more private investment, liquidity and jobs. but apart from the incorrigible, state-sponsored, clientelist system, there is also a positive, productive, creative greece and europe must finally invest in it.
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. i would like to ask the vice-president of the commission whether we are experiencing a crisis of the economy or a crisis of economic perception, of economic prescription? the one that has led to the vicious circle: austerity, recession, new austerity measures, new recession. i believe that both the institutional and political leadership of the european union must abandon its institutional and political egoism and change course. i also believe, mr president, and i fully agree with what the president of the commission, mr barosso, said, that together we will succeed, the european union and greece. yes, i believe it deeply, but through a conception, through a plan of a real partnership and not through the logic of a dominant entity over greece.
mr president, commissioner, mr president-in-office of the council, we have taken the initiative in holding this debate precisely because we believe that the fight against tax evasion and tax avoidance is and must be an integral part of both national and european strategies for overcoming the crisis. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the tax burden is not reduced to a level that is not in line with the objectives of the treaty. it is absurd to pursue the deepening of fiscal policy alone without taking corresponding steps towards convergence and coordination of tax policy, which will increase tax competition between us and worsen the fiscal problems of countries in difficulty. and it is not just the tax competition between us, it is also the flight of capital abroad, to switzerland, but also to other member countries of the european union that still maintain banking secrecy. it is clear that there are national responsibilities for tax evasion, and the same is true of the financial crisis. this has not, however, prevented the council and the commission from adopting stricter rules and policies to deal with the problem.
mr president, i would like to point out and ask the commissioner the following: greece is known to be facing serious budgetary problems; on the other hand, we know that they exist in swiss banks... (the president interrupts)
mr president, tax evasion and corruption cost 1% of europe's gdp, or about 120 billion euros. also, according to a recent study, two-thirds of the member states of europe failed in a recent assessment to score above 5 out of 10 on corruption issues. i mention this to emphasize that this problem of tax evasion is not just a problem of some member states, as some colleagues have often said, it is a european problem and must be dealt with as such. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission has already made a number of proposals to improve the quality of the information provided by the member states. of these, about one billion is dedicated to greece, until 2013, in terms of e-government and the reform of public administration. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat tax evasion.
