the next item is the report by mrs astrid lulling, on behalf of the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy, on the taxation of energy products and electricity (com (2011) 0169 - c7-0105/2011 - 2011/0092 (cns)) (a7-0052/2012)
i would like to refer to the proposal to abolish the exemptions for the shipping and aviation sectors. as a goal, the reduction of carbon emissions from both sectors is not only legitimate but absolutely necessary. the demand for services in both sectors is estimated to double by 2050, and of course it would be criminal to let carbon emissions increase by the same amount. however, our approach should guarantee the end result. both sectors, and in particular the shipping industry, would be exposed to a very serious risk of 'carbon leakage' if fuel taxes were imposed at eu level by refuelling ships outside the eu and adapting their transport routes. for this reason, in order to be effective, the taxation of marine fuel should be exclusively at world level.
i am speaking on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, i regard as absolutely positive the proposal to create a risk-sharing financial instrument to support infrastructure projects and productive investment, as mentioned by the rapporteur, mrs hübner. indeed, it is imperative to create conditions for economic growth at a critical juncture, in an economic climate where budget cuts are also significantly affecting greek public investment, while the lack of liquidity and access to finance are also hampering public-private partnerships. critical dimensions of the greek economy have deteriorated, dramatically, despite the unprecedented sacrifices of greek society. i am referring, first of all, to the growth in the unemployment rate, which is now close to 50% for young people. i hope that both the commission and the european investment bank will ensure that the conditions, for example as regards the leverage ratio and the eligible investments, are met in order to make the best possible use of this financial instrument with multiplier benefits for the real economy. finally, in addition to the necessary fiscal consolidation, i believe that a comprehensive plan for a creative development plan and a way out of the vicious circle of austerity and recession is urgently needed.
mr president, i would like to thank the rapporteur and chairman of the committee on regional development, mrs hübner, for her methodical work and her excellent report, and also mrs kleva for her very good report. i shall not go into the details of the report, since previous colleagues have analysed them in detail. i would like to stress, however, that with the proposed changes cohesion policy is once again making a major contribution to the recovery of the economy, growth and job creation. tens of thousands of new jobs are expected to be created in greece alone, while at the same time the completion of the greek part of the projects of axis 7, the transeuropean transport network, will be achieved. of course, in order to unblock the large regional development projects that have come to a standstill in the countries affected by the crisis and to make the best possible use of these instruments, the leverage should be at least two or even three times the amount committed from the cohesion policy funds.
mr president, a similar risk-spreading tool is certainly appropriate, provided that it is aimed at shifting the economy in directions that make it more sustainable and more compatible with ecological objectives. the commission has already proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them, but i am afraid that the commission will not be able to accept them. when there's a big fiscal crisis, car traffic decreases, and many economists argue that these projects are no longer economically viable. i am not sure that the commissioner has been able to give us a clear answer to this question. we would therefore like to see this tool used towards a green economy. the commissioner said this morning that the green economy has enormous potential and that it is possible to create 20 million jobs in europe, so why not create similar jobs in greece using such a tool?
mr president, the report we are discussing tonight and the proposal from the european commission is an important, an intelligent response to the attempt to deal with the economic and budgetary crisis, especially for countries that are particularly and severely affected, such as my country, greece. i therefore welcome this proposal in particular as regards this instrument, the risk-sharing instrument, which enables countries to use community resources and to disburse funds from the european investment bank or another body to finance projects carried out with contributions from the different funds and the cohesion fund. it allows the private sector to participate in the production of wealth, in the creation of jobs and, i repeat, this is especially true for countries which are suffering from severe cuts in public investment, such as greece. greece has shown interest in this mechanism, particularly in the concession of motorways, and i believe that this will be a good opportunity to restart the economy, which my country so badly needs in order to get back on a path of recovery and growth.
madam president, let us be clear: the agreement is not perfect and the arguments, and especially the reservations, that have been voiced today are certainly well founded. but the question is, what's the alternative? how do we ensure the rights and security of european citizens in the best possible way, under the present circumstances of course? the answer, i believe, is that with this agreement which we are called upon to vote on today, with all its weaknesses, which we are well aware of. but there is another element i would like to consider. the european parliament is called upon today to vote on an international agreement, and in an international agreement other parameters must also be taken into account, such as what is the future of the european union's cooperation with the united states, what do we want europe's role to be in international developments in the future? the european parliament rightly calls for a more substantial role in the negotiation of the union's international treaties and we must have that role as the european parliament in the future. but we must show that we as a european parliament are responsible and know how to meet those responsibilities.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report, supporting the compromise reached with the council after the failure of the commission's two previous proposals. the agreement provides for the strengthening of synergies between the union's lending programmes and funds in the member states receiving assistance from the union or from the international monetary fund at the time of entry into force of the amendment, an important point for greece. this measure is directly relevant to our country, as it facilitates the collection of the necessary private financing for the implementation of infrastructure projects and productive investments which can only be partly financed by public funds. in this way, major projects such as motorways can once again be started and completed.
in writing. this directive on the common consolidated corporate tax base (ccctb), which i voted in favour of, establishes certain common rules for the calculation of the tax base applicable to companies operating in the european union. the cif value is calculated according to a specific mathematical formula which includes the sales, labour and assets of each company. the directive, which is essentially technical, does not affect the rates of taxation which, under the treaty, are a matter for the member states. it nevertheless attempts to propose a common optional framework for businesses. the european parliament has repeatedly expressed the view that the introduction of the cohesion fund contributes to the completion of the european internal market and thus to the stability of the euro area.
in writing. the revised proposal for a directive modifies the structure of energy taxation so as to support the objective of an economy less dependent on and more reliant on coal and more on alternative energy sources. the report, which i voted in favour of, is positive for countries that are turning to solar energy and therefore offers greece an additional incentive to turn to new forms of energy and at the same time economic opportunities that will help the country to get out of its difficult economic situation. for example, according to the commission's proposal, energy taxes are divided into two parts. the first will be on co2 emissions and the second on energy content. in both cases, greece, a country rich in solar and wind energy, has a clear comparative advantage which it can immediately exploit and secure through low taxation and high competitiveness to its own benefit and, above all, to that of its citizens.
president of the council. - mr president, i have to say something. members of the house! i would like to thank the commissioner for his report. there is no doubt that greece is facing difficult challenges in the area of immigration and it is vital that we support greece in restoring its capacity to deal with immigration. in the council we have been discussing the implementation of the greek national action plan on asylum and migration and border control, most recently at the justice and home affairs council on 8 march this year. the commission and greece noted that some progress had been made, for example in the practical processing of asylum applications. however, it is also clear that there are still major challenges in implementation. this applies, inter alia, to ensuring sufficient staff to deal with cases and to setting up the necessary number of reception centres. it is essential that all resources are used to strengthen the implementation of the greek action plan this means both a major effort on greece's part and a well-coordinated eu effort to assist greece. the involvement of eu agencies such as the european asylum support office, frontex and member states in a position to contribute is necessary. the un high commissioner for refugees also plays an important role in this. this applies to financial resources, expertise and other practical assistance. in this context, it is positive that, as indicated, the establishment of new reception centres in the evros region is planned. i will now turn the floor to the commission, which can tell us more about greece in the field of migration. i would like to take this opportunity to stress that we in the council have discussed how we can show solidarity with member states that are experiencing particularly high pressure on their migration systems. on 8 march, the council therefore adopted a set of council conclusions on a common framework for real and practical solidarity for member states whose asylum systems are under particular pressure as a result of mixed migration flows. the central element of this solidarity framework is the idea of an early warning and preparedness mechanism in the dublin regulation. this mechanism is now included in the amendment to the dublin regulation which we have just sent to the european parliament with a view to opening the trilogue negotiations. this mechanism provides us with a basis for anticipating situations such as the one we are seeing in greece. at the same time, we have the tools to assist the country concerned should the situation arise. the council agrees on the value of strengthening schengen cooperation and external border control. the council also agreed to strengthen both the role of eu agencies and cooperation with third countries, and to promote efficient and orderly broadcasting. in particular, the agencies are crucial to strengthening solidarity through increased practical cooperation. the presidency has stressed that solidarity is an important principle which must be implemented in the form of practical and real cooperation. with this general approach of solidarity, we will of course continue to focus on the situation in greece and the implementation of the action plan. the council supports the implementation of the action plan and that it is done in a well-coordinated way, and i am glad that the european parliament has raised the issue here today, as the challenges facing greece are also the challenges facing the eu in this respect. a reform of greece's asylum and migration system and well-managed border controls is important not only for the eu and for greece, but also for migrants, asylum seekers and refugees.
member of the commission. − madam president, i would like to thank the parliament for the opportunity once again to discuss this very important issue concerning migration management shortcomings in greece, which is linked with the ongoing discussions on the common european asylum system and also concerns the fundamental rights of individuals. the commission has been continuously supporting greece’s efforts to reform its asylum and migration policies. as was mentioned by the minister, greece developed an action plan on migration management and asylum reform, and progress has been achieved in a number of areas there. two new services have been established – one for the examination of applications and one for initial reception. these are important steps. last year the european asylum support office (easo) deployed asylum support teams in greece. these teams provide advice on reception conditions in the evros area, which is the one that is the most problematic. these activities will continue this year and will be further intensified. we have also conducted technical missions in greece, including the task force for greece, where the unhcr, the iom, frontex and the easo are all involved, in order to ensure a holistic approach. we continue to make great efforts to promote stronger engagement by the turkish authorities in preventing the irregular migratory flows from transiting through turkish territory towards the eu’s borders. so there has been progress, but much more needs to be done. the humanitarian situation in evros is not acceptable. the conditions in which migrant and asylum seekers are kept are particularly worrying. progress needs to be immediate and sustainable. more efforts are needed from the greek authorities in this respect. greece should trigger emergency mechanisms to provide immediate humanitarian relief to the migrants crossing the border area. they can use eu funding to do this and to renovate existing facilities and temporarily increase capacity by renting hostels for vulnerable groups, as well as deploying habitable containers within the security perimeters in the region. the commission has on many occasions reminded the greek authorities that failing to take urgent measures could lead to significant legal consequences, but of course, on a daily basis, there are also very serious humanitarian consequences. we are there to help, as are the member states’ numerous organisations and institutions, but greece itself has the responsibility to act in accordance with international and union law. the commission will continue to provide strategic and targeted support for greece’s efforts to reform its system. this involves the provision of additional financial assistance and emergency support but also expertise on the ground, in close collaboration with the national authorities, the agencies, the unhcr and the iom. greece is among the main beneficiaries of the solidarity and management of migration flows programme, with a total amount of eur 309 million allocated so far. however, there is a problem of absorption from the greek side, so very little of this money has been used so far. for immediate needs, we recently granted an additional amount of eur 3.75 million under the european refugee fund to implement emergency measures, and we are concerned about the possibilities for using that money as well. it is crucial to streamline the delivery mechanism at national level, removing constraints and reinforcing management of the funds. support will also be provided by the eu task force on greece established by the commission to assist and maximise the absorption of eu financial assistance and the implementation of the reform package. a dedicated expert was deployed to athens in december 2011 to advise the greek authorities directly involved in the implementation of these funds. i would also like to recall the commission’s recast proposal for the dublin regulation which, like the rest of the asylum package, aims to increase the efficiency of the system and to improve protection of the applicants. we are very grateful to parliament for its strong support for the commission’s proposal and to the presidency for ensuring that they are now ready to enter into a trilogue on these issues and other related issues in the asylum package. recent court rulings again prove that the eu needs a solid and efficient common european asylum system to support the member states and guarantee a fair and effective system of providing international protection to people in need. this is a common goal for the european union and has been so for quite some time. the need for common rules and greater solidarity is now even more pressing. we need high standards and stronger cooperation to make sure asylum seekers are treated equally wherever they apply and that people in need of protection can have access to this protection. with a fair system throughout the european union it is possible for all member states to share the responsibility for this. as part of this effort, the dublin system needs to be complemented with provisions addressing the root causes of crises and ensuring that deficiencies in one system or another in member states are remedied without delay so that they do not affect the entire system and lead to human rights violations. the commission is, and continues to be, involved. i appreciate the support from parliament and the strong engagement by the council and many other organisations, but of course the main responsibility lies with the greek authorities.
i am speaking on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, without a doubt, prevention is better than cure. i fully agree with the commissioner on what she said about the individual rights that asylum seekers should have. what he did not mention, and he did not make clear to the audience, is the huge number of people entering greece as illegal immigrants. when turkey has liberalised visas for all the countries of the region and the mediterranean and for 50 euros someone can come from tripoli in libya to istanbul and then drive to the border with greece, you realise that things are different. the people who enter - who are brought in by various traders, literally people - are people whose origin we do not know, whose language we do not know, who are undocumented and, of course, all seeking asylum. so when 300 or 500 people cross the border every day, it's extremely difficult to manage such a large number of people. i therefore agree with commissioner malmström that there is a need for diversification in dublin ii and i am particularly pleased that you too see this and want to strengthen it. so when greece bears the total burden of 90 percent of illegal immigration, you realize that things are getting difficult. the commission has already taken a number of decisions on the basis of the proposals contained in the report. but the solution to the problem, commissioner, is to oblige turkey to implement the agreement it has with the european union on readmission and to oblige it to implement the agreement it has signed with greece since 1999. i think that there cannot be a candidate country which wants to recognise or not recognise the agreements with europe on its own terms. the european union must be able to take action to prevent this enormous pressure from being exerted on turkey. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we are fully committed to the union and to the ideas and values we have defended, commissioner, and we will continue to support and defend ourselves, but we really need substantial support from the union, the council, parliament and you who are properly handling this matter.

madam president, i would first of all like to point out, in connection with what has been said before, that we greeks are a welcoming people and have over time proved that we stand by our fellow man - wherever he comes from - respecting his rights. but i must stress, in relation to the reports and complaints made by colleagues, that the family of the man who was murdered a few months ago at 6 a.m. by illegal immigrants when he was taking his pregnant wife to the maternity ward to give birth also has rights. i read yesterday's letter from the french and german interior ministers asking for the possibility of an emergency reintroduction of border controls, whereas in the past heads of state have often referred to the reduction in the number of migrants they wish to accept. in connection with these reports, let me stress the following: when you don't want any more illegal visitors in your house, you don't close the living room door or the kitchen door. you close the front door, secure the main entrance. that's the solution. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. and here our home is europe and our main entrance is the external borders of europe. if you ask frontex and any other relevant agency, they will tell you that the main gateway for illegal immigrants to europe - it has already been mentioned - is the border between greece and turkey. we need the solidarity of europe - it has already been said - the resources, the know-how, the manpower to strengthen and guard our borders more effectively. at the same time, however, it is also urgent to demand that turkey finally stop showing such provocative tolerance to the circles and traffickers who take advantage of even the absence of visas at turkey's borders with many of its neighbouring countries and, of course, to take back illegal migrants who are being pushed into europe through its territory. the agreement on readmission should finally be concluded and, as regards those seeking international protection, - as has already been said - we should proceed to a fairer distribution of responsibility for examining their applications, and i am obviously referring to the change in dublin ii. if you tell me that there are similar problems in other member states, i will say that you are wrong. the european union has a duty to help the countries of central and eastern europe to develop their economic and social cohesion. in fact, the number is even higher. in your country it is certainly not every day that it is rising in the polls - because we are also in an election period - a far-right party which proposes to put mines - can you hear me? mines! - along our land border with turkey or i do not know if you often hear proposals in your countries to generalise the right of private individuals to bear arms. europe has been dramatically delayed in taking decisions and taking action on the debt crisis in greece. let us not make the same mistake on the issue of illegal immigration.
madam president, the problem of migration in greece must be seen as a humanitarian crisis where we must all come together as the european union to find a single solution. my country receives over 150,000 irregular migrants a year, which is close to 15% of the country's population every year. these people are trying to escape persecution, war and hunger and they are undoubtedly entitled to human and dignified treatment. but greece in crisis cannot manage migration flows of this magnitude on its own, because, as many speakers pointed out, greece receives almost 90% of migrants trying to enter the european union and dublin ii is locking them in. the problems of receiving and holding irregular migrants are of course great, and the asylum procedure, despite improvements, is not as fast as it should be. but it is hypocritical to compare greece with other countries of the union in terms of reception conditions, in terms of the asylum procedure, since the problem is not comparable. the immigration issue in greece will only be resolved if it is dealt with as it really is. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full.

in writing. decentralisation of external assistance managed by the commission is necessary to increase the speed and accuracy of budgetary management procedures and to improve the quality of assistance to partner countries. however, the decentralisation process should undoubtedly be accompanied by mechanisms at member state and eu level to ensure both transparency and the provision of all relevant information on the nature of the agencies' budgetary expenditure. this own-initiative report, which i voted in favour of, calls on the commission to ensure that the criteria and the procedures for assessing the quality of the projects financed are respected, in order to improve the quality of aid and to further reduce the number of unprofitable projects. it also called on the member states to make greater efforts to improve links between eu delegations and implementing bodies in order to increase the competitive advantages of the decentralisation process at national level, while avoiding duplication of efforts.
in writing. in all international organisations, in all international forums, statements and commitments to combat climate change and protect biodiversity are in excess. but the truth is that despite the declarations, biodiversity continues to decline globally in general, in europe and in greece in particular, at rates up to 1000 times faster than naturalists. the report, which i voted for, highlights these discrepancies between statements and actual data. it called on the member states and the eu to take steps to implement and make practical the commitments, and stressed the importance of member states intensifying their efforts to change the attitudes of citizens on these issues, both through their education systems and through the promotion of general rules of conduct.
i am very pleased to see that the commission has taken the initiative to make a proposal to the council for a regulation on the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster. the most innovative aspect of this regulation is the move from simple price cuts to giving consumers the possibility of choosing a different operator for roaming services, while keeping their number. this is where competition can deliver lower prices for consumers, especially in times of crisis. berec now has the responsibility for ensuring that this is done in the simplest and most consumer-friendly way. if the solution is over-complicated, this regulation risks failing its objectives. for this reason, any technical solution detailed by berec should not be over-complicated or excessively burdensome for the mobile industry. my congratulations to the commissioner.

in writing. the lack of democracy in europe is also largely due to the inadequate exploitation of the possibilities offered by new technologies and, consequently, e-government. however, for legal reasons, the electronic publication of the eu official journal was delayed. in order to overcome these obstacles and to ensure that all citizens have direct access to european decisions, the european parliament proposed the use of the flexibility clause (article 352 tfeu) as an appropriate response to the legal questions raised by several member states. for the european parliament, the electronic publication of eu legal acts, currently available in the eur-lex database, is of course more than welcome and conveys the belief that this reform will enhance legal certainty and reduce access costs for citizens and businesses. i therefore voted in favour of this report.
in writing. this own-initiative report by the ep, although non-binding, is of particular importance for greece and i voted in favour of it. regional airports and air services are an important value of our national tourist product but they are also of particular importance for the service of greek citizens given the geography of the country. in this report, the european parliament proposes a number of recommendations on policy options which, if adopted by the commission and the member states, would improve the operational and economic efficiency of regional airports and air services. some of these, such as the definition of the concept of regional airports, their emergence as important partners of the national air networks and the completion of the single european sky projects, are important points for the further development and emergence of greek regional airports. the government that will emerge after the electoral processes in greece should make use of this report for the benefit of the national regional airports and services.
in writing. with more than 1% of european gdp lost to fraud each year in a particularly difficult economic period, action to combat corruption and fraud in europe is urgent. the protection of the european union's economic interests also requires increased cooperation between the member states. while irregularity reports increased in all sectors in 2011, further improvements to the irregularity management system (ims) are needed. moreover, as the commission itself admits, 'the data in its annual reports must be interpreted with caution because the actual levels of irregularities are neither known nor estimated, and are therefore not provided'. this unsatisfactory finding makes it clear that the commission and the member states have not exhausted the policies and measures which will lead to the reduction of fraud in europe. in this report, which i voted in favour of, the european parliament calls for and expects concrete proposals, actions and initiatives from both the commission and the member states to combat corruption and fraud.
in writing. i would like to thank the rapporteur for his excellent report. our role as a parliament is to be accountable to the citizens and to ensure that taxpayers' money is used transparently and efficiently. this obligation is further reinforced by the current economic and financial crisis, which has put public finances under unprecedented pressure. the efficient and correct use of public money is more important than ever. the biggest problem identified in the commission's annual report and highlighted by the rapporteur himself in his report is the lack of consistency between the member states in the recording and reporting of irregularities or suspected fraud. this makes it impossible to assess objectively the true scale of fraud in the member states and prevents the european parliament, the commission and olaf from carrying out their tasks of assessing the situation and making further proposals. this situation must not continue and the commission must take full responsibility for recovering funds wrongly paid and for collecting the required uniform comparable data from all member states.
in writing. the costs and charges of roaming on public mobile networks within the union are a hot topic in greece as in the rest of europe. it is true that, at times, the charges for these services have deviated from the european legal framework. for example, in addition to the fixed prices in chats and text messages, in order to avoid 'bloated bills', the withdrawal limit of eur 50 applies as a rule to all consumers who have not already chosen a different withdrawal limit. however, some companies still do not offer consumers appropriate charges and do not comply with community law. in any case, excessive roaming charges and the resulting limited use of roaming services within the eu do not benefit european citizens or the european economy. the present proposal for a regulation is therefore intended to deal with these distortions and i have therefore voted in favour of it.
in writing. the kke group in the european parliament voted against the joint motion for a resolution by the conservative, liberal and social democratic political groups on 'maritime piracy', because in the name of combating it it is escalating the imperialist interventions of the eu-us-nato from the horn of africa to the indian ocean to control the resources and exploit the peoples. the resolution calls on the eu to strengthen its military and naval presence in the wider region by reinforcing the eunavfor atalanta force in coordination with nato. it is also proposed to increase the presence of armed units on board ships and to promote new euro-army and euro-fleet general staffs in the region. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat the spread of the disease. it called for the mobilisation of the european maritime safety agency and other organisations to support eu and nato naval warfare operations, increasing the risks to seafarers and people. it is imperative that the peoples intensify the struggle for disengagement from the eu and nato and the other imperialist associations, for the conquest of popular power in their countries.
in writing. the issue of maritime piracy, particularly in the somali region, has also concerned and affected the greek merchant marine fleet. the joint resolution, which i voted in favour of, reiterates the ep's serious concern at the continuing and growing threat to international shipping posed by piracy and armed robbery and calls on the high representative and the member states to urgently consider ways of freeing the 191 seafarers currently held hostage. it is clear that impunity and the inability to apprehend those who engage in piracy exposes the international community and encourages the perpetuation of these practices. parliament called for immediate and effective measures to prosecute those who have been involved in acts of piracy, called on third countries and eu member states which had not yet transposed all the provisions of the un convention on the law of the sea and the un convention for the suppression of unlawful acts against the safety of navigation to do so immediately, and called on the council and the commission to consider the possibility of setting up specialised anti-piracy courts in the countries of the region to prosecute pirates on the spot.

mr president, i heard and i share the concerns of colleagues on the visa issue, and i would also like to draw attention to another possible problem regarding this agreement. for the first time since the lisbon treaty allowed us to do so, we have concluded eu agreements on investment issues. it seems that the proposal will include provision for investor-state dispute settlement. this will mean that not only states but also individual investors can sue eu states over what they regard as obstacles to their investments. this might seriously reduce our powers here in this house, and also in the national parliaments, to regulate on environment-related, social and labour issues. it is proposed to maximise economic profit and benefits, but our own eu assessment – the economic, social and environmental assessment – says there is no solid evidence to suggest that investor-state dispute settlement will maximise economic benefit in ceta beyond simply serving as one form of enforcement mechanism, just as state-state settlement is also an enforcement mechanism. it goes on to say that a well-crafted state-state dispute settlement mechanism might be a more appropriate enforcement mechanism in ceta. so our own assessment says we should move in another direction. the idea of an investor-state settlement mechanism was introduced in the north american free trade agreement (nafta) because mexico’s courts were considered to be corrupt, but in the end the mechanism was used mainly against canada. twenty-eight cases have been taken against canada under nafta, and out of them eight have been concluded officially. four have been lost – 50% have been lost. i would like to mention very briefly the case of ethyl corp versus canada. canada decided to ban, for health reasons, a gasoline additive, and the ethyl company took canada to the nafta tribunal. it made canada pay cad 16 million in compensation, apologise and withdraw the ban. i believe that this is not the kind of regulation we can accept in this agreement.

mr president, in order to deal with the budgetary crisis in greece, measures are being implemented which provoke a reaction from society, whereas a much more socially balanced policy could be pursued. i would like to mention one example which is completely absent from the new memorandum on the adjustment of the greek economy. this is the country's dependence on imported petroleum fuels. greece, like other countries facing a deep crisis today, is totally dependent on oil imports, which, however, place a heavy burden on its economy and increase its trade deficit significantly. the annual cost of importing oil, just to run the power plants on the unconnected islands, is over eur 300 million. the memorandum should therefore place a strong emphasis on energy efficiency and resource efficiency, as this could improve the country's finances and create jobs by revitalising the economy. i think that one of the changes that should be made to the memorandum should be to that policy.
mr president, the elections on 17 june will be the most crucial in greece's post-war history. citizens are being asked to choose whether they want a responsible government that can take the country out of the crisis, or whether they prefer to succumb to populism and easy promises. in a context of such a critical nature, with such dramatic dilemmas, outside interference is working negatively and provoking reactions even from those who want to listen. the most recent example is mrs merkel's proposal to the greek political leadership to hold a referendum on the question of whether or not to adopt the euro on the same day as the elections. it is a view that is out of place and out of time, because a caretaker government cannot propose a referendum, because the parliament which alone can decide on such a thing has been dissolved in view of elections and, finally, because nowhere are referendums held because some partners demand it. when a referendum was proposed by the pasok government a few months ago, with a much more specific question, there was a negative reaction internationally, spearheaded by mrs merkel. any such intervention is absolutely counterproductive at this time. we call on the commission and the council to refrain from any action which gives the impression of interfering in the internal affairs of a sovereign country. from europe, greece needs a united voice of support and not hints and threats.

mr president, the balance between austerity and the growth of the european economies is one that european leaders must find, both under the pressure of the election results in france and greece and the anxious gaze of the markets. strict austerity measures continue to create new unemployment and wider problems of social cohesion across the european union. the electoral results of local or national elections across europe, such as those held in italy, germany and france, represent a generalized reaction to the policy of austerity. the german model of budgetary discipline must be relaxed and the economy must be developed immediately if the european societies are to regain their equilibrium and safeguard their cohesion, which is under threat. the currency is also threatened, mr president. we must respect the tolerance of european citizens. in the light of the principle of european solidarity, we must consider a more realistic solution, involving the issue of eurobonds, the strengthening of the european bank and the issuing of new money.
on behalf of the efd group. mr president, small and mediumsized enterprises are the cornerstone of the european economy and perhaps the most important tool for stimulating growth and creating jobs in the present conditions prevailing in the european union. the sector is, however, the most vulnerable to the effects of the economic crisis. the fall in the profitability of small and mediumsized enterprises in greece is 60%, the highest in europe, followed by spain at 55%. the opposite is the case in germany, where the profitability of smes has increased by 21%. in my country, greece, the sector is deteriorating the most, and access to bank financing for smes is no longer possible. many businesses are closing down, worsening the situation in the business and labour sectors. however, the latest european bank lending surveys show that the criteria for banks to lend to smes have increased. the commission is therefore invited to study and propose measures to facilitate the absorption of funds in countries which are particularly unable to secure the necessary amounts of cofinancing because of strict austerity programmes. the commission is invited to promote cooperation and synergies between the corrective fund and the european investment bank in order to find financial instruments and to be able to expect multiplier effects. the most profitable sectors in each economy must be found and the right strategic orientation must be made.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we see a large percentage of unemployed people who were employed in such enterprises; but there are also owners of such businesses who lose their jobs, which is often not reflected in the unemployment rate. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to finance the necessary measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to finance the necessary measures. it is a fact that 80% of applications for loans from small and mediumsized enterprises to banks are currently rejected in greece. this means that there is no funding to move them, or to import raw materials or products, and so they are driven to collapse. on the other hand, the conditions for access to european finance do not allow smes to move either, because they have to spend the capital first, because vat is not eligible and because they have to wait a long time to get back the money they have spent. that's impossible in a time of collapse. we must therefore change in order to put into practice what the commissioner has said. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's social policy is not distorted by the fact that it is not able to take account of the needs of the young people.
in writing. small and medium-sized enterprises (smes) are the backbone of the eu economy, as they are a driving force for growth, job creation, innovation, cohesion and the achievement of the eu 2020 objectives. however, they suffer from funding gaps and the current crisis has exacerbated this situation and has hampered the efforts of smes to find new capital. in the last few weeks we have heard a great deal about measures to promote growth and combat unemployment, particularly among young people. this growth boost can start with smes and strengthening their competitiveness. the european funds have already made a considerable contribution to this area, but i am convinced that much more can be done. in the new cohesion policy programming period, we need to ensure long-term actions to improve access to the structural funds for smes and to simplify procedures to reduce the administrative burden and red tape that often hamper their operation. in addition, there is a need for regional autonomy in the selection of sme programmes to be financed by the structural funds and for the involvement of actors at all levels to be encouraged in order to promote entrepreneurship.
in writing. i voted against the report, despite the fact that it attempts to maintain some balance by stating the need to respect fundamental human rights and the principle of proportionality. but overall the report is moving in the wrong direction based on the stockholm programme and the military presence of eu forces, through the cfsp, in neighbouring countries and the rest of the world. the report proposes to increase spending on security issues, linking the external and internal dimensions of security and further linking eu foreign policy with organisations such as nato. finally, the report refers vaguely to extremist organisations, to 'the highlighting of actions aimed at countering violent radicalisation of vulnerable populations' and states that it 'looks forward to the future work of the eu radicalisation awareness network, whose mission is to facilitate the exchange of information'. if and as long as the report, in this terminology, implies the poor social strata, then it aims at the criminalisation of their expression and reactions. we are also seeing the way in which left-wing political forces are systematically branded as extremist. but these issues are being addressed through social policies, through education, through the fair distribution of wealth and through social equality, and not through policing, repression and informers.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. the european security policy is closely linked to the concept of the rule of law in the eu, as is explicitly stated in the treaty of lisbon. the strengthening and implementation of this policy cannot, of course, be without regard to democratic values, human rights and fundamental freedoms. the stockholm programme makes it clear that europe has achieved a partial reduction in organised crime but has not fully developed its capabilities to respond to this modern threat. the next steps, which are outlined in the report, should therefore focus on four key points. these are: firstly, the clear division of competences between the eu and the member states; secondly, the increased powers of parliament as also provided for in the treaty; thirdly, ensuring sufficient financial resources for the implementation of this strategy in the multiannual framework 2014-2020; and, fourthly, the supplementing and codification of the acts constituting organised crime which the eu aims to tackle jointly with the member states.
in writing. the abolition of the pillars in the eu structure by the lisbon treaty has opened the way to closer cooperation at member state level on the issue of criminal law and its european approach. as an indication of the new policies, the treaty now provides for a procedure to suspend the ordinary legislative procedure if a member state considers that proposed legislation affects fundamental aspects of its criminal justice system. however, the increasing volume of such cases now arising at european level raises some practical issues. for example, a host of new eu initiatives can easily overwhelm national administrations. the present report, which i voted in favour of, therefore proposes the adoption of specific, very careful approaches to ensure consistency and high quality in a field of law which is open to different interpretations and in which there is a very high risk of serious divergences between member states.
in writing. consumers are exposed to thousands of messages and references on a daily basis about products and services offered in a wide variety of contexts, which increases the risk of becoming more vulnerable in certain areas, such as food and the financial sector. it is true that the market seeks to create needs in its consumer public - and particularly in vulnerable individuals, needs that do not really exist, achieving its goal through inadequate training and awareness of consumers about their legal rights. a more coherent eu strategy to strengthen the rights of vulnerable consumers is therefore necessary. i therefore vote in favour of this report because it strengthens consumer participation in the single market, ensuring consumer protection and creating a more dynamic and competitive internal market.
in writing. first of all, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on her excellent report and welcome the intention of the report to introduce the concept of vulnerable consumers, in a horizontal way, into the various legislative and policy instruments of the european union designed to protect consumers, taking into account the different needs, capacities and circumstances applicable to them. of particular importance is the call in the report for the european union and the member states to pay greater attention to and invest in consumer information and education campaigns aimed at getting the right messages to the right segment of consumers. overall, the establishment of a strategy to strengthen the rights of the most vulnerable consumers to enable them to participate in the single market will contribute not only to their social integration and the move towards a fairer and more tolerant society, but also to ensuring a more dynamic, secure and competitive internal market. however, it is necessary to ensure that this specific protection of vulnerable consumers does not in any event lead to the creation of two different levels of protection.
in writing. the possible issue of one and two euro banknotes in addition to coins has been a topic of discussion almost since the adoption of the single currency. it is true that the banknote is more resistant to counterfeiting than coins, while at the same time the issuance of one and two euro banknotes will benefit the member states as the banknote is easier to use. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the value of commemorative coins is not undermined.
in writing. in the absence of fiscal union, the internal market is perhaps the best tool in the eu to achieve growth in the context of europe's difficult economic situation. the first beneficiaries of a properly and fully functioning single market are business and citizens. however, a key to further strengthening the internal market is the faster, more effective and efficient transposition of community legislation into the member states. the present report, which i voted for because it highlights this point, stresses that all the means at the commission's disposal to promote timely and high-quality transport must be used in conjunction with infringement procedures, and that the member states must abandon bureaucratic policies and quickly and effectively incorporate the resulting community law into their national legislation.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report because i believe it clearly expresses the need for constitutional, legislative and social changes in turkey in order to achieve full equality between men and women, while improving the quality of life of women in a modern state. the council also called for the protection of women from physical violence by extending the number of shelters for battered women and for action to be taken to reduce discrimination against women of kurdish origin or those who choose a different sexual orientation. the resolution also calls on the turkish government to increase the participation of girls at all levels of education and notes the progress made in this regard. finally, two important areas where women's participation needs to be strengthened are the labour market and political life. the report rightly points to the limited number of women in work, often with minimal labour rights, and the relatively small presence of women in political life, despite recent progress. in conclusion, i would like to stress that the report is balanced and that it suggests the appropriate framework for improving the living conditions of women in turkey.
president-in-office of the council. − mr president, honourable members, commissioners, the meeting of the heads of state and government on 23 may is intended to discuss informally a comprehensive set of measures designed to promote growth and jobs in preparation for the june european council. there has not been a discussion in the council of the informal european council so i will use this opportunity to provide you with the presidency’s expectations. there has been much talk about an austerity strategy versus a growth strategy. i would like to make it very clear that this is a false distinction. what we in the danish presidency have been working on throughout our period in office is a dual approach where we pursue fiscal discipline and growth at the same time. we cannot create growth if we do not have economic stability. economic growth will contribute to public finances, and structural reforms will contribute both to consolidation efforts and to increasing growth and competitiveness. i believe that there is a wide agreement among member states on this dual approach. this informal european council meeting on 23 may will provide an opportunity to focus on the various measures that form part of the eu’s growth agenda. the aim is to inject further momentum into that process. the danish presidency warmly welcomes this increased momentum. the meeting will be a stepping stone towards the european council in june where the heads of state and government can be expected to task further work grouped under the following headings: firstly, mobilising new policies to fully support growth: the informal meeting will be an opportunity to highlight the rapid progress which needs to be made in the council and in the european parliament on a number of proposals which have the potential to boost growth. obvious examples are the single market act and the patent. the danish presidency has been particularly committed to making progress on these issues. but further efforts are needed, in particular to help boost employment, and particular attention is also needed to tackle youth unemployment. the presidency therefore warmly welcomes the commission’s employment package. a further key element to the growth agenda is the external economic policy. here again the presidency, together with the commission, is preparing for a discussion in june on how to make better use of strategic partnerships in support of our trade and economic interests. secondly, gearing member states’ policies to growth: it is essential that we continue with the reform agenda, which remains crucial to ensuring job-creating growth. this will be dealt with in more detail in june when we will bring the european semester to a close and endorse country-specific recommendations to guide member states in their structural reforms and employment policies. finally, on financing the economy, we must continue to pursue an approach based on smart fiscal consolidation. that means reducing debt and deficits, but in a way which allows scope for continuing to invest for the future. there are a number of specific initiatives which are likely to be raised at this week’s meeting and which will certainly need to be developed further. firstly, the eib could be better mobilised to support smes and help finance key infrastructure projects. heads [of state and government] may be invited to agree to the principle of increasing the banks’ capital and of strengthening existing joint instruments. it is also important that agreement be reached on the project bonds pilot phase, which we hope to achieve at our negotiations with parliament today. secondly, the structural funds should be better focused on competitiveness and convergence. for the current funds this means reallocation with an emphasis on smes, youth training and employment. we shall make sure that the eu’s future budget, including cohesion, is targeted to sustain and develop growth and employment. the meeting tomorrow is informal. there will be no preparatory discussions in the council and no formal conclusions. the purpose will be to agree on principles and ideas for generating growth in europe. let me underline that the danish presidency has from the very first day consistently stressed the overriding priority which is attached to putting europe back on the growth track. it has been the focus of the informal european council in january, the european council in march, and now again in may and june. we have been working hard to ensure rapid adaption of relevant legislation. we are grateful that this objective is shared by parliament and that we have enjoyed constructive cooperation. but now we need to take the next steps, and that is the task ahead for the council, for this parliament and for the commission. we need to work together to secure growth and jobs in europe.
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, let me first say on behalf of president barroso that he very much wanted to be here today to have this debate with you. however, his attendance at the g8 and the nato meeting in chicago made it impossible for him to be back from the us in time. on his behalf and with the team, together with my colleagues michel barnier and lászló andor, let me outline to you the commission’s action plan for growth and jobs with a view to tomorrow’s informal summit. once again we find ourselves at a turning point in the debt crisis. at the same time, the european union is now witnessing a new level of debate about growth and how precisely to achieve growth, which is definitely positive and indeed necessary. from the outset the commission has insisted on a twin-track policy response that combines both stability and growth. from the outset we have combined concrete proposals for stability with concrete proposals for growth. the commission is grateful that this house has supported those ideas since very early on. it is very good news that the member states are now also more willing to take action in this regard. i see tomorrow’s dinner of heads of state and government as an opportunity to seize the moment for growth, and as a major stepping stone to the european council at the end of june 2012. on 30 may, i.e. between the two councils, the commission will present its country-specific recommendations, addressing the specific economic challenges facing each and every member state as well as the union generally. on 30 may we will also set our line on implementing the stability and growth pact in a growth-friendly and differentiated way, applying its inbuilt scope of judgment, which focuses on the structural sustainability of public finances over the medium term and takes into account the fiscal space and macro-economic conditions of each member state. we will use these opportunities in the summits to urge member states to deliver on the commitments they have made to strengthen stability, implement structural reforms and boost sustainable growth and employment. we will urge member states to continue to build the bedrock of economic stability. pursuing sound public finances is really not an option but a necessity. we can never have sustainable growth as long as we have an unsustainable debt. we are starting to see a rebalancing of our economy. deficits are falling across europe. there is simply no other way to build confidence and cut borrowing costs. every euro spent on servicing debt is a euro less for jobs and investment. in our schuman day statement two weeks ago, the commission called for accelerated implementation of the europe 2020 strategy, which has been endorsed by each and every member state as a means of accelerating action for jobs and growth in the short term. this will be the key message president barroso will bring to tomorrow’s informal european council. he will urge the heads of state and government to back a growth initiative that will serve as a catalyst for faster and more robust economic recovery. the commission will present this in more detail before the june european council. we would also remind you that the europe 2020 strategy is the agenda for the long-term health and sustainability of our economy. meeting its climate and energy targets alone could create up to five million new jobs in europe, putting almost a quarter of our unemployed people into work. but europe 2020 must be for today as well as for tomorrow. with the right political pressure, we can start to make a difference today. let me give you just two very concrete and very topical examples. first, we have asked member states to give the commission a mandate to negotiate with third parties on tax evasion and fraud. national treasuries lose revenues through tax evasion, and these are losses they simply cannot afford. twenty-five member states want the european union to move ahead in these negotiations, while two again blocked the measure at the last ecofin council at a cost of potentially tens of billions of euros for european taxpayers. another example: we need to step up the pace of trade negotiations with the key global partners. if europe is to grow, it needs to ride the wave of global growth. our exports to korea, for instance, shot up by 20% last year – 2011 – following the entry into force of our free trade agreement. there are many more opportunities out in the world economy and we must do all we can to seize them. honourable members, there are many measures that we can take quickly to strengthen our economies and send a message of confidence. promoting public and private investment is a cornerstone of our growth initiative. we will first of all propose that member states commit a further eur 10 billion to the european investment bank. this could unleash many times that amount in new investment. the eib could expand its lending volume, which is a quick and effective way of channelling badly-needed stimulus to the real economy. let me remind you that this is a proposal which president barroso put before this house on 28 september 2011, in his state of the union address. now there is momentum to get it off the ground, with a focus on financing innovative and competitive small and medium-sized enterprises, especially in countries and regions where such financing is most needed. the eib and the structural funds can also be used more effectively through limited risk-sharing with private investors. we welcome, too, the excellent progress in the negotiations with parliament and the council recently on our project bond proposal, and we look forward to an agreement in the trialogue meeting later today. we estimate that the project bonds could unlock up to eur 4.6 billion of investment in infrastructure and innovation already in a pilot phase. we will also urge immediate agreement on our growth-focused budget for next year, 2013, with agreement by the end of the year on the multiannual financial framework (mff) for 2014 to 2020. this will be an important signal of europe’s readiness to invest in its future: in job-rich green growth and innovation-based reindustrialisation in europe. and, in truth, the mff is our marshal plan for europe. we will look at further optimising the use of the structural funds. we want to invest them better and use the funds as catalysts to boost sectors such as renewable energy sources. we will also continue to work for a swift adoption of the financial transaction tax proposal which the commission put forward last september and on which you are voting, i think, tomorrow or the day after. in june, we will bring forward new detailed proposals on how its eur 57 billion a year of revenue could be used for targeted investment. on the structural side, there are many things we can do that cost little but can have a major impact on our economy. the single market for goods has opened up vast opportunities for economic dynamism but we must be blunt and honest here: it is incomplete and unbalanced. it does not currently work for services, and that penalises those member states that most urgently need to catch up. it is also failing to bring out the full potential of the digital economy. president barroso will tomorrow urge the heads of state and government to implement the single market act quickly, starting with the european patent, which must be adopted in june. it is high time to clear the way for the bright innovators of today to become europe’s successful entrepreneurs of tomorrow. under the leadership of michel barnier, and in order to keep up the pressure for implementation, the commission will present a single market governance scoreboard in june, as well as proposals to get more out of the services directive. in addition, whilst pursuing the growth agenda, we must address the social emergency that many europeans face today with the same determination with which we address the economic challenges. commissioner andor is steering our work in this field, where our focus is on europe’s most immediate social challenges. the most pressing challenge is, of course, unemployment, and especially youth unemployment, which in some countries is at intolerable levels. in january the president announced the creation of action teams to mobilise efforts in the eight member states with the highest proportion of young people out of work. he will present the results of these efforts tomorrow. looking beyond the immediate horizon, i believe that a long-term perspective on the future of economic and monetary union is necessary to keep us all moving in the right direction. therefore, we need to reflect on what kind of european union would be required to deepen economic and political integration – for instance, so that joint issuance of debt would make sense for all member states sharing the single currency. last november’s green paper on stability bonds will be followed up soon, ideally with a medium-term to long-term roadmap that outlines the deeper fiscal and economic integration necessary in order to minimise moral hazard and ensure fiscal sustainability – in other words, the features of an economic and political union required to make mutualisation of debt rational for all. allow me to close with a word on greece. the commission’s message to the greek people is this: we want greece to remain part of the european family and of the euro. after living beyond its means for too long, the journey greece is now undertaking is difficult and demanding, but it is a journey we are taking together in a pact of solidarity. the euro area has stood by greece in all of its difficulties and we will continue to do so, not only through the unprecedented levels of financial assistance contained in the two packages and the debt write-down – that together are equal to eur 33 600 per greek citizen – but also through the many assistance and support programmes the effective implementation of which depends on the determination of the greek leaders and people. i am talking here about programmes to get the structural funds working on the ground where they are needed: loans for small and medium-sized enterprises, support for young people without jobs, and technical assistance to reform tax systems to make them efficient and fair, and to eliminate red tape for businesses. we are ready to stand by greece on its journey of reform. we want to stand by greece. having said that, greece must remain determined to reform. we are convinced that there is no easier way. we are convinced that the alternative would be far worse. so let me take this opportunity to call on all responsible forces in greece to use these few precious weeks to get the facts across, so that the greek people can make a fully informed and responsible choice. i will conclude. there is no silver bullet for economic growth. if there were, it would have been fired already. the build-up of debts, deficits and imbalances in our economies did not happen overnight. it happened over many years, and the rebalancing we are now going through will not be concluded overnight either. that is why it is so important to stay the course on sustainable and sound public finances. at the same time, the commission strongly supports the renewed drive for growth that is now sweeping across europe. we are confident that there is now the political will and determination to move forward, starting with many of the proposals for sustainable growth that the commission has already put on the table. now is the moment to go further with our actions for growth and to get them working at the service of europe’s citizens. we count on you here in this house to help make it happen. there is no reason to wait. indeed there is no time to lose.

mr president, this council must send a message to the european citizens that the union is not just a lending machine which, if we obey, gives us the loan but if we disobey, threatens to cut off our lending. greece, with the collapse of its political centre, is on its way to becoming either a new weimar republic or a new cuba, the mediterranean. the measures imposed on the country were imposed with such speed and such brutality that even we greek politicians could not describe them because we did not have time. that's why we had this reaction from society. we greeks know what awaits us if we return to the drachma. but we do not know what awaits us if we remain in the euro, with all that we have to do and want to do to remain in the euro. commissioners, give tomorrow's message on the future of europe. we have to be very clear about what we are going to say to the people, explain why we are staying in europe, why this is the future, why the new generation will behave like this, why this is what will happen. we have no arguments. the european union is a political union, a political union of the people, a political union of the people, a political union of the people. this is not the europe we dreamed of.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the question is why we are holding an informal summit tomorrow. is it to defuse the strong opposition to the extreme neoliberal policies being implemented in the european union? it is done to give false hopes so that the doubts and criticisms expressed in greece, france and possibly ireland are not generalised, or there is a genuine willingness to change the climate and to have a change of policy, which i do not see. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. they are fanatically oriented towards this policy and this policy they will follow. we cannot therefore expect these leaders to adopt a different policy. mr oli ren, is it the same again for responsible and irresponsible political forces in greece? again the same for uninformed greek citizens? i can assure you that the greek people, like the people of europe, are informed and responsible. neither your speeches nor the summits that are supposed to discuss the issue of development can distract them from the radical changes they are calling for in a europe of cohesion, democracy and solidarity.
the problem is not only the debt of certain countries but also the differences between them in the type of development. this has created discord and problems of cooperation in the institutions. the image of the european union, despite progress at treaty level, is reminiscent of the tower of babel. what is needed today is not just the stability that comes from austerity or just growth, but a combination of the two. a country with a high debt burden like greece obviously needs a programme of austerity and many structural changes to boost growth. yes, we greeks have an obligation to our creditors, but our creditors today are our partners. the greek people know their obligations but any people in want and feeling that their future is dark, can make desperate decisions in the coming elections. the european parliament has been the subject of a number of reports on the subject, and i am sure that the commission will be able to give its opinion on them. commissioners, ladies and gentlemen, instead of some people saying or suggesting that greece is the problem and that it should be removed from the euro zone, something that nobody in greece really wants, let us all help to lower the tone and to make the problems understandable, to find a more balanced way, one that fits the european economy and the social model. we can all benefit from the good practices of other countries, but the theory of a model, for example the german one for all, seems to be at odds with the harsh reality. i doubt that tomorrow's council will bring any new ideas. real solutions require going beyond the established mindset in the institutions. europe is no longer the sure winner in the world system. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption, and the council will be consulted on the matter.
mr president, it is now almost four years since the start of the eurozone crisis and it is now clear that the recipe for unilateral austerity that the conservative majority of the council has imposed on the weak countries is a recipe for disaster. my country, greece, has lived through it and is still living through it in the most dramatic way. this policy plunged the country into recession, blocking any prospect of recovery. and the predictions were always wrong. while the forecast for the first half of '12 was 5%, the recession is finally looking to exceed 6%, making it imperative to adjust the program. if the union cannot propose measures for growth which will combat the recession and create new jobs, the death spiral into which other countries besides greece have been plunged will continue. in greece, every family has one unemployed person. if he is over 50, his chances of finding a job are almost nil. the absence of a development arm in the programme for greece is strangling an entire people and preparing the ground for extreme anti-european voices. it is time for concrete, personalised development programmes to turn the tide. we all have a great responsibility.
