on behalf of the efd group. mr president, i congratulate mr rueda on his report, because in addition to proposals for incorporating the new iccat recommendation on the management of tuna stocks, which is certainly one of the most profitable fisheries, he has also presented us with a number of proposals concerning the overall proper management of the marine environment. the fisheries sector is at a critical point according to the european commission, with 75% of fish stocks overfished and, in the mediterranean basin in particular, 82% overfished. the issue of illegal (iuu) fishing should be given priority. particular attention should be paid to combating illegal fishing and to effective control, especially in relation to third countries which are not members of the european union. it is not disputed that vigo and the european fisheries control agency have achieved positive results. however, it is not enough.
rapporteur. − mr president, allow me first to thank the shadow rapporteurs for their contributions and their productive work, which was indispensable for the finalisation of this report. allow me also – although this is not customary – to thank the commission, and especially commissioner šemeta, because despite the initial reluctance to introduce an eu financial transaction tax, the commission modified its position, endorsed the main points of parliament’s arguments adopted in various resolutions in the past, and finally responded to our call and presented a legislative proposal. i consider it a very fortunate coincidence that we are having this debate in plenary today, on the same day as the informal european council summit, whose agenda is exclusively dedicated to growth and job creation, because i believe that the financial transaction tax is an integral part of europe’s strategy to exit the crisis. this is because it can raise significant revenues which can be used for stimulating growth, job creation and smart fiscal consolidation as well as public goods such as development goals and tackling climate change. this will bring a fairer and most balanced distribution of the cost of the crisis, which is today being borne by labour and the productive economy. let me remind you that we are all in favour of a global financial transaction tax, since the broader the jurisdiction the more effective the tax. our challenge is how to reach a global agreement, and since europe has the largest financial market in the world it is up to us to make the first step and open the way. that is why we are in favour of an eu-wide financial transaction tax. we are aware that the unanimity rule makes it exceedingly difficult for us to advance on such issues. we have taken huge steps on enhanced economic and budgetary rules and compliance, which contrasts with a lack of progress on tax coordination. a minimum of tax coordination in the context of this enhanced budgetary and economic surveillance is certainly a must. it is an integral part of the new architecture which we are designing. we cannot be held hostage by a handful of member states, and that is why we can support, as a first step, enhanced cooperation among a certain number of member states – once we have exhausted our efforts to achieve an eu-27 agreement, of course. on the technical aspects of the proposal, the success of the financial transaction tax depends on its design, which should leave no scope for tax avoidance. the report seeks to strengthen the design of the directive and make sure that the framework will be watertight and tax evasion minimised. we have introduced two new elements: the issuance principle, which complements the residence principle, and the ownership principle, which ensures effective enforcement of the law. this is a financial transaction tax which can safely be introduced at eu level, or by certain member states, without significant risk of relocation. let us keep in mind that the cost of relocation will be much higher than the cost of compliance, due also to the very low rates. let me underline that the clear precondition for an effective financial transaction tax is its wide scope, covering all transactions without exemptions. exempting institutions from the scope of the tax could pose systemic risks and encourage risky strategies. exemption would open the door wide to tax avoidance. dear colleagues, i call on you to support by a large majority this tax, without exemptions, because it is one of the fairest and most effective tools we have to exit the crisis and ensure that it does not happen again, and also because i believe that it is now time to give substance to our claim that the financial sector should be at the service of the real economy.
mr president, i should like to begin by thanking the rapporteur, mrs podematias, the shadow rapporteurs and all those who contributed with their amendments and their work to the report you are proposing today. i would like to believe that this report will be a positive contribution by the european parliament to the fight against speculation and to the issue of supervision and financial sector consolidation. at the same time, it would be desirable to reach the broadest possible agreement in the context of the coordination of fiscal and budgetary policies and to include this tax there, in order to avoid distortions in the single market, although, as we all know, such an agreement is difficult to reach. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the financial sector is not left behind. it is an objective of the union which i consider very important. we must also see to it that the much-discussed development of the present time, which the union so badly needs, is not impeded. i therefore support the amendment to exempt investment funds and also pension funds. finally, i want to talk about the issue of resources. it has always been my notion and i have linked this tax to resources and to their availability. i believe that this is an opportunity for the union's own resources to have a source and for this tax to be used to give the impetus needed for growth in europe. it's a moment of creation and we must seize it.
member of the commission. − mr president, honourable members, minister, i want to thank you for your strong support for an eu ftt, which is an important signal to the member states. discussions in council continue, and i would like to thank the danish presidency for keeping up the momentum in the technical analysis and the negotiations process. at the informal ecofin council in march 2012, ministers agreed that work should continue on the commission’s proposal. on this basis, options which could gather a higher level of support in the short term should be explored. i welcome the presidency’s intention to have an orientation debate at the 22 june ecofin. in the meantime, i will continue to defend the broad-based ftt at eu level. this is what we need for fair taxation, a level playing field in the single market and to meet global challenges such as climate change and development. we heard a lot of arguments in favour of an ftt at eu level, and it is right. we also heard some concerns on specific points. the further technical work by member states’ experts will bring clarity and solutions and, of course, the commission will contribute to this work. let me emphasise one general point. the ftt will help to rebuild the relationship between the financial sector and the ordinary citizen, which has been greatly damaged in recent years. in helping to restore consumer confidence, and pointing the financial sector more towards the real economy, the ftt can become an important contributor to our growth agenda.
rapporteur. − mr president, firstly in order to be consistent in our position, let me remind you that in europe today there are already nine countries that have a form of ftt in place, including the most thriving financial centres in this continent. these jurisdictions are still major financial centres despite these taxes. that clearly means that a carefully designed ftt does not lead to relocation of financial institutions. secondly, some colleagues expressed fears about the negative effects of a financial transaction tax on growth, enterprises and households. the truth is that enterprises and households are suffering right now as a result of the financial crisis, caused by the financial sector because of high vat levels and high taxes on labour. regarding exemptions, i fully agree with commissioner šemeta that exemptions create distortions and room for loopholes. i would like to remind you that in the committee on economic and monetary affairs, we reached a compromise provisionally exempting pension funds until the review of this directive. we did this in the spirit of compromise – a compromise which i regret is not yet in place – despite the fact that the ftt would have minimal impact on conservative funds. this compromise can better secure the pensions of europeans by incentivising them to follow prudent investment strategies. but if we were to suggest further exemptions on investment projects with risky investment policies that would not be in line with what we want to achieve. in conclusion, i would like to urge you to endorse by a large majority the report for a common system on an eu financial transaction tax. this would send a strong political message to the council – especially today since there is the informal summit of the european council on growth – and confirm that this house has been, and remains, consistent with the major policies and approaches that it has defended from the very beginning of this crisis.
in writing. regulation of this class of oil tankers at european level follows major natural disasters caused by oil spills from tanker wrecks, such as the prestige disaster in 2002. in order to learn from past mistakes, this regulation seeks to reduce the risks of pollution of european waters by hydrocarbons through the introduction of the double-bottoming system. in short, the eu proposes that cargo tanks should have a second inner casing, at a sufficient distance from the outer casing. this design will protect the cargo tanks from avarice and thus reduce this risk of pollution. it is clear that for greece, a country surrounded by the sea and through which a large number of oil tankers pass annually, this regulation is important. that is why i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. china's economic growth is directly dependent on the economic prospects and capabilities of its partners. this means that its own trade policy must respect the production base in europe and the corresponding european policies. the balance of trade between china and the eu is therefore an essential precondition for the smooth continuation of the chinese economy and for the return of growth to the european economy. the european community has therefore been demanding this reciprocity in trade between the two sides for a long time. the eu is calling on china to become, among other things, more import-friendly for the benefit of all. the report also notes that the eu urgently needs to implement a coordinated re-industrialisation policy, which greece should also take seriously, and should encourage the purchase of european products. it is not just a question of defending a model of social market economy, but of defending the european concept of development, democracy and human rights.

mr president, the failure of the efforts so far to bring peace to the two sudanese states, and in particular the failure of the recent peace agreement, does not bode well for the chances of avoiding new conflicts that have been going on for decades and have resulted in the continued impoverishment of the two countries. the two countries are economically interdependent and therefore ways of cooperation must be found. to this end, both states must cease to support and encourage the enemies of the other side, as this makes the peace process more difficult. the weakening of sudan, due to its dependence on the south, may result in a weakening of its relations with its african neighbours and possibly in the strengthening of relations with arab and islamic countries and the rise of islamic forces. this is a matter which must be given special attention. sudan's economic situation has deteriorated significantly in 2012 due to the cessation of oil flow. the economy is thus expected to contract by 9.4% this year and inflation is also expected to soar to 19% in 2012 and to be on the rise. the european union must take account of these worrying developments and the high commissioner must act accordingly.

the first item on the agenda is the report by mrs edit bauer, on behalf of the committee on women's rights and gender equality, on the application of the principle of equal pay for men and women workers for equal work or work of equal value (2011 2285).
member of the commission. − madam president, i would like to thank all the speakers for their contributions. i can assure you that they will be noted, including the more critical comments. the gender pay gap is a complex phenomenon and goes far beyond the issue of equal pay for equal work. as you know, and as illustrated by the life-cycle approach to the problem, there are various factors that can explain this inequality between women and men. in addition to undervaluation and horizontal segregation, women and men’s choices of studies and careers are often influenced by traditional stereotypes. this leads to gender segregation in labour markets, where female-dominated occupations are undervalued; to vertical segregation, whereby it is more difficult for women in the labour market to reach higher positions; to inequality in the burden of caring, or, as one speaker rightly put it, different private-public alignment of lives; and, indeed, to direct discrimination within the same job. the consequences are lower pensions and, as was mentioned, a higher risk of poverty for women than for men. obviously, this should not be acceptable in the 21st century, at least not in europe. so the problem deserves all our attention, and i agree with the rapporteur, ms bauer, that we need a holistic, multi-level approach. yes, we need legislation, but we also need implementation of the legislation. yes, we need public awareness campaigns, best-practice sharing, better statistics, better information and more research. we are working in all these areas and we need the efforts of all the stakeholders to achieve solutions. let me reiterate that the commission intends to present a report to parliament on the application of the 2006 directive, reviewing the operation of that directive with a specific focus on the application of the principle of equal pay in practice. obviously, this will be informative for future steps. the commission is constantly monitoring whether the existing legal framework is being correctly applied in practice at national level. it is our job to do this for all types of legislation, and it includes considering whether member states are applying the appropriate sanctions as required by the directive. we are also preparing a study assessing several legislative and non-legislative actions to tackle the gender pay gap, and that will feed into the implementation report on the 2006 directive. it is scheduled to be published in 2013 but the exact date of publication has not yet been determined. i would also like to mention, in answer to some of your questions about the introduction of quotas for women on company boards, that the public consultation on this issue is currently running until the end of may. the results of the consultation will then feed into the decision on whether to introduce legislation. to conclude: the bauer report sends clear and important signals, and the commission appreciates the support of this house in tackling this challenging issue. we all agree that equal pay is no longer just a matter of fairness: it is also a necessary condition from an economic point of view. the commission counts on the support of the european parliament to make further progress and successfully tackle the gender pay gap.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place today, thursday 24 may, at 12 noon. written declarations (rule 149)

in writing. the eu's macro-financial assistance (mfa) has been in operation since 1990 and is designed to provide assistance to third countries experiencing short-term balance of payments difficulties. to date, more than 20 countries have been helped with loans or grants totalling more than eur 7 billion. however, the european parliament has recently called for a specific framework regulation on the mfn and the commission has responded to this request, which seeks, inter alia, to create a formal legal instrument and a more efficient and streamlined decision-making process in this area. the commission proposal does, however, have some gaps and confuses certain actions, for example by allowing the imf to be involved in these actions to such an extent that it effectively reduces the eu to a subsidiary role. the present report highlights these points and that is why i voted in favour of it.
in writing. i believe that in our time it is unacceptable to discriminate against people on the basis of their sexual orientation. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat homophobia, including the introduction of a european anti-homophobia law. the fact is that each country in europe has its own culture and its own pace of adaptation and development, and so we cannot impose exactly the same laws on all countries without exception. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to promote equal opportunities for men and women in the european union, including the creation of a european network of equal opportunities for women. europe must set a good example to the world, especially to countries where homosexuals are often sentenced to death.
in writing. the unprecedented economic crisis europe is experiencing is, among other things, a danger signal for the future use and exploitation of its resources. our planet is expected to reach 9 billion people, and according to the food and agriculture organization of the united nations (fao), food production must increase by 70 percent by 2050, while 60 percent of the world's ecosystems are being degraded or used in unsustainable ways. it is clear that europe and with it greece certainly need a new agenda and policy for the future development model. to do this requires not only technical but also political and institutional changes in the member states. the european parliament, in this report, which i voted in favour of, considers it its duty to stress the need for member states to take initiatives by setting specific and prioritized priorities in this direction.

in writing. i voted in favour of this report. the fact is that the gender pay gap as a phenomenon still exists. the latest official eurostat figures confirm the gender pay gap and show that the pay gap ranges between 4.4% and 27.6% in the member states, despite the numerous legislative provisions in force on the subject. the proposals for a regulation on the introduction of job evaluation systems free of racial prejudice and discrimination, and the creation of a single action guide to be consulted by companies and member states for the determination of the value of work and the comparison of occupations, must be put into practice immediately. it is a primary concern of each member state to apply both the new and the old principles of equal pay for men and women in order to speak, in the year 2012, of work of equal value.
member of the commission. − mr president, first of all an answer to some technical questions which have been asked. there was a question on whether food security ngis were included in the scoping. the answer is yes, food security is covered by the eu rules on sanitary and phytosanitary issues which themselves are part of all eu free trade negotiations. the same applies to geographical indications; specifics of course will be addressed during the negotiations. there was also a question on mutual recognition of organic produce. recognition of eu organic foods in japan is one of the early deliveries on our ntb list so this problem is being rectified and will be solved in practice before any negotiations start. the second set of questions are about the role of inta in these negotiations. i believe that in the case of japan the european parliament has been duly informed that we were about to propose that the council authorise negotiations, first orally by myself and director-general demarty in the inta meetings of april and may respectively, and secondly in writing by jean-luc demarty through an e-mail to inta chair moreira on 17 april. also director mauro petriccione debriefed the japan monitoring group of inta in may on the state of play of the scoping exercise and the european parliament now has an opportunity to give its views on this resolution. the commission has fully lived up to the commitments under the framework agreements. by the way, the inta members have received the result of the scoping exercise but it is a restricted document which cannot be sent to all members of parliament. there were also a number of questions requesting that the commission should wait to start the negotiations until the european parliament has formally taken a decision. the procedure is that we have to propose negotiating directives to the council. the council has to decide upon those negotiating directives. it is up to the council to decide when they give such a mandate to the commission. i hope that you are able to come forward with a detailed resolution before the council takes its decision. of course the agenda is within your hands, within the hands of inta and the plenary, but i would suggest that if you could come up with a resolution by september then probably you would be in time to influence the debate in the council. questions were also asked on whether parliament should not be part of the negotiating delegation. i am against that: parliament is not made to negotiate; parliament is made to debate, to come forward with resolutions, to make proposals and ultimately to vote on the ratification of the free trade agreement that will possibly be concluded with japan. that is what is in the treaty and i think it is very important for all institutions that we keep to that treaty. nowhere, by the way, does a parliament negotiate free trade agreements. you will of course make reference to the united states, where the government can only go forward when there is a fast track, but when you look at the practice you will see that, unless there is a fast track the american congress does not conclude the agreements. the whole idea that you have an agreement and then start introducing amendments can never work; it simply does not work, it has never done so in the past and will not do so in the future either. now on the whole topic of the scoping and how we go forward, i believe there are two very important points. in the scoping, i think we have covered all items which you would normally cover in a broad free trade agreement. on top of that we have asked the japanese to give a solution on a number of non-tariff barriers. recently they have come forward with a proposal on those non-tariff barriers and we have observed that this is a satisfactory answer – provided, of course, that it is honoured in practice. that is why i have also told the foreign affairs council on trade that our idea is to come forward with negotiating directives whereby we give the japanese a year to deliver on these promises. they cannot deliver immediately because legislative and administrative instructions have to be changed. on the other hand it does not make sense to negotiate if they do not deliver. japan being a friendly country – a democracy by the way, one of the biggest in the world – i think you realise that politically it does not make sense to tell them: look, now deliver on all of these non-tariff barriers and then within a year we will see whether or not we will start negotiations. you do not do that. the only right political approach is to say ok, we have started negotiations, those are the promises which you have been making, we are going to check this within a year and then we will make our decision on whether or not we continue with the negotiations. a second and very important element which we have very clearly stated is that whenever we do something about tariffs they should do something about the ntbs so that there should be a parallel in the approach of dismantling trade impediments: on the one hand tariffs and on the other hand, the non-tariff barriers. we will keep to that very strict parallelism also in negotiations. my last remark, mr president: we are now discussing whether or not we should start the negotiations with japan; we are not discussing whether or not we agree with the result of a negotiation because there is not a negotiation yet. it is very extraordinary to ask a country you want to negotiate with, and that wants to negotiate with us, to deliver on a certain number of topics before you even start negotiations. normally you do not do that; it is quite extraordinary. what you cannot ask the commission is to say look, now first deliver; take another year and then we will see whether or not we agree to even speak to you. that you cannot do; that we will not do so we are going to go forward with our proposals to the council and then of course it is up to the council to decide on the negotiating mandate.

in writing. first of all, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on his report. the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) aims to support workers made redundant, particularly in regions and sectors that are disadvantaged by the opening up of the globalised economy. the committee's rules provide that a maximum of 0.35% of its annual amount may be allocated each year to technical assistance. the commission requested eur 1,12 million for 2012 for this purpose. the council proposed reducing the amount of technical assistance requested to eur 730 000, on the grounds that the future of the egf in the next multiannual financial framework remains unclear. although the european commission and the european parliament accepted the council's proposal, we should make it clear that the negative consequences of globalisation, combined with the continuing economic crisis, are further highlighting the added value of the egf as an instrument of eu social policy. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. in addition, the support provided is a practical expression of eu solidarity.

madam president, madam vice-president, the defence and protection of human rights is the basis for the establishment and functioning of democracy, for social peace, for coherent development, for sound international relations, and is therefore rightly a cornerstone of the union's external policy. the instruments we have so far - political dialogue, trade agreements with human rights clauses - have proved inadequate. i therefore welcome the initiative to appoint a special envoy for human rights and support the recommendation of parliament to the council. i think it will give visibility and coherence to our policy. the human rights expert must be able to work independently and to work in close cooperation with the relevant authorities, civil society and other international and regional organisations. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. that is why i believe that it will be a good choice. we see the importance of protecting human rights in the arab states for good relations and peace in the world. the european parliament has been a party to the european convention on human rights since the beginning of the century, and it is not the council that has been the party to the convention. i believe that it is time to give a coherent and effective response, and the appointment of this new institution will help to achieve this.

the next item is the statement by the vice-president of the commission / high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy on the negotiations on the un arms trade treaty (2012/2636 ((rsp))
madam president, the legal vacuum and the anarchy that exists in the absence of an arms trade treaty have tragic consequences, cause human misery and betray the european union's commitments to promoting the rule of law, human rights and peace in the world. we therefore strongly support the need for a comprehensive, legally binding and robust treaty covering all aspects of the trade in conventional arms, including small arms, hunting weapons and their ammunition. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the treaty is fully applied. without transparency, the practical implementation of the treaty cannot be ensured, nor can there be progress in combating the corruption that often accompanies the arms trade. the original text of the resolution we are discussing today contained good references to this, but these were removed by amendments by the people's party. we hope that the european parliament's plenary will reconsider this position and send a strong message of support for the establishment of a binding arms trade treaty, a message of crucial importance in the run-up to the crucial united nations conference next month. the european parliament has already expressed its views on the situation in the middle east and the situation in the middle east.

and i have seen that it has been well received, particularly in africa - is that 600 listed and unlisted companies should be obliged to say what they pay and to whom in the extractive industry and in the forestry sector. i am also careful not to increase the burden on small and mediumsized enterprises, as mrs thein has requested, but we have a duty to encourage transparency. it's a just cause. i thank mr bodu again - vice-president of the commission. − mr president, honourable members, i would like to thank you for this discussion. i am convinced that the proposed recast directive on a european railway area is a good deal after hard work, and i hope that you will vote in favour of the proposal. with regard to our objective, various mysterious, important and sensitive words have been repeated, but i will simply say that our objective is to create a fast and reliable cross-border railway system which can serve people and businesses. many barriers remain. the proposed, or intended, fourth railway package will propose additional steps towards taking down these barriers, including technological barriers which are very substantial. so let us not mystify matters. liberalisation is not a goal in itself: it is a part of the whole package. of course, i am happy to answer the honourable member – who has left the chamber – who asked about the money for investment. i hope that the whole house will support the connecting europe facility in the commission’s proposal for the next multiannual financial framework. it increases resources for railways substantially. so, yes, we have thought this out. the commission was also accused of forcing somebody to privatise railways. this has never been the case. the commission has never forced anybody to privatise anything. as regards the proposal for polish derogation, we are trying to create a single european railway area: we are not yet trying to create a single eurasian rail area. how can we achieve our objective with derogations, exemptions, exceptions, opt-outs and abstentions? creating green jobs while finding innovative solutions to our water challenges. in addition to implementation, your report acknowledges integration as another major focus of action to be covered by the blueprint. in this regard the blueprint’s capacity to deliver on agriculture and cohesion policy depends very much on the agreement that parliament will reach with the council on the multiannual financial framework. the commission proposals have good potential for improving water efficiency and irrigation, increasing water metering, and promoting reliance on green infrastructures, such as flood plains, and wetland restoration or buffer-strip protection along riverbanks. our proposal on the common agricultural policy also clearly envisages the addition of the water framework directive to cross-compliance. the blueprint can translate the green potential of the commission proposals into concrete possibilities, but this will only happen if you and the council lend your support to the essence of the ideas that we have put forward. another major theme of your report that i would like to cover relates to addressing gaps in water policy. our assessments show that there are limited but important gaps that we need to fill. the two most important concern water re-use for agriculture and industrial uses, and water efficiency in buildings. the blueprint will propose ways forward, such as examining eu standards to facilitate the take-up of water reuse and the efficiency of appliances in buildings. any future proposal on this will, as usual, have to be subject to a thorough impact assessment. i am happy to see that you have identified the same gaps, as well as the necessity to improve our water infrastructure and reduce leakage. the blueprint will also address this, for instance by highlighting ways to remain within sustainable leakage level, and to finance water infrastructure through the cohesion policy. finally, with regard to global aspects, the blueprint will focus on ways of delivering on our rio+20 commitments, in particular in relation to the water-related millennium development goals and to a human right to drinking water and sanitation. of course we look forward to engaging with parliament in the follow-up work to the 2012 water blueprint.
the committee on petitions' opinion was drawn up by mr glinne, rapporteur. - the mr president, in my report on behalf of the committee on petitions, i had to include all the problems that have arisen with regard to water, water which is part of our existence. some of the problems identified in the opinion adopted unanimously are the uneven implementation of the framework directive, the adequacy of water resources, the sources of water pollution, the conflict with other european policies and the involvement of citizens at all stages of the procedures, as provided for in the framework directive, both in identifying problems and in finding appropriate means. the opinion places particular emphasis on the disposal of hazardous toxic and radioactive waste which is discharged into water and landfills, with a high impact on the environment and public health. but what i think is central is that water should not be a source of profit but, on the contrary, public ownership and management of water and water infrastructure should be ensured away from market rules. i'm sounding the alarm for upcoming developments. in greece and portugal, the troika is pressing for the privatization of water companies. we know from the past that where this has happened there has been an increase in tariffs, the water quality has not improved, there are court decisions, they have been re-nationalized. in greece, unfortunately, on the eve of the elections, the interim government sent the water companies of athens and thessaloniki to the privatization fund. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area.

simultaneously. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we need to shield our banking system so that we can market. we must develop transeuropean networks, make use of the structural funds to make all regions attractive for investment in accordance with their comparative advantages. at international level, implementation of the european union's external action programmes, and to address fiscal and macroeconomic imbalances affecting international economic stability and affecting the union's development. also be a key instrument in tackling the imbalances between the north and the south, the fiscal, economic and social imbalances. an innovative proposal is therefore to set up an 'investment in writing. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on his report. macro-regional strategies help to promote cooperation, enhance synergies with major community policies and create significant european added value and should be based on the principle of multi-level governance in order to ensure cooperation between local, regional and national actors. the establishment of a macro-regional strategy in the mediterranean will be able to help the participating countries to deal with the specific problems arising from the characteristics of certain regions: drought, marine pollution and tourist development. as regards island character, the european commission should implement a strategic plan addressing the transnational disadvantages of island regions and making island regions as competitive as those inland in order to reduce the gap between the different levels of development of european regions and ensure their effective integration into the single market. this can best be achieved by allocating the appropriate resources and adopting an integrated approach in the transport and energy sectors. moreover, it would be desirable for the commission to take a positive stance when examining state aid which is legitimate compensation in relation to the handicaps of insularity.

in writing. the report on the danish presidency of the council of the eu in the first half of 2012 was welcomed by the eu institutions and the political staff of the monopolies in the european parliament. under the danish presidency, the anti-people regulations on 'strengthened economic governance' were promoted and made eu law, the 'financial pact' was signed, measures to abolish collective agreements, wage and pension cuts, and the raising of the retirement age in all eu member states. the eu-us-nato imperialist intervention in syria and iran has been intensified to exploit the resources and the peoples of the region. this is the work of the danish 'left government'. promoting the interests of monopoly groups, capitalist restructuring and anti-worker measures across the eu, while demolishing what is left of labour, insurance and social rights, while poverty and misery increase daily. this is the usefulness of "left governments" and "left management" of capitalism for the plutocracy. to satisfy the demands and aspirations of the monopolies, in an environment of "social peace", of delusions and hopes that are crushingly denied, leaving the workers in bondage to fatalism, compromise, submission to the union of capital, hand and foot surrendered to the barbarity of capitalist exploitation.
the european council's decisions are certainly moving in the right direction, but there is still a great deal to be done before these general commitments can be translated into concrete, credible and effective decisions and actions. i would like to stress only two points: the first concerns the financial transaction tax. the european parliament would certainly prefer a decision to be taken at the level of 27 member states. but since this is proving impossible, we believe that the conditions and conditions must be created as soon as possible for those who want to and can go ahead, through enhanced cooperation, while respecting the basic features of this tax which should apply to as many financial institutions as possible and cover the widest possible range of financial transactions. the european parliament has supported the financial transaction tax and not the stamp duty. the second point concerns the decision taken by the european council to finally break the vicious circle between the banking sector and the debt crisis. this is a correct principle that should apply to everyone, regardless of the reasons that led to recapitalisation in the banking sector in each country. and i would ask you, mr president of the commission, to assure us today that this principle of equality and fairness will apply and that any country which is in a programme, if it is proved to be implementing it, can expect this equal treatment in this matter.
the european parliament, as is its custom, must say, as it always does, that it is not satisfied with the council's decisions: it wanted something more, it wanted to take a bigger step. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a good example of the need to ensure that the safety of workers is not compromised by the use of asbestos. the debt crisis and the recapitalisation of banks, which seem to concern italy and spain in particular, are also important decisions. but these decisions must concern every country implementing the programme which has been agreed on equal terms, such as greece, since this would relieve my country of a very heavy burden of some 50 billion euros and would be fair and just towards the new government which will be called upon to implement the programme which has been agreed. the financial transaction tax is also important in tackling the problem, as is the use of enhanced cooperation, since there are some countries that do not want it. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval, and the council will be able to accept it.

in writing. the kke voted against the european parliament's report on the development of macro-regional strategies in the mediterranean, as it moves in the reactionary direction of strengthening the eu monopolies in the mediterranean basin. the proposal to create a macro-regional cooperation area in the adriatic-juni region is not about 'financing actions' to meet the needs of the working people in the region, but about deepening the single capitalist market in the islands, about supporting the large business groups with hot money from the community funds, that is, with workers' money. it strengthens the eu's political and economic intervention in the western balkans and north africa region using democracy and human rights as a pretext. the report, faithful to eu policy, does not propose aid with additional resources, but a targeted direction of financing to projects with a high return on capital, which, in addition to limited jobs, create temporary jobs that are lost when the projects are completed. once again the big monopoly groups will be the winners and the big losers will be the workers who are called upon to support and increase the profits of the plutocracy at their own expense

in writing. i voted against the report as i completely disagree with the attempt by the european council and all the political groups in the ep, with the exception of the gue/ngl, to privatise european railways. i strongly disagree with parameters that create the framework for layoffs of workers, for taking responsibility for safety and maintenance of railways by private companies, the privatisation process, and in a common european direction, will deprive the member states of the possibility of rationalising, modernising and upgrading the public railway networks in order to improve the services provided to citizens and increase public revenues. the full liberalisation of the railways, even under the indirect guidance of the european institutions, will in no way ensure their upgrading. on the contrary, competition from private enterprises will increase the risks of society itself ultimately bearing the costs of possible malfunctions.
in writing. it is a fact that the relatively slow progress in the negotiations between parliament and council on agreement on this directive is delaying a major reform initiative in the eu which has the potential to bring economic benefits to many member states, including greece. however, parliament does not intend to adopt a text which weakens the basic objectives of this directive, such as access to rail transport services, infrastructure financing, infrastructure charges and the powers of the regulatory body. the fact is that the parliament and the commission are adopting a more ambitious political stance and want the directive to be transposed into the national law of the member states as quickly as possible (within 12 months), in contrast to the council, which is seeking, as regards the entry into force of this new directive, 36 months for most of its provisions. the european parliament has therefore decided not to back down on these crucial points, a position which i supported by voting in favour of the report.
in writing. the report on the recasting of the single european railway area directive is pushing even harder for the privatisation of public rail transport in all eu member states and handing it over to the large business groups operating in the sector. the so-called eu 'third railway package' completed the splitting of the rail transport project by separating the infrastructure network from the main transport project. this has facilitated privatisation, since the profitable part of the transport project is sold off, while the infrastructure network with the costs involved remains in the public domain. the reform of the 'third package' seeks to remove all obstacles (administrative and technical) to privatisation, to complete the interconnection of the railway network in all member states and to promote the takeover of state-owned railways by monopoly groups, particularly in the strong capitalist eu states. the result will be a wave of redundancies and worsening working conditions for workers, skyrocketing prices for passengers and goods, and a deterioration in the quality of transport and railway safety. this policy has been implemented and is already being implemented consistently by the governments of nd, pasok in greece and by the previous and current coalition government.
in writing. intellectual property rights (ipr) are the eu's most valuable tool for supporting innovation, which is also a key priority of the europe 2020 strategy for smart, sustainable and inclusive growth. piracy and counterfeiting are estimated to cost european businesses €250 billion a year, which of course has a significant negative impact on jobs and economic growth in the member states. the present revised proposal for a regulation, which i voted in favour of, seeks to improve ipr legislation and in particular to better implement regulation (ec) no 1383/2003 adapted to new challenges such as the sale of counterfeits via the internet.

in writing. sustainable water management ensures that ecosystems in the member states receive sufficient quantities of quality water to function and provides security of access to food and water for all. for our country, which is facing issues of overgrazing, climate change and periodic droughts, this debate is becoming vital. it is clear that future economic growth will only be sustainable if it is decoupled from the adverse environmental impacts of water. the steps taken so far by the eu and the member states are not enough, however. the 'plan to protect europe's water resources', to be published by the commission in november 2012, aims to create a european water strategy that overcomes the shortcomings and omissions that have emerged so far. the european parliament welcomes the adoption of a more aggressive strategy in this area, which i voted in favour of.

member of the commission. − madam president, there are two parallel procedures as usual on the basis of our proposal for the 2013 budget and this is being done in the very specific climate of today’s europe. these are the fiscal consolidations seen as condicio sine qua non of sustainable development in europe and on the other hand, even at a time of crisis, the need for investment to create conditions for future growth and jobs. this was clearly stated in the conclusions of the recent european summit. i think that the answer as presented by the commission in the 2013 draft budget is the correct one and i think the mandate of parliament as expressed by the rapporteur, mr la via, is going in the same direction. we need restraint – that is clear – in administration; there are performance savings in the other areas, very restrained growth of commitment, 2% only at the end of the financial perspective but, on the other hand, we need a sufficient level of payments to pay out what is legally binding – what is financially and legally binding for 2013 – and this is the end of the financial perspective that cannot come as a surprise to anybody. at the end of the financial perspective the programmes are gaining maturity and the payments are accelerating, so the bigger need for payment. there is also the specificity of the 2013 budget; the n+2 rule and the n+3 rule means that we should pay at the latest in 2013 for the commitments from 2010 and 2011. meeting of the payment obligations is not my duty. this is a collective duty of the european union. i underlined the areas with the biggest growth potential – research, development and cohesion. deferring payments is not an option because it either results in a liquidity problem in the member states or a shortage of investment possibilities and increasing appeals for the future. that was clearly reflected in the discussion over the payment methodology agreed by the chair of the committee on budgetary control. perhaps that is a modest forecast for the future on the payment levels at the end of may. i think we see a lot of understanding of the logic of the multiannual financial perspective for the annual budgets at the end of annual perspective in the mandate as expressed by mr la via. what is coming on the other side, in the council, is a matter of concern. there is a propensity to cut, even though knowledge is widespread as to what the real needs are for 2013, as we are not basing our forecasts and our draft budget on our own assumptions. this comes from the member states – we are cutting. we are less optimistic than the member states themselves in shaping the level of payments for 2013. nevertheless, i believe that we are able to find interinstitutional cooperation. i have to thank the rapporteur for already making the indicative list of pilot projects and preparatory actions available and making implementation in 2013 easier. once more, i am confident that even under the specific extreme conditions of our time, we can find a proper way of dealing with the budget for the next year.

member of the commission. - mr president, i am pleased to see that there is very broad, almost unanimous support for the way in which the compromise has been found to get this regulation through as quickly as possible, which will allow us to grant these transitional payments. there has never been any question of not granting direct payments in 2013, but it is true that we needed this regulation to specify certain elements, certain transitional measures, both for the new member states, which have a system of surface payments and which also give a supplementary payment, because of the difference which still exists in relation to the community average. these conditions have been clarified by this regulation. but it will also allow - as some of you have mentioned - certain member states, such as the united kingdom, to maintain this voluntary adjustment between direct payments and rural development in 2013 so that they can continue to finance rural development measures. some of you have already mentioned the work being done to reform the common agricultural policy for the next budgetary period. i remain very attentive to the work that the committee on agriculture is doing on the reports presented by the rapporteurs, and i hope that we will very soon begin formal discussions between the council, parliament and the commission to achieve a fairer reform of the agricultural policy, with, if possible, substantial budgetary support, commensurate with the ambitions of our agricultural policy.

in writing. − ι voted in favour of this proposal because it establishes common rules for direct support schemes for farmers under the common agricultural policy and certain support schemes for farmers. it also includes a number of provisions aimed at simplifying the current legislative environment and easing the administrative burden for member states.
in writing. 2013 is the transitional year for preparing for and adapting to the new cap 2014-2020, which will have even more disastrous consequences for small and medium-sized farmers and agricultural production, by supporting large agro-capitalist enterprises. the proposed transitional provisions contained in the commission regulation and the report of the european parliament on the transition to the new cap do not change the essence of the cap's anti-agricultural policy. nor is the possibility of transitional national support available to countries with high public debt and deficits to allocate national resources to farmers. this has the effect of increasing the inequality within the eu inherent in the capitalist mode of development, of uprooting the poor farmers more rapidly and of further strengthening the big farmers, preparing the ground for the even more anti-agricultural 'new' cap.

in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal because the amendments relating to the expenditure to be financed by the eagf and the recovery procedure constitute a simplification in that they replace two council regulations and result in a decrease in the administrative tasks incumbent on member states.
in writing. this regulation aims to establish specific rules to ensure the quality of organic products, the correct application of the rules by the authorities, bodies and undertakings concerned, the proper functioning of the single market and trade, and the correct information of consumers. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's agricultural policy is not distorted by the introduction of the organic label, and that the commission is not obliged to take any action to prevent the introduction of the organic label. in the report, which i voted in favour of, the ep reaffirms the commission's implementing powers to ensure uniform conditions for the measures taken by the member states to ensure the correct labelling of organic products.

in writing. − i voted in favour because this bilateral agreement with the russian federation was negotiated in the framework of its accession process to the world trade organization. it is important that the european parliament has been seized for consent, which is a prerequisite for the council to officially conclude the agreement.
in writing. piracy and counterfeiting cause significant economic and moral damage to the creator and clearly need to be combated at international level. at the same time, however, the attempt to tackle this phenomenon should not restrict fundamental rights such as freedom of expression, the promotion and exchange of ideas and the right to privacy, a position which i have repeatedly supported in the european parliament. in view of this, and since the court of justice of the eu has not yet examined and ruled on the compatibility of the agreement with the fundamental freedoms and rights guaranteed by community law, and since it was not possible to postpone the vote, i voted against the acta agreement in the european parliament plenary, in order to bring it back with the necessary improvements and amendments, in particular as regards the protection of freedom of expression on the internet. i am awaiting the decision of the court of justice, following the referral of the agreement by the college of eu commissioners to it, and i look forward to further initiatives and proposals to be taken by the commission on the protection of copyright and intellectual property.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report whose main political message is that the eu budget must play an important role in overcoming the current economic and social crisis, in promoting growth and job creation. as regards cohesion policy, the problem of late payments that occurred at the end of 2011 should be addressed by making sufficient payment appropriations available in 2012 so that shortfalls do not worsen in 2013, when the implementation of cohesion policy programmes is expected to accelerate. the commission has also proposed that the commission should be given the task of drawing up a report on the implementation of the programmes. i also call on the council and the commission to assess carefully the actual needs for payments under title 1b and not to make cuts which are unrealistic and unfounded and which run counter to the forecasts provided by the member states themselves, which serve as the basis for drawing up the draft budget. otherwise, the ep would have to oppose any reduction in the level of payment appropriations compared with these needs.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this resolution because there is a need to end the widespread abuse and maltreatment of pets and stray animals throughout the european union and because it proposes a legal framework at eu level. in order to protect pets and stray animals, this framework must establish and enforce, amongst others, animal management strategies, promotion of responsible ownership and establish information and education programmes in schools on animal welfare.
in writing. i voted in favour of the joint resolution. the european parliament welcomes the progress made and the measures taken by the european council to deal with the eurozone crisis. it is important that this growth is no longer on the agenda short of austerity. the council's commitment to break the vicious circle between banks and sovereign debt and to reduce the divergence between the yields on euro area sovereign bonds is moving in the right direction and is an important step for a banking union in the eu as a whole. however, these decisions require a rapid implementation programme (integrated financial framework, integrated budgetary framework, integrated economic policy framework and ensuring the necessary democratic legitimacy and accountability of decision-making within emu). the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the health of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is based on the principle of equal treatment for men and women.
in writing. the report undertakes to present the service of banking capital by opening a bank account for those who - few - do not have, as an alleged service to the needs of "citizens" and protection of their alleged right to have a bank account. the main issue of interest in the report is the development of the eu's capitalist internal market and electronic commerce, for the benefit of financial capital. the empty words about the supposed right of everyone to have access to a bank account and the non-discrimination of banks are only to mask the real purpose, which is to serve the interests of banking capital. the report's eulogies cannot conceal that banking capital uses access to bank accounts of workers-depositors to "sell" them various other "services" (credit cards, micro-loans, right to overdraft from the account, etc.), thus creating their over-indebtedness, exploiting the fact that the capitalist system cannot meet the needs of the people, thus forcing workers to resort to speculative banking capital. the report recognises the right of banks to charge 'reasonable' fees for bank accounts, i.e. to wipe out small depositors, leaving the legitimate scandals of banks with excessive charges on small depositors under various pretexts and justifications untouched.
in writing. access to basic payment services is a right of consumers to reap the benefits of the internal market and in particular of free movement, the transfer of money and the purchase of goods and services at transaction costs that do not constitute discrimination. but beyond that, access to basic payment services is gradually becoming a prerequisite for social inclusion in terms of employment, health care and housing. it should therefore guarantee it to all eu citizens, regardless of their social status and the remoteness of their place of residence. the fact that the lost opportunities due to lack of access to a payment account are estimated at between eur 185 and eur 365 per consumer reveals the importance of this service. the ep calls on the commission to present a detailed assessment of the situation in all member states by september 2012, so that all necessary measures can be taken.
mr president, on the schengen question too, we have been engaged in an endless exchange of responsibilities in recent times. we sometimes say that it is the fault of the state which is asking for internal border controls, and sometimes we say that it is the fault of another state when the external borders of that state, which are also the external borders of the union, are not adequately and securely guarded as provided for by the schengen area. i think that every time we're confronted with these kinds of accusations, we get disoriented. when the founding fathers of the european union set out to create our union, they started with a basic assumption: we are all to blame for failed policies. they said it was all our fault, and they came to a common conclusion. the european union is not only a political union, but also a political union of the peoples of europe. it's not just about helping someone who may have a weakness, it's about taking responsibility together. in this context, you will see that it is very difficult for all of us, and for the citizens who are watching us, to understand what happened last year between france and italy, or what is happening with the checks at the german border on czech buses or at the dutch border with similar situations. how can we therefore take the view that greece is not doing its job on its external borders when frontex itself agrees that greece alone, without the help of its partners and without the help of turkey, cannot secure its borders? i would like to conclude by saying that we should emphasize this, commissioner, and i would also like to address you, because we expect a lot from the commission. the member states at that time, in a fearful approach, i have spoken about several of their representatives, are taking a position on the subject of evaluation and the changes we have in the regulations with the approach that we should be handling these matters ourselves and not be more. no, we want the community method, we want to have a higher level of functioning of schengen, we want future generations to know that we have responded in this period to more europe and not less europe by going backwards on this issue.
in writing. the commission's report on the proper functioning of the schengen treaty states that 75% of illegal immigrants apprehended in the schengen area entered it via the greek-turkish border. the issue of illegal immigration should be tackled globally at european level and it is necessary to take special measures for the member states bordering the eu as they are also under the greatest pressure in a difficult period of economic crisis. i believe that the dublin ii treaty, which places a disproportionate burden on the countries of entry of illegal immigrants in terms of managing illegal immigration, needs to be revised and reviewed. the reintroduction of internal border controls in the area proposed by the commission and the justice and home affairs council of the eu does not actually address the problem, but rather highlights the political isolation of individual member states.

president-in-office of the council, on behalf of the vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − mr president, i sincerely thank all honourable members who expressed their views on this important issue. let me start with a general statement. the european union is not accepting instrumentalisation of the democratic process in pursuit of other agendas or in order to falsify popular will – anywhere or by anybody. the european union believes in free and fair elections and that fair competition and genuine participation in the upcoming elections will be fundamental in achieving the legitimacy of election results. the european union stresses that politically motivated persecution, direct or indirect, by law enforcement agencies or the use of selective justice against political contenders are not compatible with democratic values. in this regard the european union is monitoring and will continue to monitor the situation in georgia leading up to the elections. it will also monitor the conduct of those elections. the high representative baroness ashton met last week with president saakashvili. her services have met with representatives of the georgian dream coalition. both sides have asked for increased eu involvement, in particular to ensure increased transparency. the european external action service is already doing a lot with the organisation of security and cooperation in europe, including media monitoring – to which many of you referred during the debate. however, the european external action service is also looking at how it can provide further support to ensure maximum transparency. this could include further monitoring of polling, exit-polling and supporting independent civil society organisations. the european union embassies have set up a special on-the-spot taskforce led by the eu delegation. so we are watching and we are helping. overall our position on georgia is very clear. we welcome strong european union-georgia relations. we have seen good progress on the eastern partnership agenda and on the association agreement negotiations and we are delighted that steps towards visa liberalisation are now moving ahead. there are other strategic interests too. the eu recognises the increasing importance of georgia as an energy-transit country and appreciates its cooperation in developing the southern energy corridor. the eu also recognises georgia’s significant contribution to peacekeeping efforts around the globe. we have seen a lot of positive changes since the rose revolution but we need to keep up momentum. this means consolidating democracy, broadening and deepening reform efforts, in particular in the judiciary, the economy and the social sphere, and spreading the benefits of development to marginalised groups. but above all, regional security and democratic evolution are key to securing all the benefits which our association can offer.
the debate is closed.
the next item is the statement by the vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy on the situation in syria.
mr president, developments in syria are marked by an intensification of violence and an escalation of the assad government's attacks with bombings in the cities of homs and douma. many human lives, over 4,000, have been lost in the last eight months. at the same time, the spectre of political and religious antagonisms and ethnic rivalries has come to the surface. the situation on the turkish border is also becoming more tense and there is a risk of a military conflict, whether intended or not, at any time. the european union is also calling for the immediate release of all political prisoners, including those who have been held in custody in turkey, and for the immediate release of all political prisoners, including those who have been held in custody in turkey. christians in the area feel unsafe. on the international diplomatic front, the russia-west conflict remains, and moscow is refusing to attend the friends of syria summit next friday. a global crisis is looming and please we must be on the lookout for it.
mr president, we denounce the decisions of the european council of 29 june on syria and iran, because they mark an escalation of the imperialist intervention of the european union, the united states and nato in the region, in the context of fierce competition with the capitalist powers of russia, china and india, and reinforce the threats of the united states and nato, as well as turkey, qatar, saudi arabia and israel in the region. at the moment, us aircraft carriers are massing in the area and mobility is increasing at the souda base in greece. the imposition of a no-fly zone under the pretext of responsibility for the protection of civilians is being considered, which has been used in other imperialist interventions. the united states, nato and the european union and states in the region are interfering in syria's internal affairs, misinforming, exploiting the conflict to justify a new imperialist war. the developments in syria are complementary to the occupation in iraq, afghanistan and libya. we also denounce the decisions of nato, which, under the pretext of so-called atlantic solidarity with turkey, is leading the imperialist armada against syria for a strategic strike against iran. sanctions are being tightened and the european union is threatening open military intervention if the imperialist plans to overthrow the syrian government are not passed. as we know, the decisions to impose an oil embargo on iran have been in force since 1 july, with disastrous consequences. the developments in syria concern the syrian people. the peoples must resist the interventions of the european union and nato and overthrow the imperialist plans.
mr president, i would agree with the comments made by the commissioner, jo leinen and corinne lepage, and with a lot of comments from other colleagues. if governments failed in rio to agree the level of ambition we would have liked them to achieve, it is true that people see that businesses did arrive at this level of ambition. condemning the weak wording of this agreement from the earth summit in rio often makes us blind as to what has been achieved. we all wanted more in rio, but it is not true that nothing has been achieved. let us implement what has been agreed. in my view, it is often better to have a weak agreement which is fully implemented, than a wonderful agreement which is not. therefore, let us fulfil those obligations. we have many opportunities to do so. let us fulfil the obligation on the oceans. let us make the world environment organisation a reality. let us build on the momentum that has been built globally, moving beyond gdp. on the issue of roadless areas, for the first time in the framework of these earth summits, we had a presentation by two global satellite mapping tools on the world’s roadless areas. they were mapped by google maps and eye on earth. it presented the fact that the best way to protect an area is to keep it roadless. if you want to connect people within a protected area, there are other ways to do so other than by roads e.g. railways and waterways. i call on the commissioner to support this global fight and help make the protection of roadless areas a reality within the eu.
in writing. a total of €1.5 trillion is expected to be invested in europe in transport, energy and ict infrastructure over the period 2010-2020. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the creation of a european network of national experts in the field of health and safety at work. the commission has therefore proposed that the council adopt a directive on the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster. the commission proposes that the new multiannual financial framework should facilitate such investments with the connecting europe strategy. for greece, this initiative has particular significance as it focuses on cross-border sections and can therefore benefit from it. the report, which i voted for, encourages this initiative, seeks convergence and agreement with the council, but in any case it stresses the points to which particular attention should be paid.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because of the importance of project bonds for the economy of the eu and its member states. with investment in the eu steadily declining in recent years, the economic and financial crisis aggravating this situation, national fiscal constraints and bank capital requirements, new ways of stimulating investment leading to economic recovery and growth are needed. the project bonds will make investments in vital infrastructure projects more attractive to capital market investors by mitigating the risk associated with them and are expected to have a multiplier effect to mobilise further investment. in this way, project bonds are expected to play a key role in the recovery efforts of member states, and in particular those facing greater fiscal challenges. it will also make a decisive contribution to the strategy for development that many eu leaders are calling for. in addition, i would add that the risk to the eu budget, and therefore to european taxpayers, is strictly limited, without exceeding the maximum eu contribution, namely the amount of eur 230 million.
in writing. as the commission proposal shows, the 2012 budget is in surplus of eur 1.49 billion. this amount will be reduced by the total contribution of the member states to the eu budget, which is a relief, albeit a very small one, for national budgets, especially those of countries under great economic pressure. the european parliament, however, finds the council's unilateral decision to cut research expenditure by more than two thirds on the grounds of the low absorption and under-performance of the programme to be unsatisfactory for obvious reasons. this point and the ep's reaction are clearly emphasized in this report, which i voted in favour of.

madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i was sad to see that once again in parliament, this time in the committee on the environment, an amendment was voted down on the labelling of products from cattle fed on gmos. i wonder who is it that does not want the public to know what it is consuming, who is it that does not want the public to be able to choose, who is it that supports the interests of the companies or what the companies think is in their interest, namely to operate in secret. the same is true of the issue of traceability. this is a very important issue because if we have the right traceability and electronic tagging, we can actually prevent deaths, loss of life of our fellow citizens from diseases and epidemics. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur and socialist member mrs roth-behrendt on her excellent work and hope that the vote in the plenary will reverse the bad results in the committee on the environment and will work to really protect the rights of european citizens and public health in europe.

the next item on the agenda is the one-minute speeches (rule 150).
mr president, last week the troika asked the greeks to work six days a week. the greeks have reached their limits, and the troika seems to be ignoring basic facts. in its latest report, the oecd calculates working hours for the people of all its member countries. the greeks work harder than anyone: 2,032 hours of work per year. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i am concerned because the commission's priorities include, once again, the revision of the working time directive. i would remind you that when this was attempted in the previous legislature, the european parliament stood up. i would therefore like to conclude by quoting the words of the president of the commission, mr barroso: it would be wrong, in every respect, to try to dismantle the welfare state and our social models, especially in countries following austerity programmes.
