the debate is closed. the vote will take place shortly. written statements (rule 149)
the next item is question time to the commission. i should like to welcome once again commissioners oettinger and almunia. the specific topic for question time today is the state of play of the energy market acquis (3rd package) implementation. i will interpret this topic as widely as possible but i should also warn you that if your question is completely off-topic and if it deals, for example, with the common agricultural policy, you are liable to be cut off. i should also like to remind you of the ground rules. questions are taken exclusively on a catch-the-eye basis and each member has one minute to put his or her question and 30 seconds to put a supplementary. the commission has two minutes to reply if only one commissioner is to be responsible for answering. if more than one commissioner is to reply, each commissioner has one minute’s speaking time.
we took note of your remark.
member of the commission. - we have different legal bases for our europeanisation of energy policy. the first is the euratom treaty, which is still the primary law, the second is the treaty of lisbon, with its own section on the europeanisation of energy legislation, and the third is our general internal market rules, which have been in force for decades in the case of oil and coal and have been in force for 15 years in the case of gas and electricity. and then we have the 20 2020 targets as binding energy and climate policy. one thing we don't have is the choice of technology. so the question of whether nuclear power is to be used or not, or coal, if so, how much and for how long, or renewable energy beyond the binding european target, i.e. more than 20%, is a matter for national governments and parliaments. we respect that. we will be presenting our comprehensive report on our stress tests in a few weeks' time, which will provide comprehensive information on the standards, strengths, risks of the more than 130 nuclear power stations in the eu plus our partners in ukraine and switzerland, and also suggestions or demands for retrofitting, for higher standards. we shall have a debate on that. but the final decision will then remain with national governments and parliaments. we currently have about 30% of nuclear power in the european electricity mix. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's energy policy is not distorted by the fact that the community is not able to take action to combat the effects of nuclear power. we respect that decision. we are living in a multicultural europe: italy with the referendum not to build a nuclear power station, germany with the decision to withdraw in ten years, poland with two power plant plans, the united kingdom with the intention to increase the share of nuclear power in the electricity mix from 25 to 60%, and france with the election winner hollande, who wants to reduce the share of nuclear power from today's 76 to 50% by 2025.
member of the commission. - mrs hedegaard is the commission's lead on the ets. we believe that the ets is a market-oriented solution to achieve co2 emission reductions and we will go into the regulatory phase next year. we now have a problem: because of the economic stagnation, because of the crisis, the price of co2 has not been 14 to 18 euros, as it has been for many years, but 6 to 8 euros. this does not create a price signal. we are therefore considering whether we should temporarily withdraw certain emission allowances, certain quantities, from the market in order to achieve a certain increase in the market price. the commission will take a decision on this and other matters in the coming weeks. the external problem is that co2 and ets are not recognised or adopted in any other region of the world. this means that a global market for co2 emissions is not yet emerging. first, shale gas: shale gas in the united states has completely changed the global market for gas. the americans used to be big gas importers lng gas , but today they are largely self-sufficient in gas and have a cost factor: gas in the usa costs only 30% of the gas price in european markets. we presented three studies last week on shale gas and its environmental impact and risks. we are working closely together here: mr potočnik, mrs hedegaard and myself. we are accompanying demonstration projects. we welcome the fact that demonstration projects are being tried in a few member states. at the moment we believe that it would be too early for european, more far-reaching, binding rules. in general, we believe that shale gas can play a complementary role in the european gas market, but will not play a substitute role. in the long term we will also need conventional gas from third countries. shale gas will be complementary but not substitutive.
member of the commission. - there are a number of studies on shale gas. and certainly some of the clients are also in the market and therefore not necessarily completely independent. our research services are also active. we maintain that the commission is independent and that our experts are not bound by market interests. another point: renewable energy is not really an opponent of gas, but a partner. electricity from solar and wind energy requires supplementary electricity production, and gas is the natural partner. whether the gas comes from russia, norway or algeria or from our rock formations, the renewable energy partner doesn't really care.
member of the commission. - mr president, we have the same problems in transposing the single market packages into national law as in other areas of law. this is not a special case for energy, but applies equally to consumer protection, the economy and other sectors of the internal market. i do not think that we should use the european budget as a punishment when member states are late in implementing eu law. we believe that our infringement procedures are successful and that in the next few years we will see the single market law in the field of energy being implemented in all member states on a one-to-one basis. the majority of the ministers are very constructive. however, the willingness to implement is often more reserved in the parliaments of our member states.
member of the commission. − i would like to see our infringement procedures in court being dealt with more quickly. we don't need any more advanced instruments. we will show in the single market communication that we are making good progress in the implementation of our single market packages in the member states.
that concludes question time.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund for denmark to support the 550 workers made redundant by a shipyard in the city of odense and by the four suppliers and producers of the shipyard. these redundancies were the result of the opening-up to the globalised economy and the transfer of the yard's activities to asia. it is clear that the negative effects of globalisation further highlight the added value of the european globalisation adjustment fund as an eu social policy tool. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. moreover, the support provided is the practical expression of the union's solidarity. finally, the case of denmark is the best illustration of the fact that this financial instrument does not limit its activity only to countries facing economic and budgetary challenges but can be equally useful in helping the reintegration of the displaced into the labour market even in economically strong countries. this in turn underlines the importance of this fund and demonstrates the need to continue it during the upcoming multiannual financial framework 2014-2020.
in writing. − i voted for the mobilisation of the egf in favour of denmark in order to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant due to the global financial and economic crisis.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) for spain to support 320 redundancies in 377 construction companies in the aragon region. the economy of the region has been severely hit, as the opening up of the global market has adversely affected important sectors of the economy, such as construction. this situation, combined with the problems faced by the automotive industry in the same region, makes aragon one of the most economically vulnerable regions in spain. the negative effects of globalisation, combined with the continuing economic crisis in spain, further highlight the added value of the egf as an instrument of eu social policy. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. moreover, the support provided is the practical expression of the union's solidarity. the spanish regions are subject to a process of rationalization and drastic cuts. the eib's support will be able to contribute to the fight against unemployment without significantly burdening the efforts of fiscal consolidation of the spanish national budget as well as the budget of the region itself.
in writing. − the construction sector has been severely affected by the crisis in spain, but also in many other eu member states. the main reason for this is that loans have been drastically reduced, thus creating a sudden drop in demand for new houses. furthermore, i voted on the mobilisation of the egf in favour of spain in order to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant as a result of the global financial and economic crisis.
in writing. the report is an open mockery of the peoples. it refuses to condemn the governments of certain countries - including greece - which have been shown to have actively participated and cooperated with the cia and the usa in the kidnappings, illegal detentions, torture of 'terrorist suspects' and the maintenance of secret prisons on their territory. it goes so far as to call on the bourgeois governments which participated in the crimes to carry out investigations against themselves in order to reveal the truth, which is proven and known to everyone except the european parliament. the committee's position that the european parliament's committee and its reports were hypocritical and essentially aimed at defusing popular discontent and concealing the responsibilities of the governments of the eu member states is confirmed. the so-called 'war on terror' spearheaded by the eu-us and the various intelligence services, is really aimed at striking at the class movement that challenges the capitalist exploitative system, the stifling restrictions and the infringement of popular freedoms and democratic rights.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report as i believe it puts the necessary pressure on both the eu institutions and each of its member states to continue the investigations to establish the extent of the abductions, secret detentions and torture unlawfully carried out by the us intelligence services (cia), with the complicity or tolerance of european states in violation of any notion of international law and respect for human rights. the resolution expresses in the clearest possible terms the european parliament's wish that human rights should not be sacrificed in the name of fighting terrorism, that terrorism suspects should not be tried under a special judicial system, that the guantanamo base should be closed immediately by the us government, and that the un resolutions on human dignity should be respected in general. it also called on the european council to accept the obvious fact of the violation of the european treaties in the illegal transfer of prisoners by the cia through european territory, to investigate the cases and to ensure that such incidents are not repeated. finally, it rightly urges the european parliament's temporary committee to take action to further investigate cases in the event of european council blockage.
in writing. i voted in favour of the two mcavan reports (a7-0164/2012 and a7-0165/2012) because they strengthen pharmacovigilance following the scandal created by the use of the french pharmaceutical company servier's mediator drug, which brought to light serious weaknesses and malfunctions in the eu's drug control system and the unacceptable lobbying methods of the pharmaceutical companies. although the side effects of mediator were known as early as 1998, its withdrawal from the market was delayed by 8 years, resulting in about 500 deaths and 3,500 hospitalizations for heart problems. the amendments i voted for cover the gaps in the legislation, namely the lack of evaluation of a medicinal product after its authorisation, the withdrawal without automatic notification of all eu member states, the lack of justification and the lack of transparency for the withdrawal. drug safety is a huge issue. we want informed patients who can participate alongside healthcare professionals in pharmacovigilance. the amendments i voted for, namely the activation of the urgency procedure, the clarification of the transparency obligations for pharmaceutical companies and the re-registration of suspect medicinal products in the list of medicinal products subject to additional monitoring, are aimed at closing all the windows that may undermine the safety of medicinal products and the protection of public health.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because of the determination of the commission to further strengthen the pharmacovigilance system at eu level and include the relevant stakeholders, such as patients and healthcare professionals, in developing an efficient and effective system.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because, by switching to more stringent sulphur limits, it would be possible to achieve significant health and environmental benefits in a cost-efficient way by implementing the 0.1% limit more widely than just in europe’s existing secas, resulting in a different level playing field as regards the cost impact of reform.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it provides the possibility for member states to grant decoupled aid to vine growers under the single payment scheme (sps) as part of their national vine growers’ programmes. moreover, i strongly support that the european parliament underlines its position against the abolishment of planting rights in the sector.
in writing. it is common knowledge that tax evasion and avoidance costs the governments of the member states of the european union a considerable amount of uncollected revenue and the economy of my country, greece, has been hit very hard by this phenomenon. i voted in favour of the gáll-pelcz report because i believe that the creation of a common taxation system in the eu will strengthen cooperation between the member states in the fight against tax evasion and will also be another step towards a genuine monetary and economic union, just as we should have done from the beginning of the adoption of the euro.
in writing. it is clear that the scale of the budgetary crisis is testing both the cohesion and the economic policy of the union. the multiannual financial framework, which was launched in a stable and optimistic environment in 2006, is being completed the following year in a completely different economic environment. the original objectives and priorities must therefore be adapted to the new circumstances. in this joint resolution, which i voted in favour of, the european parliament opposes the importance of greater unification as a counterbalance to the crisis and stresses the need to draw up a road map immediately for the future rational and efficient use of community resources. certainly new challenges, which are of particular concern to greece, such as the widening youth unemployment, the importance of more intervention initiatives by the european investment bank, and the importance of creating a climate of economic growth and investment should be included in the new multiannual budget package.
in writing. this report is guided by the understanding that donations should be voluntary and made on a non-profit-making basis and in an environment of transparency. it is clear that 'buying' a humanitarian supply is not the right approach. on the other hand, non-monetary incentives offer moral compensation, which is also consistent with and relevant to the spirit of selflessness and generosity. good examples already in place in some member states, such as the united kingdom, demonstrate the validity of this approach. in any event, however, and beyond the final policies that member states will decide to implement in this area, it is clear that the existence of websites allowing the sale or purchase of gamete cells online raises significant legal and ethical problems and raises questions about the transparency of these transactions. the lack of a legal framework in several member states makes this situation worse, and this is the focus of the report which i voted in favour of.
in writing. the education and training 2020 (et 2020) programme is a follow-up to the education and training 2010 programme. however, in today's difficult economic environment, smart investment, production-oriented and innovation-driven initiatives must be at the forefront in order to restore positive and sustainable growth rates. although the commission proposal takes account of this to some extent, the commission communication does not include critical areas such as the demographic problem in europe, the language barriers in the eu, the greater investment in lifelong learning and retraining programmes, especially for the less-privileged social groups. education and training, which are highly social goods, should not be degraded by the crisis but should instead be seen as a means of escape from it. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval.

in writing. for another year, the council resorted to the popular tactic of horizontal cuts in all budget headings without relying on a thorough and detailed analysis. although it is widely accepted and proven that cohesion policy is the main and most appropriate investment instrument that can lead the eu to growth and job creation, the council's cuts focused on the regional competitiveness and employment objective, the territorial cooperation objective and the cohesion fund. it should be noted that these cuts are in complete contradiction with the conclusions of the june summit. as regards payments under this heading, the draft budget is considered as the minimum and is based on the assumption that all payment needs from previous years will have been covered in 2012. with the council's cuts, however, the risk of this non-payment being passed on to 2013 as it was from 2011 to 2012 is more than visible. such a development could jeopardise the proper implementation of these programmes in the last year of the current multiannual financial framework. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
on behalf of the efd group. madam president, i congratulate mr brok on the excellent work he has done on a very difficult report. i hope that developments after 2013 will not substantially disrupt the global status quo and will justify the forecasts of the report and the high representative. i am not able, of course, to cover all the chapters of the report because of the time limit, but i would like to focus on the eastern mediterranean and the southern neighbourhood, which are of vital importance to the european union. the tendency in the west to focus only on economic issues risks ignoring critical geopolitical aspects of the crisis. this is what the great analyst ian lesser points out, saying that greece may be going through a critical economic period but that we should not dwell only on numbers. the geostrategic position and importance of greece is enormous when greece's neighbourhood, from the balkans to the near east, is entering a very unstable period and with unforeseeable consequences for the immediate future. we must therefore be vigilant and we must prevent social tension in greece. the exploitation of natural gas in the eastern mediterranean region can also be a factor of security and stability for the union and contribute to the achievement of energy diversity. it is essential that the union move in this direction and you too, baroness ashton, must show strong political will and determination to issue mining licences within the eez of the member states, as defined by international law.
madam president, the debt crisis in many member states and the uncertainty about the future of the eurozone are undoubtedly affecting the union's image and calling into question its ability to play a meaningful role on the international scene. so i do not think this is the time for long empty declarations. the union must rediscover itself and set realistic targets based on its real potential. it is important that in this year's european parliament report on foreign and security policy there is a clearer call for the union to identify such strategic priorities. as parliament's rapporteur on enlargement, i would like to thank in particular mr brok, who has shown a constructive attitude on enlargement issues. i would also like to welcome the clear statement that the european parliament firmly supports the european perspective of any european state that respects the values of the union and is willing to fulfil the copenhagen criteria. enlargement is a major success of external action and must remain at the forefront and in the union's toolbox.
mr president, lady ashton, we are all very disappointed today. beyond the findings, we need decisions on syria. the country continues to drown in blood and the west watches helplessly. our inability and the security council's inability to pass a strong message allows assad to believe that he can still win. this is our failure. the neighbouring countries, particularly lebanon and jordan, but especially turkey, are under great pressure from the huge waves of refugees. according to un figures, in august alone, 100,000 syrians fled their country, most of them seeking refuge in neighboring turkey. turkey is objectively unable to cope with such a size alone. the search for safety and survival will bring these people to european host countries such as greece, italy and eventually to the rest of the european union. the consequences of this move will be huge. even at this last moment, we must join forces to find a way to end the violence immediately, but also to strengthen humanitarian efforts and to give a perspective and a hope to these people.

vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − mr president, i would like to thank all members of this house who have contributed in this very important debate. i think the views of members have been coherent and consistent in saying two or three things with which i agree. first of all we need to continue, and to step up, our work with civil society in russia and to be as supportive as possible of all those who seek a democratic future for their country. we also need to be clear of the importance of russia in terms of how we work with russia in our activities across the world. i have already pointed to examples of that, but i would again refer to the iran talks, where russia is an important and key partner with us in trying to address the nature of the nuclear programme of iran. i would not wish honourable members to be under any illusion that russia plays anything but an incredibly important role in that. so too does the partnership for modernisation, but we have to do more to push forward on this. it is important. it is an area of work, both in terms of economic life and in terms of political life, where we can do something. but the concerns that have been raised have been well said. the concerns about individuals and about what is happening in terms of the basis of this debate, which is the political use of justice, have been well noted. i thank you for the support for what we are doing and look forward to continuing to work with you on this.
the rapporteur. - the mr president, i should like to begin by thanking my fellow rapporteurs, mr stevenson, mr gallagher, mr haglund, mr torvalds, and all the other members of the committee on fisheries for their help in drafting this report. i would like to thank the shadow rapporteurs and my colleague, mrs zaharaki, who dealt with the subject. the fisheries sector is certainly at a turning point. the previous reform of the common fisheries policy failed to achieve the basic objectives pursued, as the european commission itself acknowledges. but even that is experience. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the common organisation of the market in fishery products, which is to be adopted by the council on 1 january, is a step in the right direction. the european union accounts for around 4.6% of world fisheries and aquaculture production, making it the fourth largest producer in the world. however, it imports 60% of the fish consumed in the european union. according to available data, 75% of the union's fish stocks are overexploited and more than 60% of fish stocks in european waters are fished beyond maximum sustainable yield. the european union loses €1.8 billion in potential revenue every year because it cannot manage its fisheries sustainably. at the same time, it lost 30% of its jobs in the period 2002-2007. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the fisheries sector is not left behind. these are the pillars on which the report we have presented to you is based. the main emphasis is on maximum sustainable yield. the pursuit of maximum sustainable yield is an ambitious objective that will contribute to the recovery of the population of fish stocks. the poor state of stocks requires us to be ambitious, especially at a time when we are given a historic opportunity to lay the foundations of a european fisheries policy. the report also highlights the difficulties in applying msy, especially in the case of mixed fisheries or where the scientific data to support it are incomplete. the commission and mrs damannakis should take note of this, and i thank them for their assistance, as i thank their office for its assistance and advice in the preparation of this report. the commission is called upon to increase funding to member states for safe and reliable data collection. the reliability and availability of their data should, i believe, be one of the most important priorities of the reform. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is a measure which concerns us because it is clearly unacceptable to throw food back into the sea which could be consumed. in our day and age and in the current economic climate, we all know how wasteful this practice is and how destructive it is to the environment and to the viability of the stocks. i would like to say that the commission has taken the necessary steps to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. i thank you again and i ask you to support my report.
mr president, i would like to draw the attention of the house to, and ask it to support, the salavrakos report and the rapporteur’s amendment, amendment 1, and paragraph 4 of the haglund report. this text is a cross-party compromise and calls on the commission to ‘assess the possibility of establishing a network of closed areas in which all fishing activities are prohibited for a certain period of time in order to increase fish productivity and conserve living aquatic resources and the marine ecosystem’. this is really necessary; as you know, the council has taken a very rigid position on the existing proposals. we have to put productive things on the table. this is a proposal which can help fishermen and fisheries. finally, with regard to the stevenson report, i would like to support colleagues that have asked for labelling indicating where and how the fish was caught and which systems were used.
member of the commission. − mr president, i shall be brief as i have almost exhausted my time limits. i would like to thank again all the rapporteurs and all the speakers because the discussion was very interesting and of substance. this is very helpful and gives us a very positive sign for the future. i think that this is a positive moment in the cooperation between the commission and parliament. this is the way i would like to see it and i hope that you are going to vote tomorrow for a good reform, a good radical and conclusive reform. this is what we really want. i do not want to comment on your opinions, but, because i think it is fair, just to comment on the spirit that i have witnessed in some interventions. this is not a power game of the commission against parliament or anybody else here. this is not a power game. we need your cooperation. i am here to cooperate with you in the reform. so, please, it is unfair to say, for example, referring to the gallagher report – i already congratulated mr gallagher on his excellent job and also the rapporteurs and the shadow rapporteurs – that this will be the end of the commission doing nothing and so on. after all, this is a report on our proposal. the commission services have worked a lot in some months to reach this proposal for this instrument, so it is unfair to say that the commission failed to answer this question and that parliament will now have to respond. everybody would like to cooperate here. at least, i would like to cooperate with all of you, and i very much welcome your ideas and your proposals. i understand all about the theoretical ideas, that the commission and european union are against fisheries and against fishermen, that we are trying to implement neo-liberal ideas, etc., but here are some concrete questions and i think that all members have to give concrete answers. do we want to have the date of the catch? do we want to have it on the fish products? yes or no? i would like clear answers on this because otherwise, when i then go to discuss this with small-scale fisheries and small-scale fishermen everywhere, i will have to say that the commission tried to protect their interests but the european parliament declined to do so. so, after all, this house also has some responsibility.
president-in-office of the council. − mr president, honourable members, i would like to thank all the speakers for their very fruitful interventions, to which i have listened very attentively. i have taken careful note of the key points that were raised. let me once again assure you that the reform of the common fisheries policy is a top priority for the council as well as the european parliament and the commission. the reports by the european parliament that we have in front of us show that the council and parliament can go a long way together on major issues. it is hoped that this will also be the case for the european maritime and fisheries fund. the presidency therefore looks forward to continuing constructive and fruitful dialogue between the council and the european parliament with a view to finding agreement on the best way forward. i should like to make a final point on labelling and, in particular, on the catch date being made mandatory in fish product information. i hope you will bear with me for not being an expert on this – and, of course, i do not like to contradict my dear commissioner damanaki – but i must explain the rationale behind the council’s position. the council holds that the catch date is difficult to establish, and trace, on fishing trips lasting several days or weeks. on such trips, the fish is often immediately frozen on board and, therefore, kept in perfect condition. mandatory information exists already concerning package date and best- before date, and this information should be expanded to include qualitative information on whether the product was defrosted before selling. the catch or landing date could be part of the voluntary information whereby the producer or merchant seeks to distinguish one product from another.
the rapporteur. - the mr president, the rapporteurs of the reports on this subject who have preceded me on the floor have covered most of my points. i would like to emphasize that in drafting this report i have had in mind certain principles: the moderate adaptation of the measure of legislation, the feasibility and controllability of the measure, the acceptability of the measure and the permanence of the measure, because too much change is harmful. i thanked all the colleagues who helped me to draw up the report. i have not thanked the fishermen, who will accept the results of this arrangement. i would therefore like to remind the fishermen of europe that jesus christ chose them as his disciples and, like the 'fishermen of the needle', gave them the power to fish for souls. i want to assure them that we are looking to the future of fishing, that we are aware of the history of fishing and that we believe that fishing will continue to exist well beyond 2020.
mr president of the european commission, thank you for your inspired speech. i would urge you to address it to the council of heads of state or government at the next summit because i think they need to be convinced. the european parliament will be with you as you move in the direction you have described. i would like to focus on one point, mr president. you were right to say that europe needs change and reform and that the european social model should not be abandoned but reformed. but, mr president, what do we mean when we talk about reforms? do we give priority to reforms that serve your vision, that is to say, those that promote social justice, growth, the functioning of the single market, such as tax reform, the fight against tax evasion, the liberalisation of markets, or do we give priority to reforms that undermine your vision, such as the complete deregulation of the labour market, the abolition of collective bargaining and the role of the social partners? we must decide, mr president, what kind of reforms we want to serve.
i am one of those who understand the efforts made by the commission to normalize developments in the european union, and i congratulate you on that. we must not only criticize, we must also praise. let me elaborate on my reasoning: i read a statistic that the european union today represents 4% of the world's population and 30% of the world's gdp. does this mean we got rich by spending? because there is this method - get rich by waste, by consumption. but with this fiscal discipline that we are imposing now, we are reducing gdp, we are getting poorer and unemployment is increasing, worsened by technological progress. the fact that the swiss franc is at parity should also be a cause for concern, because it points to a great deal of tax evasion. is there an immediate plan for unemployment and tax evasion?

in writing. the committee on legal affairs and citizens' rights has called for the commission to be given the power to adopt legislative proposals to ensure that the european parliament is given the opportunity to make its views known. it is part of the eu's strategy for a 'single area of freedom, security and justice' which aims to shield the exploitative system more effectively by enlarging repressive mechanisms and restricting popular freedoms and democratic rights. the criminal treatment and the conditions for the administration of criminal justice are a sovereign competence of each member state, which is gradually and in a targeted manner being transferred to the eu institutions through a series of eu legislation. to this end, issues that raise workers' awareness, such as the protection of children, victims of crime, etc., are selected to instill in the public consciousness the alleged need for a common eu criminal law. the provisions of the directive which provide for the possibility of victims giving evidence without being physically present in court are also dangerous. such provisions are promoted in the 'anti-terrorism' legislation of the eu and its member states by denying fundamental rights of the defence of the accused, such as the possibility for the defence to verify the credibility of the testimony by means of a live trial.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. until now, the eu lacked a comprehensive european legislative framework that provides all victims of crime, regardless of their legal status, with recognition and the widest possible protection within the union. this initiative contributes to the extension of such rights as the right to information, the right to interpretation and translation, the right to be heard, the right to compensation, the right to avoid contact between the victim and the perpetrator. however, it is noted that the definition of 'victim' is missing from community legislation, which should be clarified in future legislative acts. in addition, despite the comprehensive list of rights provided for in the commission's communication on victims of crime, items such as psychological support for victims, additional protection for victims with special needs and ensuring the protection of the privacy of victims, which are included in the european parliament's report, are missing.
in writing. i voted against the report by mr gallagher as it proposes measures to make the eu more effective in implementing agreed fisheries management measures in a way that creates disincentives for unsustainable fishing.
in writing. the european parliament's group of the european people's party voted against the report and the proposal for a regulation because it is part of the revision of the anti-people common fisheries policy. the aim of the reform is to further capitalist restructuring of the fisheries sector by destroying small-scale fishermen - which has already largely been done with the current cfp - and strengthening the large fishing fleets and aquaculture monopolies. the proposal to restructure the common market in fishery and aquaculture products is designed to further strengthen the monopoly groups in the sector. the 'producer organisations' - for which various eu funding instruments are envisaged - will increase the size of the monopoly groups and will mean the final destruction of the poor and middle-class fishermen. it introduces transferable fishing licences to be managed by producer organisations, which will increase the size of monopoly groups and will mean the final destruction of poor and middle-class fishermen, since monopolies in the area will accumulate a large number of licences. the proposed ban on discarding unwanted catches will favour monopolistic concentration in the sector, as only the monopolies have the appropriate logistical processing infrastructure, while poor middle-class fishermen who cannot cope are driven to extinction.
in writing. the kke voted against the report because it advocates an even more aggressive common foreign and defence policy and calls for the multiplication of eu military and civilian operations. the new position of 'eu commissioner for human rights' is intended to provide cover for eu interventions such as the european endowment for democracy which will fund secretly 'dissident' parties. the report calls for an even more aggressive eu role in the western balkans, the middle east, central asia, the horn of africa and sub-saharan africa. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'common carrier' in the context of the directive on the carriage of goods by road. it gives full support to the imperialist intervention in syria, calling for additional measures in the name of 'the right to protect civilians from the regime's massacres', as it had done in the libyan war. this new pretext for imperialist intervention is being attempted to be reduced to a new un principle. the report calls for full alignment of the eu with nato in afghanistan and elsewhere, with the decisions of the 2012 chicago summit, and fully covers nato's plans for so-called missile shields and nuclear weapons in europe.
in writing. i voted against the brok report because i believe that it encourages the european parliament and the other institutions of the union to pursue the militarization of its foreign policy, through the cfsp, at the expense of a pro-peace policy based on cooperation between all the member states of the union and non-member states, away from dangerous national rivalries and political-military interventions. as a member of the euro group of the left, i express my firm opinion - contrary to this report - on the complete separation of the union from the nato war machine, on the dissolution of the north atlantic treaty, which has no democratic and moral legitimacy, on disarmament in europe and the need for immediate drastic cuts in armaments programmes and military expenditure, on respect for international law and the charter of the united nations. the continuing tendency of the dominant powers of the european union to identify foreign policy with military power and the nato military machine with the union's perspective is not only contrary to its declared values but also hinders any effort to build a europe and a world of peace, cooperation and solidarity between states and peoples.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. the aim is not to revise the council's 2010 cfsp report but to provide an approach on the future of the eu's strategy in the external environment. the lisbon treaty also provides that the union's competence in the field of the common foreign and security policy covers all areas of foreign policy, thus paving the way for a more coherent and consistent strategy. the new initiatives should, of course, strike the right balance and be based on realistic bases, taking into account the different economic, political and geo-strategic interests of each member state. the report, which i voted in favour of, takes these two points very well and, rather than simply calling for a new european security strategy, it contributes to the outline of the eu's external policy and to the establishment of benchmarks for monitoring and assessing progress in the years ahead.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr brok's report as it gives a good sense of the eu's priorities and strategic directions. i believe that it contains important steps towards the formulation of the union's foreign policy priorities in a long-term and strategic framework.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr haglund's report because i believe that the proposals it contains enhance the sustainability and recovery of fish stocks and reinforce the role and importance of multiannual recovery plans for fish stocks.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, personally, i find today's discussion unacceptable, not because i am not in solidarity with the romanian people, but on the contrary, because i believe that certain things that have been heard in this hall and certain things that have happened in the past are an insult to the romanian people and their democratic traditions. i believe that the romanian people are capable of finding a solution to the problem they are facing, all the more so, mr president, since i believe that the root of the problem lies in the very difficult, very bad social situation facing the people of romania. now i'd like to continue in greek, because it'll be easier for me to express myself. on behalf of the gue/ngl group mr. president, i think that this debate is the result of the unacceptable situation experienced by the romanian people. as you know, a harsh austerity programme is being implemented at its expense, a programme that is being generalized throughout the european union. the more cruel and unjust these measures are for the majority of workers, the more we will have such situations. in conclusion, i would like to say that those who warned the peoples of the former socialist countries that they would face very difficult and unacceptable situations, such as those in romania, when they joined the european union, were unfortunately right, because in addition to poverty and unemployment, they also suffered from the anti-democratic measures taken against them.
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, this is the second time this year that we have had to address a very serious situation concerning the rule of law in one of the member states of the european union. after hungary, romania. and the debate today, like those we have had in the past on a similar question, was very much led by party political concerns, similar attitudes in similar debates. the commission is the guardian of the treaties and this is why we always need to act with fairness and impartiality. when there is an attack on the rule of law or a breach of eu legislation, irrespective of the political colour of the government, the commission will remain firm in expressing concerns and taking actions, and this does not apply only now but has also applied in the past. i still remember a time when i took action on a very similar matter, which was in 2009 when i was telecoms commissioner. i started infringement proceedings against romania to protect the independence of the national telecoms regulator. at that time a right-wing government coalition had introduced emergency legislation to deprive a court ruling of its effect and to remove the president of the national telecoms regulator from office. it was the same rationale that the commission followed earlier this year when we brought hungary to the court of justice for violations of the independence of the hungarian data protection legislation and other infringement procedures. of course such legal issues may seem small in the overall context. so what about the big picture? what about the rule of law in general and what about the independence of the judiciary? i agree with those who asked these questions. yes, the commission has to play its role as guardian of the treaties and it has to go after breaches in eu law by means of infringement proceedings. it is a general principle, and small violations count too. what about the big violations? here i would to draw two lessons from the recent experiences. first, of course, we always need to tackle these matters with independence and objectivity. respect for the rule of law has nothing to do with the political party in power. for president barroso and for myself, this has been very important throughout these processes. lady justice is blind. she does not recognise party political colours. she only reacts to the rule of law which is in danger. secondly, we can see very well by these very concrete examples that we have experienced this year that we lack effective mechanisms in the eu to enforce respect for the rule of law more generally and more systematically. today everybody mentions the situation in hungary and romania. are we sure that we will not see such a situation again in a couple of weeks in another eu country? now let us be honest – and some of the parliamentarians have said it very clearly – we face a copenhagen dilemma. we are very strict on the copenhagen criteria, notably on the rule of law in the accession process of a new member state but, once this member state has joined the european union, we appear not to have any instrument to see whether the rule of law and the independence of the judiciary still command respect. we as a european union need to stand firm on our values and on the rule of law, and that is why i think that we need to put in place an objective mechanism to assess the judicial systems in all of our 27 member states, because our infringement procedures are too technical and too slow to react to high-risk situations concerning the rule of law, and because the article 7 procedure is a nuclear option that should only be used by the commission, parliament and the council when there is really no other solution. but what do we have in between? i would therefore like to work with the european parliament and with the justice ministers of all member states on a new intermediate and more focused mechanism. i propose to add to the economic and social benchmark in the european semester a new mechanism for measuring, comparing and benchmarking the strength, efficiency and reliability of the justice systems in all member states. (applause) so my experts are at this moment developing for the european semester a justice scoreboard that will allow a detailed assessment of the justice systems of all member states, their strengths and their weaknesses and i am going to start a discussion with parliament and with the justice ministers in the coming weeks. we europeans have built a community based on the rule of law – rechtsgemeinschaft as the germans say – and we therefore cannot allow the rule of law to become an object of any party political game. we need a strong, reliable rule of law in all 27 member states of the union and i count on this parliament to strongly support the european commission in this endeavour. (applause)

the next item is the debate on the report by mr david martin, on behalf of the committee on international trade, on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council amending annex i to council regulation (ec) no 1528/2007 as regards the removal of certain countries from the list of regions or states which have concluded negotiations (com (2011) 0598 - c7-0305/2011 - 2011/0260 (cod)) (a7-0207/2012).
member of the commission. − mr president, i could give you one minute of my speaking time, for example, provided of course that you are not going to interrupt me afterwards! i understand a number of the remarks that have been made but i would urge you to stay with the facts as they are. what this is really about is that a number of countries have not yet signed and/or ratified an agreement they concluded in 2007. that is the reality. so this is about the past. the idea that we want to force them to conclude and ratify a definite and final economic partnership agreement is not correct. i have mentioned the eight countries – a limited number of countries – that could be affected in one way or another by what we are proposing. i will read them out again: cameroon, côte d’ivoire, fiji, ghana, kenya and swaziland – which are lower middle-income countries – and botswana and namibia, which are higher middle-income countries. it does not, in any way you could imagine, affect a developing country. in the developing countries you have two separate categories: the least developed countries (ldcs) who benefit from everything but arms – and that continues – and the other ones that benefit from the generalised system of preferences (gsp) or the gsp+. so it is not about those countries. what it is really about is countries – albeit a limited number of countries, but in a much bigger category if you look at it worldwide – where, if we do not put an end to the market access regulation in the form it exists, which is always considered to be temporary, unilateral access to the european markets would be created, with those countries exporting duty-free and quota-free products other than arms. that is what you would create. so in fact you would create inequality between the eight that i have mentioned and – i do not know how many but i would estimate – 50 or 60 worldwide that are in a different position. secondly, are we forcing anybody to do this? no. we are not. if they do not want to ratify the agreement that they themselves concluded in 2007, then they do not do have to do so. even then, our door is open to negotiating a new agreement with them. but i cannot see how we would be unfair or bullying or blackmailing (and a lot of similar words have been used here) in asking somebody who concluded an agreement in 2007 – that means five years ago, with a further three or four years of negotiations, and especially given that they should have adapted their system already – to now ratify what they signed, initialled and promised to ratify. do it! they have already had five to seven years, depending on the starting point, and we are giving them an additional year-and-a-half just to ratify, not to negotiate a new agreement but to ratify. is this unfair? is this blackmailing? is this jeopardising our position with respect to africa? really, i do not believe so. bernd lange spoke of a confrontation between a strong commission and weak states. that is what i heard you say. first of all, thank you for calling us strong: that is not bad. some of these states are weak but i did not mention the weakest, the ones that are in debt, the ldcs and the developing countries. we are talking here about lower and middle income countries. that is what we are talking about. the question is not whether they are weak or strong. the only thing we ask of them is to ratify what they negotiated and concluded in the past. we are not bullying them about anything other than living up to the commitments that they made quite some time ago. finally, a remark to mr keller, who said the policy would be contrary to my statement, which is also in the communication, that trade can promote development. that is not true because you can currently see, with the existing schemes, that the trade volume is going down drastically. why so? because trade is about more than just exporting something to another market. it is a means engaging with that market and having trade movements in both directions. it is about aid for trade and about establishing a vibrant marketplace that can export products to the european union. i would warmly welcome their doing that to a much greater extent than they have done in the past. personally i am very sad about the fact that the trade volume with africa is going down. it should be going up. i am ready to be very flexible in the economic partnership agreements (epas) that we still have to negotiate with a number of regional entities. i have no problem at all with asymmetry. i hope the countries concerned can export much more to the european union. we are ready to help them with aid for trade. we are ready to help them with trade facilitation – my colleague, andris piebalgs is in complete agreement with me about that – but they should live up to their commitments so that we can proceed and establish a sure legal system, legal obligations and legal rights in a negotiated framework. that is what we are asking for and that is also why i stand by the proposal that we have made. i really hope that tomorrow when you vote you take this into account. this proposal is not against developing countries and it is not against africa. on the contrary, it is for the good of having mature trade relations with countries that should develop and should take off. most of the countries that have not yet taken off are in sub-saharan africa, and that is no accident. we should do something about this. it is our moral duty to do so.
member of the commission. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the commission has already made a number of proposals. this is not the first time that we have sought to strengthen our common position on external energy relations. i can only agree with mr kariņš, who has described the whole process. i can only thank him for his commitment, his diplomatic skills and, as a result, his successful moderation of parliament. what's the matter? the first is that we have government agreements between member states and third countries which we know are not based 100% on european law, and are therefore incompatible with european law. we do not believe that these agreements were concluded by the member states because they wanted to annoy the european union, but that they were brought into these agreements by the supplying countries under pressure. so disclosure is the right way to see that contracts between member states and third countries are based on european law, on internal market law, and where this is not the case, that they need to be amended. it's about competitive advantages and disadvantages. if one supplier and his state know all the contracts, but the member states only know their own, then we are not on the same page. transparency rules can help to remedy this. then it's about investors. they are generally interested in private investment in networks, such as gas pipelines. these infrastructure projects require a certain degree of legal clarity. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. more transparency on the agreement's compatibility with european law will help to avoid unpleasant surprises, to avoid infringement proceedings, to create legal certainty and thus to encourage investment. the compromise reached now falls into law just like that. i would like to thank you again for your excellent cooperation in parliament. but we will also look at how it works in practice. the strict monitoring and compliance with our current new laws in the coming years is to be controlled. the commission will be monitoring whether we have enough instruments. if not, we shall be proposing in a few years how to develop the subject of energy policy. it is a first step, a right one, and an important one, but there are certainly more steps to be taken. thank you for your cooperation.
the committee on foreign affairs and security adopted a number of amendments to the proposal. - the mr president, the new information exchange mechanism will in fact increase the consistency and coherence of the union's external energy relations, while also strengthening the union's strategic position in its relations with third countries. i am speaking today as the spokesman and rapporteur for the committee on foreign affairs. it should not be forgotten that the european union remains heavily dependent on energy imports, making the functioning and energy supply of the energy market in many cases uncertain. this new mechanism can indeed substantially strengthen the bargaining power of the member states through the single european market and the commission's expertise in community law. at the same time, the legal certainty that will result for future investment decisions through a stable legal framework will make it easier for the union to finance relevant projects and may in several cases speed up the time to implementation. clearly the better coordination that can be achieved through this mechanism should not be interpreted as less flexibility in the member states. the whole project will be ancillary in nature and should not replace the member states as the competent entities for concluding energy agreements with third countries. the commission's involvement can, however, have a multiplier effect by effectively assisting the member states, particularly at the negotiating stage of such agreements.
on behalf of the efd group. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment is a very important one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. for example, we are currently dealing with the energy supply of lithuania, in which the commission has already opened an investigation against gazprom for antitrust practices. this report can solve such problems. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we hope that the new mechanism will not disrupt the existing good energy situations and relations of the member states with third countries.
mr president, mr kariņš, congratulations on your report, it was very rational. i have a question, commissioner, on the procedure for examining transnational agreements: the commission's external affairs service will play a decisive role? i think that energy should be outside the rules of foreign policy, because energy is characterized by a long duration and predictability which foreign relations do not have.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to improve the quality of the products and the quality of the services provided by the european union. i think that the proposals for mountain products should also apply to products from island farming, so that they too can be included in the system of optional quality indications. we can all understand the peculiarities of islands, their serious disadvantages, their permanence, their physical and demographic problems, their distance from the centre, their small size, the difficult topography of the land, their climatic peculiarities, their dependence on activities with limited possibilities. many of these elements give specific and particularly high quality characteristics to agricultural products and the labelling of these products as island products would allow an increase in their added value. i would therefore ask the commissioner to indicate what action is envisaged to promote this prospect and, of course, if possible, within what timeframe.
madam president, behind the fine words of the directive on cultural heritage, there are, in my view, the interests of the monopoly groups operating in the field of digital technologies and the internet. the purpose of the directive is not, as agreed with the council, the preservation, conservation, promotion, dissemination and access to cultural creation and heritage, but a highly profitable activity, namely the large-scale digitisation of archives and collections and the creation of european digital libraries. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval, and the council will be consulted on the matter. the directive, with its cleverly worded provisions, opens the way to the commercialisation of orphan works and their use for the benefit of private capital. it thus allows, first, the contracting out of the search for the author to private undertakings, for a fee, and, second, the contracting out of the digitisation to private individuals, in public-private partnerships, while at the same time charging users a fee, using the digitisation of those works as a pretext. in this way, the entire digitisation and distribution work will be passed on to the business groups while the workers will be required to pay for the use of the projects. we are seeing once again that all the decisions of the european union lead to commercialisation. it is a policy that is bombing the foundations of civilization and it is a policy that must be changed.
in writing. the european parliament often seeks transparency and access to all community documents and information in its reports, as a basis and prerequisite for democracy and to facilitate the work of the ep. the extension of the ec's powers by the treaty of lisbon also covers the field of international agreements. in this context, parliament pursued and achieved a number of key objectives in the negotiations, including access to classified information for members and ep staff on ep premises, in accordance with ep security rules. the draft agreement, which i voted in favour of, concerns access to classified information on matters beyond the closed area of the common foreign and security policy, such as the so-called mixed agreements.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on european social entrepreneurship funds, which makes important additions by improving the commission's original text. the ep emphasises the supervisory level in order to ensure that the resources of the egtcs are used properly and to eliminate the risk that the egtcs are used for other purposes unrelated to the promotion of social entrepreneurship. it also extends the number of companies that could join the fund to include those whose objective is to achieve social impact rather than generate profit for owners and shareholders. since such patterns are also observed in greece, this provision will benefit our country in this difficult period for entrepreneurship and the labour market.
in writing. i voted in favour of the auconie report because i believe that the measures it proposes help to tackle the social consequences of the financial crisis. the common framework of rules on the use of the name 'european social entrepreneurship fund' contributes to the good functioning of the internal market.
in writing. the rich cultural heritage of the eu should be given equal protection and promoted. the fact that a very large part of this heritage is unused in archives and libraries and in some cases completely unavailable to the citizens of the european union makes it necessary to create a regulatory framework. the report, which i voted in favour of, proposes that orphan works should be made available digitally so that all citizens can benefit without having to move them. europeana has already made a significant contribution to this; however, it does not yet include orphan works. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to cover the protection of copyright and related rights in the field of intellectual property. the proposal for a european parliament and council directive on the permissible uses of orphan works will pave the way for their exploitation in books, newspapers, magazines and films, while respecting copyright in all cases, even if the owner cannot be found.
in writing. we live in a digital age. improving the system for managing copyright is essential to support innovation and creativity within the single market.
in writing. the european parliament's committee on foreign affairs and security has called for the eu to take action to prevent the spread of the virus and to ensure that the virus is contained. as with libya, the european parliament is becoming a beacon and paving the way for a new criminal imperialist intervention by military means against the syrian people. it boldly and provocatively demands the creation of so-called "humanitarian aid" zones and corridors, which will serve as the no-fly zone in libya - the necessary pretext for military operations by the eu-us-nato imperialists that will bloodshed the syrian people. the joint resolution is already preparing the open intervention of the imperialists by invoking the 'right to protect civilians' to give the necessary legitimacy to military operations. it seeks to coordinate the various military gangs into a single opposition to give the eu-us-nato criminal plans for the control and exploitation of the resources and peoples of the region a non-existent popular support. it is imperative that the peoples step up their anti-imperialist action. to fight against the new crime of the imperialists. to block the way to intervention, imperialist war and bloodshed of the peoples of the middle east and the wider region
in writing. i voted in favour of the joint motion for a resolution because i believe that the rapid delivery of humanitarian aid to syria is necessary because international humanitarian law must be fully respected by all those involved in the crisis.
in writing. i voted in favour of the joint motion for a resolution as the human rights situation in russia has deteriorated dramatically in recent months and the necessary measures must be taken to protect civil society, freedom of expression and assembly.
president-in-office of the council. − mr president, as president of the council, it is a pleasure for me to attend parliament’s debate on the draft budget of the european union for the financial year 2013. on 11 september i also had the honour of presenting to you in detail the council’s position on the draft budget for 2013, as adopted by the council on 24 july. last week, at the budgetary trialogue, i had the opportunity of giving some initial reactions to the amendments which the european parliament intends to vote on. i wish to recall that the council reached an agreement on its position on the draft budget for 2013, following difficult negotiations, and accepted a limited increase in commitments and payment appropriations compared to 2012. we are all aware of the considerable effort and budgetary constraints that member states are currently facing at national level, and we have to take these into account. i believe that, as the member states endeavour to consolidate their budgets, it is crucial to deliver the right message to european citizens. today i will take note of the positions taken by the european parliament. let me now focus on some issues to which the council attaches particular importance. firstly, the council considers that the existing ceilings of the multiannual financial framework must be respected. it is also important to ensure adequate margins under the different ceilings in order to be able to cope with unforeseen situations. the council is therefore concerned about the european parliament’s amendments, which considerably reduce the margins in all headings, the only exception being heading 5. moreover, the european parliament proposes to go beyond the ceilings of subheading 1a, requiring the mobilisation of the flexibility instrument. secondly, the council regrets the increasing payment appropriations that the european parliament intends to vote on, especially when their level exceeds the commission’s draft budget. the council is aware of the european parliament’s intentions to support and highlight several of its political priorities. however, the overall level of payment appropriations retained should be sufficient but not overestimated. thirdly, the council wishes to find an agreement with the european parliament on a reasonable level of payment appropriations for the current budgetary year, including draft amending budget no 5, the global transfer and the upcoming draft amending budget no 6. finally, i would like to remind you that the council is opposed to the broad use of reserves that you intend to vote on, especially when their release is accompanied by conditions which are not in line with the provisions of the financial regulation. appropriations should only be entered in the reserve in the absence of a basic act and in cases of serious doubt about the adequacy of the appropriations or their implementation. to conclude, let me reiterate the council’s hope and conviction that the existing excellent climate of cooperation will prevail during the rest of the budgetary procedure, leading to a mutually-satisfying agreement on the 2013 budget. we fully respect the european parliament and your intentions, even when we are not in a position to entirely share the stance you adopt.
member of the commission. − mr president, honourable members, minister, we are discussing the budget for 2013, the last year of this financial perspective. however, we should draw on the experience of the tensions we have in this year’s budget; they should be kept very strongly in mind. therefore i welcome –not surprisingly– the reading of the proposal by the committee on budgets about the restoration of our draft proposal for next year. we share the same priorities. it is very clear to me that budgetary efforts nowadays should undertaken be in close conjunction with the growth, jobs and competitiveness agenda of the european union. this should not be just an illusion of a growth strategy; it should be very well equipped with money. we notice that parliament is very restrained as to the level of commitments. that is why it is leaving some unallocated margins for unforeseen events, and it is easy to foresee that this will also be needed for next year. last friday we adopted the amending budget for agriculture for next year that more or less confirms our early estimates, but as mr la via clearly stated, the main sensitive issue is the level of payments. we cannot endorse the council’s decision to cut our proposal by more than eur 5 billion, because this is simply contradicting earlier announcements on the growth and jobs agenda for europe: they were made in june, and in july come cuts of that amount. this ignores the reality of what is really needed this year, and next, and we cannot simply continue with the systematic underfunding of the european budget, as it is hindering the implementation of so many programmes. this year we should also base our programming of the implementation of the budget – which is now more than eur 10 billion bigger than last year (an indication of the maturity of many programmes) – on the need to cover this year all that should be paid this year in order to enter next year with a clear vision of what is really needed in 2013 and not carry forwards all the accumulated problems. in some areas we are really running short of money. for example, for the social fund we have to stop payments in the area of competitiveness, as there are no more resources available. there is a very high level of implementation generally in cohesion funding. as for the research grants, they already needed to be reinforced back in july. so the tension this year indicates the need for an amending and correcting budget for this year in order to cover what is really needed in the remaining period of this year before we can discuss and agree on the 2013 level. i think the position on administration that mr vaughan takes is the right one. we should be constrained, but of course there are limits to savings, given the new responsibilities of the european union. so this should be below the level of inflation. our letter of executability is coming soon in order to complete the full picture. that is also about pilot projects and preparatory actions. of course we are not happy with the reserves, but i understand that we could end the conciliation after the written explanations without reserves. our role is to submit everything that is needed as the basis for sound programming for 2013 so that parliament has a full picture of what is really reasonable in the 2013 budget.
the committee on regional development has adopted an opinion on the proposal for a council decision on the conclusion of the agreement between the european economic community and the republic of south africa on trade in textile products. - the mr president, mr commissioner, mr president, ladies and gentlemen, for another year the committee on budgets of the european parliament has taken a responsible position by rejecting the horizontal cuts introduced by the council. these cuts are not in line with the real needs of the european union and are in complete contradiction with the pact for growth and employment agreed by the 27 heads of state or government last june. let me concentrate on title 1b. we all agree that cohesion policy is the main and most appropriate investment instrument which can lead the european union to growth and job creation by addressing precisely the investment needs of the regions. it also makes a decisive contribution to the effective reduction of regional disparities and the achievement of social, economic and territorial cohesion. we as the european parliament must ensure, and we do, that the various policies of the european union are adequately financed, particularly those which lead to growth, jobs and investment.
the committee on petitions' opinion was drawn up by mr glinne, rapporteur. - the mr president, i would also like to thank the rapporteurs, mr la via and mr vaughan, for their report on the draft general budget for the financial year 2013. as the committee on petitions, our opinion is basically about the european ombudsman's expenditure and budget forecasts for the current financial year. i would like to point out that the increase in the budget of the european ombudsman this year is 3.89% and that this increase is for technical reasons, such as the increase in the rent in strasbourg and the forced move to new offices in brussels because the european parliament decided to convert the current offices into a museum. the expenditure is therefore inflexible and should not be considered as a substantial increase in the cost of the european ombudsman's procedures for this relocation, which has a crisis-time cost for parliament. finally, i would like to point out that the increase in appropriations for contractors by some eur 110 000 is, according to our information, perfectly normal and subject to the general staff regulations.
