the next item is the report by mrs antigone papadopoulos, on behalf of the committee on civil liberties, on the proposal for a european parliament and council decision on the european year of citizens (2013) (com (2011) 0489 - c7-0217/2011 - 2011/0217 (cod)) (a7-0271/2012).
the committee on petitions' opinion was drawn up by mr glinne, rapporteur. - the mr president, i congratulate mrs antigone papadopoulos on her excellent work and the very important report she presented on the european citizen. i hope that the initiative will be substantial and not just a mere formality. the committee on petitions, which i represent here, is putting forward proposals and in particular stressing the right of every citizen of the union and every natural or legal person who contributes to the deepening of democratic institutions to refer matters to the european parliament. we place particular emphasis on the right of citizens to free movement, including as a means of achieving the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy, and as such we consider the mobility of young people, especially students, to be of vital importance to the european union. the commission should also be able to provide information to citizens on their rights, in particular through the european e-justice portal. i would like to say, mr president, that we are all very happy to support the commission's proposals, but we are not prepared to accept them. the economic crisis we are experiencing has disrupted people's relationships. i hope that the european union will be given the opportunity to re-establish these relations. the crisis will pass.
i am speaking on behalf of the ppe group. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european union's financial interests are protected. twenty years after the concept of european citizenship was adopted by the european union, we celebrate the european year of citizens in 2013. this is linked not only to the free movement of goods, services, persons and capital, and especially of persons, not only to cross-border relations and cooperation, but also to the rights of citizens as expressed in the lisbon treaty, e.g. the citizens' initiative and consultation which are of enormous importance. if there is one drawback to the union today, it is precisely that many of its citizens know something general and vague about the union, but do not know who is doing what, with what responsibility and at what level. and because governments tend to assume or propagate that everything good comes from national governments and, conversely, everything bad comes from brussels, the european year of citizens can therefore offer an opportunity to understand things better, to raise awareness among european citizens so that they realise that it is possible to have both access to information and participation. in fact, mr president, with the new report being prepared, european political parties will have access to and direct participation in european political parties, which will give them a great deal of flexibility, help national political parties and in general make citizens better aware of what the european union is and where it is going.
mr president, it is very important indeed to speak in the context of the european year of citizens about the problems of citizens, and 2013 is very crucial because it is a year before the european elections, so it will be able to determine the image that citizens will have of europe and of the european parliament and what they will vote for, while at the same time they will vote for europe, and not just for a party. on the other hand, it is a year which comes after several years of crisis and we must convince that europe has solutions to the problems of citizens, it does not have solutions only for numbers. it is very important that there should be more europe, european solutions to the crisis, but not linked to austerity, not just to the deprivation of citizens' rights. the commission's proposal is to be adopted by the council and the council, and the council will be consulted on the matter. we want more europe, we want european citizenship and we want citizens to be involved in politics and to be heard.
mr president, in greece in crisis the far right is becoming more and more nightmarish. golden dawn operates as a paramilitary force with assault battalions abusing civilians, with mps threatening their colleagues and speaking vulgarly in parliament. i am afraid that at this level too greece will be only the beginning. the responsibility of the political system as a whole is great because it has faced a neo-fascist party, whose leader greets with a nazi salute, as any party. but the government in particular has the primary responsibility. when the minister of public order does not punish the police officers who allow a golden dawn mp to release arrested people in incidents, when the minister of the interior answers a parliamentary question from that party about the number of foreign children in kindergartens, while it is clear that citizenship is not a criterion for enrollment in them, then democracy is in danger. the greek government must prove that there is a coordinated state and that greek society is not unprotected and this bet is as important as economic recovery. the european union must respond in every way to the rise of fascism in europe.
mr president, the harsh social reality that greece is experiencing today is unprecedented in its post-war history. soaring unemployment, especially among young people, growing child malnutrition, unprecedented homelessness and suicide make up a very dark picture. violent attitudes, racist views and extremist forces with neo-nazi characteristics have never had the slightest resonance in greece. today they do! social cohesion is dangerously fractured. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. can europe emerge from the crisis without losing its soul? the next few days and weeks are extremely critical. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval, and the council will be able to accept it.
mr president, both the opportunities and the challenges facing island regions have to do with the specificities of islands: the islands face serious difficulties in transport and fuel prices, which have been rising since the economic crisis, but at the same time they are a fertile ground for research and development in renewable energy sources. however, the existing european measures for islands do not result from a single plan or are based on a comprehensive assessment of the situation of island regions. although three years have already passed since the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, which in article 174 emphasises island status, we have not yet developed a truly integrated approach to islands. the commission has already taken a number of measures to improve the energy performance of the member states. for example, parliament has in a written statement since the beginning of the year called for support for sustainability in the islands to meet or exceed the eu 2020 targets. the commission has not yet responded to this request.
mr president, when more than 100 000 passengers were affected by the eruption of the icelandic volcano in april 2010, member states were quick to declare that the single european sky could not be delayed any longer. europe needed, and did not have, the flexibility of a common airspace. two and a half years later, we are in a political impasse in which the immediate completion of the single european sky seems rather utopian, and the formal establishment of the functional airspace blocks (fabs) is even delayed, which, moreover, does not guarantee an improvement in aviation performance. what is needed at this moment is the political will to overcome bureaucracy and the interests of the immovable, to subdue national egos. and that is why, especially today, we must not overlook the weight that symbolism also has. the single european sky is a technical project with known benefits - which have already been mentioned - but at the same time it is a symbol of european integration and we should remember this.

vice-president of the commission. − mr president, firstly we would like to thank you for the opportunity to respond to the european parliament’s interim report on the next financial framework. we all agree that this proposal is probably the most important item on the agenda of the european parliament, the council and the commission. through the decision on the mff we are clearly sending a message to the outside world on whether we are able to deliver on europe 2020, have enough resources to fund our common policies and can play our important role on the international stage. we are also all aware that, for the first time, the multiannual financial framework is being negotiated under the new treaties. both janusz lewandowski and myself have been strong supporters of the clear will of parliament to exercise its right fully. i would like to thank the cyprus and danish presidencies for having established a very good framework and excellent cooperation with parliament’s negotiating team and with the commission. i would say, in particular, that the informal exchange of views before the last general affairs council was very important in this respect. as you know, the general affairs council is the place where the discussions on the mff are taking place. it is where janusz and i have been defending the commission’s proposals and where there is also active involvement of the european parliament in the whole process. we have studied your interim report in great detail and we very much support your starting point that this is an investment budget. we have to repeat this message again and again, because this is the message we have sometimes lost in the negotiations and in the europe-wide discussions. we have a very strong argument on our side, because even the european council recognised the catalysing role of the eu budget when it adopted the compact for growth and jobs in june. if you allow me, i shall make a couple of comments on the more horizontal issues before i hand over to my colleague janusz lewandowski, who handles all budget questions for the commission. first, on the question of the volume of the budget, we are convinced that our proposal of 1.08 % of gni represents a credible budget and is a balanced figure which would enable us to stabilise the financing of the cap and the cohesion policy and allow for a noticeable increase in the eu 2020 strategy priorities. we very much appreciate the focus parliament has put on the improved quality of the spending of the eu budget, because we would like to achieve a qualitative leap in terms of eu funding and european added value. we would like to increase the efficiency, effectiveness and simplification of their delivery. on the issue of the application of macro-economic conditionality, i take note of the report’s opposition to the commission’s proposal, and i would just like to underline that the commission’s proposal has been conceived not as coercive measures but rather to incentivise structural reforms and thus optimise effectiveness and absorption capacity. they are not meant to penalise the member states or regions that need financial support most urgently, but rather to ensure that precious capital will actually help address the very deficiencies identified in country-specific recommendations. on specific points raised in the report on administrative expenditure, i can confirm that the 5 % staff reduction over a period of five years is a major effort in the face of the crisis. it is an effort to which all institutions and agencies should contribute. finally, the issue of timing. i know that most of us in this chamber are convinced that we need a decision before the end of the year so that we can continue with efficient spending after 1 january 2014.
member of the commission. − mr president, let me start with the own resources issue. i would like to thank the european parliament right away for giving real impetus to the reforms and discussions. after the unhappy and rapid end of previous attempts, it was the european parliament, through its resolution, and alain lamassoure personally, who again initiated the attempt to reform the revenue side. alain was the person most insistent at the time that we should go ahead with a reform of the revenue side. parliament’s interim report contains a very forceful message – namely the unity of the budget. unity can be understood in different dimensions, but it is essential to understand the unity of the european budget as the complementary nature of expenditure and revenue. expenditure and revenue should reinforce each other and not stand in each other’s way. so we are agreed as to the deficiencies of the present system and the remedies. it is important to know that the commission today decided to authorise enhanced cooperation on a financial transaction tax. so the ball is now in play, as it is also with the vat system which will replace the current – very complex – statistical vat system. to complete the revenue side of the story: if one wants – and as london, for example, has said we need – a more transparent, simple and fair system of revenue, then we have the system of annual corrections, which is very transparent, simple and fair for everybody. i will now make a few points on horizontal issues, starting with flexibility. if there are more constraints, we need more flexibility. that is clear. what is less easy to foresee are unforeseen events, so therefore the emphasis on flexibility in our package has been increased. there is also a very clear signal in this respect in your interim report, and i would like to underscore the use of the so-called contingency margin. this has been useful on several occasions under the current financial framework – for example, the economic recovery plan was made possible through contingency margin decisions – but we are basing this on the acquis accumulated in this financial period and the experience of this financial period. so we are leaving things open on how to shape flexibility properly in the 2014-2020 budget. on the so-called ‘large-scale projects’, our decision to place galileo in and gms iter outside was based on the different nature of those large-scale projects. but what is essential is to ensure their proper financing, perhaps by ring-fencing, so again here we are in agreement. we are also in agreement, i am happy to inform you, as to the infamous ‘reste-à-liquider’ (ral). rals are generated through annual budgeting, and the only real solution to the issue of these outstanding ral commitments is through proper annual budgets and not through artificial devices that kill the predictability of commitments. a final point concerns simplification, which is a universal request that has come from every corner of the european union and on which we have to deliver. we have to deliver, and we have a new financial regulation. inga gräßle was a very tough partner in this negotiation, but it was very much conducive to the final goal of simplification. now, the second stage is to ensure against the propensity to make it more complex at the level of 57 spending programmes. so we need a scan of what is sectoral, in order not to allow a watering-down of the simplification proposal under the financial regulation. with such important and experienced players as today’s speakers, i am sure that we can offer the european union a decent 2014-2020 budget that provides predictability of investment for that seven-year period.
the committee on foreign affairs and security adopted a number of amendments to the proposal. - the the multiannual financial framework, mr president, will be maintained for seven years. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it seems that the council is calling for substantial cuts, which are contrary to the real situation of the union and to the economic crisis, because the multiannual financial framework can indirectly help. as regards the global europe section of the external action service, the european commission has rightly proposed increased funding and this should be there because the union has to play its international role and respond to unforeseen policies or other situations that arise on a daily basis. the european union must therefore be able to use all the instruments it has at its disposal, both for the various interventions in the beneficiary countries, particularly those which are being democratized, and for the external action service itself. mr president, parliament must support, on the whole, the increase in the multiannual financial framework as proposed by this house, and insist on it, because to remain within the framework that the council wants would also be an unacceptable symbol for the citizens of europe of how the council of the european union sees tackling its fundamental problems.
madam president, we all agree today that the multiannual financial framework is of paramount importance for the coming period, a period characterized - as everyone has said today - by high unemployment, recession and negative growth rates. so while we are all aware of the needs we have, it is particularly striking that for the allocation of funds, and i am referring in particular to the cohesion fund and the regional development fund, and i would like a reply from the commission and the presidency if possible, we take account of economic size and per capita income in the regions from the years 2009 and 2010. i would point out that this leads to great injustices, because from then until now we have had very rapid changes and adverse developments, in greece for example the economic contraction reached about 18.4%, a figure that is equivalent to that of germany and america during the great depression. does the commission see this injustice? i know that this has been put forward by the greek government. will we have any progress on this?
in writing . while the european parliament understands the budgetary constraints and rationalisation efforts of the member states, it must insist that the eu's long-term budget is a strategic tool for investment, growth and competitiveness and, of course, job creation across europe. the commission's proposal is to make the european union more competitive in the world market. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's development policy is not only a policy of development, but also a policy of development. cohesion policy is the main part of the response to the crisis, as it makes a decisive contribution to growth and job creation and deserves and is entitled to adequate resources. the european parliament therefore argues that cohesion policy funding should be maintained at least at the 2007-2013 level. by maintaining the current level of budget and with a modern, thematically targeted and results-oriented cohesion policy, i am convinced that we will achieve much more.
mr president, as regards the nobel peace prize, i would say to you, gentlemen - you who are to receive it - that you should give due credit to the european citizen who really deserves it, namely the citizen of the south, who can no longer even survive in the conditions in which he finds himself, and the european citizen of the north, who continues to support the south in a spirit of european solidarity. these two citizens are fighting and remaining steadfast in their european vision. mr president, since we have entered into a growth and employment agenda, i would say that we should look at the fact that the amounts of the structural funds, which are intended for the south and which are crucial for their development, are not being reduced.
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. without the growth dimension, fiscal consolidation efforts will fail. the capital increase of the european investment bank is an important step and can indeed have multiplier effects, particularly in countries undergoing fiscal adjustment. the citizens of my country, and of other countries, who are experiencing the harsh realities of fiscal adjustment, must soon come out of this situation. the single supervisory mechanism is indeed an element of the deepening of monetary union and its creation must be accelerated. banks are too often part of the problem, rather than part of the solution. mr president, there have been comments here on whether or not there is equality between the sexes. we must move from talk to action. europe has won the nobel prize not only for what it has done in the past 60 years but also for what it must do in the future, and more particularly in the immediate future, as a factor of stability, democracy, freedom, social justice and development.
mr president, the last european council confirmed that in the medium and long term there are good chances of moving in the right direction, but in the short term what is urgent is to overcome the crisis and that is why we need speed in decisions in the spirit of social justice, respect for democracy and consistency in implementation. and that is why, mr president of the commission, it is important and we welcome the decision of the european commission to put forward today the proposal for enhanced cooperation on the introduction of a financial transaction tax, because the truth is that it is one of the few socially fair measures that we have been able to introduce in this period. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the citizens must be convinced that their sacrifices are worthwhile, because without the citizens there can be no success in implementation and the union can help in this if it is consistent with its commitments.
in writing. the debate in the european parliament's plenary on the outcome of the eu summit on 18/19-10-2012 confirms the kke's assessment that the attack of capital, eu and bourgeois governments against the working class and popular strata is intensifying, in the context of inter-imperialist rivalries, both within the eu and with other imperialist centres and the dynamically rising capitalist economies which are intensifying in the face of the dead ends of the bourgeois management of the capitalist crisis. what unites them is their strategic choice to cheapen the labour force, to crush the lives and rights of the working class in all member states, in order to meet the ruthless monopoly competition. this is a disgusting reality which cannot be hidden either by the sham award of the 'nobel peace prize' to this imperialist wolf pack or by the 'magnificent' plans 'for a real economic and monetary union'. plans for greater integration of the eurozone and the eu are accompanied by the intensity of the centrifugal tendencies that are developing as they stumble upon the insurmountable contradictions between the bourgeois classes over the distribution and extent of the destruction of capital, overcoming the crisis and the difficulties of managing it that they entail.
in writing. i voted in favour of the joint resolution. the european parliament, in drawing up the new budget, is placing emphasis on supporting sustainable growth, competition and employment, particularly for smes and young people. for our country, where the youth unemployment rate is 55% (a negative record in the eu), adherence to this target group is an absolute priority. the text also makes it clear that savings at community level, although necessary, cannot be made at the expense of initiatives which support the european economy and growth. in this context, i voted against an amendment which affects, among other things, the agricultural production of certain products in our country.
in writing. the dispute over the multiannual financial framework 2014-2020 between those who want to reduce the budget and those who seek to increase it, attempts to hide its class orientation which is the allocation of workers' money to the strengthening and financing of the eu monopoly groups in the form of community funds. the common denominator of both conceptions is the strengthening of the competitiveness - profitability of the monopoly groups based in eu member states in the merciless war between themselves and with other imperialist centres as well as the emerging imperialist powers to secure their profits and control the markets. the european parliament proposes to restructure spending to strengthen the eu's most reactionary features, such as the european external action service (eeas), the galileo space and war programme, centres of excellence in research, etc. the allegedly strong resistance of the european parliament (jointly supported by the political representatives of capital and the opportunists) to the reduction of funds is yet another theatrical manipulation of popular forces, concealing that any increase in funds is directed at strengthening the monopoly groups and is accompanied by an intensification of the attack on the rights of the working class and the popular strata.
in writing. the question of the transfer of taxes from the member states to the community budget has been a matter of concern to the union almost since its inception. this regulation proposes a specific method to be used by the member states to calculate this new vat resource and to determine the procedures for its transfer to the eu budget. the proposal would base the new eu vat own resource on a percentage of vat on supplies of goods and services, purchases and imports subject to a standard vat rate in each member state. this ensures greater transparency and less need for adjustments to the vat base. however, the complex calculation method still exists and the question of direct taxation to the eu budget without having to be collected first by national administrations remains open today. i therefore voted in favour of this report, which embraces the commission's positive proposals, while at the same time setting out the shortcomings and the further actions and initiatives to be taken.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) for sweden to support the redundancies at the pharmaceutical company astrazeneca. the swedish authorities argue that the pharmaceutical sector is increasingly affected by globalisation. it is clear that the negative effects of globalisation further highlight the added value of the european globalisation adjustment fund as an eu social policy tool. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. moreover, the support provided is the practical expression of the union's solidarity. finally, the case of sweden is the best illustration of the fact that this financial instrument does not limit its activity only to countries facing economic and budgetary challenges but can be equally useful in helping the reintegration of the displaced into the labour market even in economically strong countries. this in turn underlines the importance of this fund and demonstrates the need to continue it during the upcoming multiannual financial framework 2014-2020.
the first item on the agenda is the commission statement on the commission work programme for 2013 [2012/2817 ((rsp)) ] i would remind members that there is no 'catch-the-eye' procedure for this debate.
vice-president of the commission. − madam president, i will try to react to the comments made in the interventions by the honourable members concerning the commission’s work programme. i am very sorry that i was not able to deliver the text of the commission’s work programme because it was approved just one-and-a-half hours ago. we had wanted to present it to the house immediately. however, now it has been adopted, i am sure that it will be available very soon, today. concerning the relationship between delegated and implementing acts, we have been working together with mr szájer on a common understanding on how to use delegated acts and how to use implementing acts. we both agree that the source of our proposals, whether we propose delegated or implementing acts, should be the treaty, because it is very clear on what kind of policies you need delegated acts for and for what kind of policies you need implementing acts. this was a topic we discussed at great length with the conference of presidents and with the conference of committee chairs. what we agreed was that we need to coordinate much better in our legislative process, and much earlier on, because what happens sometimes is that the delegated versus implementing act issue becomes a bargaining chip at the end of negotiations, which is not correct. very often this makes things legally unsound, and the commission has very often had to approach the european court of justice to reverse some of the decisions which have been adopted in the legislative process following the choice in our review of the wrong legal instrument for a particular legislative proposal. a suggestion would be to cooperate closer early on, to respect the treaty and not to use delegated or implementing acts as a bargaining chip at the end of the legislative process. concerning the regulatory burden, here we have absolutely the same view, which is that we need to reduce it. the commission has already put on the legislative table proposals for reducing the administrative burden by more than 31%. it is now our common task – and the commission will take this very seriously – to see how this possibility is actually being used at member state level. we are working together very intensely, but we do not always see all the simplifications we have introduced at european level trickle down to the national level. we have to continue to work on this together, so that the real reduction of the administrative burden is also felt at national level. i agree with mr harbour that there is always something to learn and improve on when it comes to the impact assessments. i consider we have been doing this for some time already, and i am very glad that the european parliament is also introducing its own capacity to perform impact assessments on the amendments proposed in parliament. i believe that through mutual efforts we can really improve the quality of the overall impact assessments for our proposals. concerning our efficiency, for the year 2012 there is a proposal for 16 main initiatives. we have delivered eight already, and it is still not the end of the year. so i believe we will respect our traditional high level of fulfilment of our programmes. i have checked the figures, and in 2010-2011 we always delivered on over 90% of what was in the programme. if we have slightly different figures, then let our experts get together so they can work on the same basis and arrive at the same figures. concerning the employment crisis, i fully agree with enrique guerrero salom that this should be a top priority for the next year, as i indicated in my introductory remarks. this december we will introduce the youth employment package, as part of which we will propose the youth employment guarantee scheme. this will be based a little on the experience we have gathered through the work of the eight action teams we sent to the member states with the highest youth unemployment. we believe that through the quick and swift reallocation of funds – which in most cases has already been done – we can create more than 400 000 jobs for young people that will be supported directly by the eu funds. this morning we were discussing the mff. i can tell you that in my remarks to the council there is one line which is repeated in all my remarks – make sure that 25% of the eu funds under the next financial perspective are devoted to the european social fund. the commission is very clear on this. we are saying that any kind of reduction in that percentage is not acceptable. i hope that we will be able to convince the member states of this, because i also see it as a reflection of our using eu support to create the conditions for youth and the elderly to be employed and to have proper qualifications. the last issue, which was repeated by several speakers, is the situation concerning my former colleague, commissioner dalli and the tobacco directive. concerning the resignation of commissioner dalli, it was quite clear that, after the olaf investigation and the discussion of the olaf report by the president of the commission and the commissioner, his position was untenable, especially vis-à-vis the reputation of the european commission. therefore, as you know, the process has already started for the appointment of the new commissioner. i believe, after my initial contacts here in the european parliament, that we can hold the hearing of the new commissioner within a couple of weeks and that the new commissioner could be in office very soon. i have already discussed the plan for the coming weeks with the chair of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety. we will have a meeting with mr harbour in a couple of minutes. what i want to make absolutely sure is that the work which has been started will continue. all the trialogue sessions will continue as planned. concerning the tobacco directive, for the commission it is absolutely vital that it is this legislator, this parliament, which will work on the tobacco directive. we want to make absolutely sure that we will present a good proposal upon which i believe this parliament can reach at least a first reading agreement. of course, it would be much more natural if the new commissioner could start this process from the beginning, through the trialogues, until the end. i was very encouraged by the possible schedule for the appointment of the new commissioner. i believe that the timescale will be very short, and i can assure you that it is a top priority for the commission to present the tobacco directive in time for this parliament to decide upon it.

the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is not a purely political matter. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. as a member of the committee on foreign affairs, i too worked with my colleagues to deal with the problems that would arise from the territories occupied by israel and not recognised by the european union, i.e. the post-1967 territories, and we put a question through mr brok to commissioner de gucht and commissioner de gucht came here and made it clear that one party can well impose on the other, through the protocol, that products coming from those areas are not recognised in certain areas. the technical issue is therefore essentially solved. the conflict between israel and the palestinians, mr president, is something much deeper, something which will not be affected by this agreement, which of course also concerns products which are of high quality and are offered at very good prices by israel. it is naive to think that such an agreement can have anything to do with the substance of the solution. the question is much more complex and we are all aware of the responsibilities that exist in general and the problems that the solution faces and we are of course aware of the efforts of the european union to give full support to the palestinian side. so we have no reason to link it to solving the problem. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
madam president, by voting today we are once again supporting the rights of passengers in all modes of transport. but we're taking it a step further. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's transport policy is not distorted by the introduction of new technologies. today's report brings together a fragmented legislative landscape, and is a step in the right direction, so that passengers are more fully aware of their rights, regardless of the means of transport. we are of course taking account of the specificities of each mode of transport, but it is very important that there should be a fundamental basis which applies to all modes. we are achieving greater transparency and greater awareness of citizens' rights.
in writing. the kke group voted against the report on the draft amending budget for 2012 because it transfers 10% of eu funds from the cohesion fund and the european regional development fund to the direct financing of monopoly groups with the so-called 'risk-sharing instruments'. the funds from the eu budget, which are again workers' money, are being used to guarantee the low-interest bank loans of the monopolies, to carry out projects in high-profit sectors which have nothing to do with the needs of the people. this is the growth that the eu and city governments are fighting for. hot money to corporations to maintain and restore their profitability, harsh austerity and the crushing of workers' lives and rights, skyrocketing unemployment. capitalist crisis and capitalist development are the two sides of the coin of the savage exploitation of the working class and the popular strata. the satisfaction of the people's needs is impossible without untying and overturning the chariot of capitalist european union, overthrowing the power of the monopolies in each member state and passing it into the hands of the working class and its allies.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal, which amends council decision 2008/971/ec to allow the import of the ‘qualified’ category of forest reproductive material into the european union from certain third countries that satisfy the conditions to meet the ‘qualified’ category and the updating of the name of the authorities responsible for the approval and control of production.
in writing. citizenship of the union is automatically granted to all citizens of the respective member states and does not replace but is complementary to national citizenship. this commission report responds positively to the proposal for a european year to enhance awareness of the rights attached to citizenship of the union. however, although the proposal gives a boost to the concept of citizenship, there are problems associated with the everyday lives of citizens such as, for example, the lack of equal opportunities for all, mobility opportunities for young people and lack of administrative efficiency. increasing awareness therefore means increasing citizens' participation and co-creation in the european construction. the proposals contained in this report contribute to this and that is why i voted in favour of it.
in writing. i voted in favour of mrs papadopoulos' report. it is fundamental to democracy and pluralism that european citizens are aware of their rights deriving from their european identity/citizenship. the establishment of the european year of citizens (2013) is an important initiative and contributes to the deepening of the democratic institutions of the european union.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report as i believe that respecting fundamental rights values is a prior condition for eu-russia relations. the reform of the judicial system is essential in order to be in conformity with international standards.
in writing. i voted in favour of mrs foster's report because its aim is to reduce or even eliminate flight delays, to reduce the cost of air transport services and the price of tickets, to increase air traffic and create jobs and, ultimately, to promote economic growth in the tourism sector.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) for ireland to support the 432 redundancies at talk talk broadband services and three of its suppliers in the telecommunications sector. it is regrettable that this company has decided to ally itself with three key suppliers outside the eu, where a significant part of the work has been relocated, reflecting a strategy that is detrimental to eu industrial jobs and to the eu 2020 strategy in general. the negative effects of globalisation, combined with the ongoing economic crisis in ireland, further highlight the added value of the egf as an instrument of eu social policy. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. moreover, the support provided is the practical expression of the union's solidarity. the irish government is making efforts to streamline the budget and drastically cut expenditure. the eib's support will be able to contribute to the fight against unemployment without significantly burdening the national budget's fiscal consolidation efforts.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) for spain to support 878 redundancies in 35 metal manufacturing enterprises in the galicia region. the economy of this region has suffered a severe economic blow, as the opening up to the globalised market has negatively affected important sectors of the economy, such as this one. the commission considers that the aid is compatible with the internal market if it is granted to the producers of the product concerned and if it is granted to the producers of the product concerned. the negative effects of globalisation, combined with the continuing economic crisis in spain, further highlight the added value of the egf as an instrument of eu social policy. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. moreover, the support provided is the practical expression of the union's solidarity. the spanish regions are subject to a process of rationalization and drastic cuts. the eib's support will be able to contribute to the fight against unemployment without significantly burdening the efforts of fiscal consolidation of the national and regional budgets of spain.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) for the netherlands to support 435 redundancies in 54 companies active in the building sector. this report shall cover the nine-month period between 1 february and 1 november 2011. during this period, the crisis derogation allowed for the provision of financial assistance to employees made redundant as a result of the current financial and economic crisis and the increase in the co-financing rate. it is regrettable that, despite the continuing crisis, the council has blocked the extension of this derogation. the case of the netherlands is the best illustration of the fact that this financial instrument does not limit its activity only to countries facing economic and budgetary challenges but can be equally useful in helping the reintegration of the displaced into the labour market even in economically strong countries. this in turn underlines the importance of this fund and demonstrates the need for it to continue during the next multiannual financial framework 2014-2020.

in writing. the kke's position is well known that the so-called 'fund for adaptation to globalisation' is a means of manipulating the conscience and defusing the indignation of workers, by distributing crumbs of consolation to workers who are thrown into unemployment by the capitalists. but even these crumbs do not go to direct support for the unemployed, but to the urban governments, to various training programmes and so on. the kke voted against the report because it is provocative to ask the french government to disburse funds from this fund for the thousands of redundancies made by the french monopoly group peugeot, which, even in the midst of a capitalist crisis, has billions of euros in profits (1.7 billion in 2010 and 1.3 billion in 2011). the workers' movement cannot be reduced to claiming the crumbs of the globalisation fund to vent the workers' anger, leaving untouched the french monopoly which throws workers into the street to maintain its profitability and the bourgeois french government of social democracy, which defends monopoly interests with all means. the kke supports the workers' struggle to re-employ the dismissed and the working class' struggle to make the plutocracy pay for the crisis that created it and not the working class and the poor.

in writing. the committee voted against the report because both the 1978 international convention on maritime education of the international maritime organisation and the so-called 'manila amendments' proposed for incorporation into eu law are far behind and do not address the needs for modern and high-quality maritime education, ensuring the effective protection of human life at sea and the environment. the public nature of maritime training is not guaranteed, and the working time arrangements are medieval. they provide for work of up to 14 hours a day or more, with various exceptions, which leads to the rapid deterioration of the physical and mental health of seafarers, increasing to the extreme the risks of maritime accidents with incalculable consequences. the eu strategy is to ensure the lowest possible level of maritime training by increasing the exploitation of seafarers by shipping capital, which is facilitated and thus replaces seafarers with structured labour rights with others - mainly from third countries - without rights and with humiliating starvation wages. the kke supports the struggle of the working class shipping movement for modern, high-level maritime training, 5 days, 7 hours, 35 hours, strict measures to protect the health and safety of seafarers, wages that meet modern needs, full and stable work with rights for all seafarers.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposal because it is of utmost importance in securing maritime safety, and that crews being trained in different countries and under different systems operating on board the same ship have the capacities needed to perform their duties in a safe manner. the proposal includes several key aspects that strengthen this outcome. these are: updated standards relating to medical fitness, fitness for duty as well as alcohol abuse; new requirements concerning certification for able seafarers, and for electro-technical officers, as well as security-related training for all seafarers; clarification and simplification of the definition of ‘certificate’ amongst others.
in writing. the kke group deplores the decision of the eu council to conclude a protocol to the euro-mediterranean association agreement between the eu and israel 'concerning the verification of conformity and acceptance of industrial products (dca) ' and its approval by the political staff of the monopolies in the european parliament. this protocol effectively allows direct trade between the eu and its member states and israeli settlements in the israeli-occupied palestinian territories and the export to the eu of israeli products produced there. the agreement is in effect a recognition and legitimization of the israeli occupation and its crimes against the palestinian people. it is a continuation of the eu's overall imperialist policy in the region, which is systematically upgrading its political, economic, commercial and military relations with israel, both in the framework of the eu-israel association agreement and israel's accession to the single european sky. the european union is not only condemning israel's settlements in the occupied territories, but is also recognizing the israeli occupation and arming its murderous hand against the palestinian people.
in writing. the economic environment in which the eu finds itself creates cracks in the development goals set by the europe 2020 strategy. it is a fact that europe is lagging behind in competitiveness many asian economies while at the same time shrinking demographically. the comparative own-initiative proposal, which i voted in favour of, is a response to the commission's communication 'an action plan for change', while calling for greater emphasis to be placed on the use of funds to fight poverty in the eu (which is enlarging) and on those factors which constitute an asset for the eu such as innovation, entrepreneurship and the social state. although not binding, the report contains useful proposals to the member states and the commission to support employment and growth prospects without social exclusion and without discrimination.
in writing. i voted in favour of the own-initiative report on proposals to support small and medium-sized enterprises. in greece, small and mediumsized enterprises and family-type enterprises account for 98% of enterprises. the economic crisis, in addition to effectively excluding them from access to credit and liquidity, threatens their existence. the recent assurances by the eib that it will provide them with substantial amounts are important but do not solve the problem altogether. support for small and mediumsized enterprises means support for entrepreneurship, innovation and jobs. in this proposal, the european parliament calls on the commission and the member states to show even greater determination in this direction. among other things, it is proposed to broaden the entrepreneurial mindset in the eu and attract more people to the business world through community programmes, as well as providing incentives such as sme-friendly tax systems and other tax benefits.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because i believe that the motion for a resolution highlights the crucial importance of small and mediumsized enterprises for the european economy and proposes very appropriate interventions to remove obstacles and develop entrepreneurship.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr moreira's report as trade and foreign investment must be better used to stimulate growth, which will lead to greater job creation and increase the well-being of the people. i believe that more steps must be taken towards eu-us trade relations as it is widely accepted that the potential of the transatlantic relationship has not yet been fully exploited.

madam president, i would like to wish you a happy birthday, but i think that is not enough. i feel that to be correct, we should wish for "long and good years", "long and effective years", "long and fast years". the technology is beyond us. so far we have all been giving the example of success in referring to the single market when we point out how cheap we are now to talk on our mobile phones and this is an achievement of the single market. but i have to give you a negative example: we all pretty much have in this room the famous ipads. but if this ipad, this smart device, you bought it, for example, in belgium and you go to the uk or another member state, if you have a problem, the answer is "go to the country where you bought this device". i say this to show that we must be quicker in our actions because it is not enough to talk, it is not enough to celebrate. we must act. we have talked about 12 basic actions and 50 complementary actions. of the 12 key actions, 11 have been left behind, and this was acknowledged at an official commission event. all of this is troubling me. i am, however, optimistic because in times of crisis the single market is more necessary than ever for the european citizen who is really suffering.
president-in-office of the council. − madam president, the celebration of the 20th anniversary of the single market saw a series of events taking place in brussels, at the european parliament and in all member states which culminated in the ‘single market week for new growth’ from 15 to 20 october. i personally had the honour to participate in the closing event in nicosia on 20 october, at which commissioner barnier, the chair of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection (imco) – malcolm harbour – and imco member malgorzata handzlik spoke about the past, present and future of the single market in front of numerous representatives of civil society. as members have confirmed in their speeches, the establishment of the single market brought not only huge benefits for citizens by increasing the choice of products and services and lowering their prices but was also a driving force for businesses, fostering their competitiveness and innovation capacity. however, despite impressive achievements the single market and its benefits are not yet fully visible to the citizens, for whom it was originally created. the single market still remains for many a very abstract and bureaucratic concept which is unfortunately too often reduced to top-down rule-making by brussels. furthermore, even after 20 years of its existence, there is still a considerable gap between expectations and reality regarding the single market, with european citizens and businesses still facing obstacles in exercising their rights – or to put it more bluntly – even after 20 years of success, there is absolutely no room for complacency. on the contrary, what we learned from the celebrations is that we have to step up our efforts in order to pursue the establishment of a seamless single market. firstly, we need to better inform consumers and businesses on the existing tools, in order to create the kind of ownership which is absolutely necessary for stakeholders to move the single market forward. secondly, we need to pass on the message that despite the global financial crisis the single market did not fragment further – which is an encouraging sign of inherent strength, and hence the eu has to build on that. moreover, completing the single market is not only about passing new laws, but increasingly a matter of implementing correctly and enforcing the already existing rules. thirdly, as europe and other parts of the world struggle to recover from the financial crisis, boosting growth and employment is the number one priority for leaders across the eu. europe has enormous unrealised potential to restore growth and confidence by unleashing the power of such a single market. in this respect, the presidency welcomes the launch earlier this month of the new single market act ii by commissioner barnier. the proposals concentrate, very rightly, on areas of the single market with the highest potential for growth, and should also work as a vector for social and territorial cohesion and for integrating europe’s markets better by improving mobility for individuals and businesses, encouraging entrepreneurship and making finance more accessible across the eu. in addition to the above, the 12 key actions proposed concentrate on the network industries and the digital economy. integration in these areas is essential to creating the conditions for more economic growth and new jobs. notably, the creation of jobs is an absolute priority for the presidency as the economic crisis has threatened social cohesion. unemployment – especially among the young – is skyrocketing in some places. in this respect i would like to seize this opportunity to repeat our presidency’s full commitment to finalising as many as possible pending dossiers on the single market act i, in particular those covering alternative dispute resolution, on-line dispute resolution, venture capital, social investment funds and the accounting directives. promising progress has been achieved on patents, as was mentioned by commissioner barnier, and i am grateful to him, as well as to the european parliament for the understanding and positive cooperation on this very delicate issue. we are indeed now at a watershed and if we can overcome the persisting difficulties we will at last have the chance to put in place a genuinely european patent system. the european council has set the goal of agreement on all these proposals by the end of 2012. in short, we will all be committed to intensifying the current pace of work. i trust there will be sufficient flexibility on the part of both legislators, enabling timely adoption. let me conclude by recapitulating the following imperatives: the single market is the biggest economic – but also political – asset of the european union. despite the recent economic downturn, the single market has continued to function and to deliver. however, in order to be able to reap all its benefits it is essential to breathe new life into that single market, with european citizens and consumers placed at its centre. i remain confident that together we will manage to successfully implement the envisaged measures in order to make the single market a vibrant and living reality for europe’s citizens. congratulations to commissioner barnier and the commission, the european parliament, and in particular to the chair of the internal market committee, malcolm harbour, to the rapporteur, regina bastos, and to all the other members and other people who are working so hard to make a genuine single market part of our daily life in the european union.
member of the commission. - madam president, i would like to thank each and every one of you very sincerely for the quality of this very dynamic debate and for your very large participation. many of you have said - mr scicluna, at the very beginning, mrs werthmann, mr obermayr - that we need the single market for growth. i repeat that it is not enough for the single market to function properly. there are many national, private, public and european initiatives which should encourage growth. nevertheless, the single market is a necessary condition. if it works well, all the private and public initiatives supporting the single market will work better and be more effective. that is why i cannot agree with mr zeribi, who was not here when i spoke and has now left, and who has called me on several occasions to tell me that i was complacent. frankly, that's not the tone of my speech, there's no room for self-satisfaction, no room for nostalgia and melancholy right now. not one! we must take note of the progress made, and you have been in many political groups to highlight all this progress in terms of employment. mr ferreira also left but he was talking about leveling down. that's not true, man. the 2 500 000 students who have taken part in erasmus, the 70% reduction in the price of mobile phones, the millions of citizens who now have access to cheaper air transport - all this is not a bottom-up approach! these are the advantages of the single market. but i think we also have to look ahead. mr pargneaux was just telling us about the new model. i know! we are working and must work together on very concrete initiatives for this new economic and social model. mr pargneaux asked me about the initiatives for enhanced cooperation. take the patent for example. i would like to thank the commissioner for his report, and i would like to thank him for his work in this area. or the basic bank account, which you all agreed to request. these initiatives are part of this model. there you go, no self-satisfaction. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the single market is not a one-off event, but a one-off event, and that the single market is not a one-off event. let us make sure that it works well and reorient it towards what i believe and you believe too: the competitive social market economy. the four words are important and not just one or two, as we may have believed, including in brussels, in the last twenty years. many of you have spoken, both in concrete terms and by telegraph, of networks. these are the arteries of the single market. that is why we have made it a priority of the single market act ii. there is a lot to be done in this area. mr creutzmann and mr pirker referred to the railway issues which are being discussed by the committee on transport and by mr simpson. we have procedures for certifying railway companies, for approving rolling stock, which are far too long, far too expensive. it is not a question of liberalization, but of promoting harmonization so that the free movement of persons and goods is effective. we have to harmonize our policies, including, mr correia de campos, energy. the absence of a single energy market, which mrs vergnaud also mentioned, costs consumers 13 billion. we must therefore harmonise the single energy market. i could give you other examples in the maritime field. why is it necessary - this is just an example - that when a cargo ship arrives in naples, a cargo ship arriving from rotterdam has to complete the same formalities as a cargo ship arriving from shanghai? there is no single market. we must tackle these problems in a concrete way. that is the aim of all the measures we are going to take with mr kallas in the single market act ii. i also told mr becker that we do a good job of collective work, as you do yourself. the single market act i and ii implement the work of many committees of the european parliament, as i do not work alone but with twelve of my fellow commissioners. this is particularly the case with my colleague, mrs kroes, who is responsible for the digital agenda. i have listened very carefully to what president harbour has said, but also to what mr rübig, mrs rapti, mr arias echeverría, whom i thank very much, and, more recently, to what mrs jazłowiecka, mrs stihler and mr creutzmann have said on the digital single market. i think that we have embarked on a very large project, which is not going fast enough. if we were to think, not too late, about what a single market act iii could be in 2014, mr president harbour, we should probably focus on new measures to give momentum to the digital single market. in any case, i intend to discuss this with mrs kroes and my other colleagues. mr de jong and mr karas spoke of smes and product safety. i am very concerned that all the measures we take - and mr tajani is also concerned - in the single market act are weighed against the advantages and disadvantages for small and mediumsized enterprises. i'll take care of it. i would simply like to call on you, as i call on the council, mr mavroyiannis, the cypriot minister, whom i thank for being here, to speed up the adoption of certain texts. on public procurement, mr tarabella, accounting standards, capital requirements iv, for which we have made proposals - the rapporteur, mr karas, knows this - in favour of sme financing by the banking sector, on transparency. these are very concrete texts where, if you look in detail, you will find measures or provisions which are more favourable or less restrictive for small and mediumsized enterprises. in addition, i would like to thank mr kósa for mentioning the daily lives of disabled people. we will be concerned, together with mrs reding, about the safety of products, and specific measures will be proposed in 2013 on the access of disabled people to the greatest number of products. many of you have mentioned the social dimension and i thank you for that - the competitive social market economy - and in particular mrs regner, mrs rühle, mr de jong and mr rübig. one of the keys, once we know that we have to move, that we have to reform, is what some countries do better than others - i compare, for example, the quality of social dialogue in germany with the often lack of social dialogue in my own country. we cannot reform, we cannot win the battle of competitiveness and sustainable growth without social cohesion, without social dialogue, without respect for the social partners. i would like to express my agreement with this and also say to those who have expressed concern about privatisation, mrs rühle, mrs regner, that the treaty clearly provides, and i believe definitively, that each country has a free choice in the organisation of its public services. and i am the first, where i am, to want to preserve this freedom of choice for the quality of public services. simply, if one or two countries want to choose a different economic model, it is their sovereign choice to use external providers for certain public service missions. we are therefore accountable for compliance with the rules on public procurement or concessions, and in particular the rules on transparency. i have therefore tabled some texts on public procurement and concessions, but i am absolutely in favour of ensuring that each country has the freedom of choice, particularly in the case of public services. mr schwab and mr buşoi have again mentioned, and i thank them for that, implementation, as has minister mavroyiannis. i am encouraged by the call by president van rompuy and the european council for governments to implement quickly and concretely many of the texts we have put forward, and i will ensure, with the commission and you, that we can achieve concrete agreements in the coming weeks or months. mr bufton, earlier you and mrs mazej kukovič mentioned an important aspect, namely the external dimension of the single market. we're not all alone. we must continue to be a continent open to trade, without naivety, as perhaps we have had a little too much of in the past. but we must look at the rest of the world, which sometimes still hopes that europe will be there, but no longer expects us, whether it is brazil, china, the united states or india. so let us consolidate this single market because, ladies and gentlemen, i assure you that when you go to these big countries - i was in brazil last week - you see that the only reason they respect the europeans is clearly the strength of this single innovation. since i am also speaking of consumer and citizen protection, i would like to reiterate to mr kósa that i am very attentive to the daily living conditions of disabled people. it is in this supporting, as you know, the social enterprise sector. many companies are actually geared towards disabled people. finally, i repeated to mr karas, who spoke of the protection of citizens and in writing. 2012 marks 20 years since the single market came into being, creating a borderless area within which people, goods, services and capital can move freely. the benefits, economic and social, are clear. however, both experience and the economic crisis have brought to light weak points. this own-initiative report presents an overview of practical obstacles (information, implementation, legislative gaps). the single market must also be modern, and in this context this report, which i voted in favour of, underlines the need to create a digital single market that works properly and safely for consumers and businesses. it is also clear that better governance and co-operation are essential conditions for its creation.

the next item on the agenda is the report by mr jean-paul gauzès, on behalf of the committee on economic and monetary affairs, on the european semester for economic policy coordination: implementation of the 2012 priorities [2012/2150(ini) ].
mr president, i should like to begin by congratulating mr gauzès on his report and also on his commitment to the question of the economic governance of the european union. the report we are discussing today is the first full account of the european semester and the conclusions drawn from it are therefore very important for its future application. the european semester is emerging today as an excellent tool for coordinating our policy objectives and responsibilities between the european and national levels. the crisis has highlighted the significant differences between the regions of the union, the macroeconomic imbalances between national economies and the extent to which they have widened in recent years. the difficulties in addressing these weaknesses and the specificities of member states in their efforts to achieve fiscal adjustment and growth call for us to make use of all the possibilities, in policies and resources, to achieve the common objective of stability and growth. the european semester is therefore a tool of this kind. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the draft budget for the financial year 2000 and on the draft budget for the financial year 2000. the involvement of national parliaments is very important for the effectiveness of our policies, since national parliaments can now take a more responsible part in the planning of reform programmes and budgets submitted, as well as in the evaluation of budget implementation and their effectiveness. they can certainly contribute to more successful governance, but also to making local actors and the european people more familiar with european objectives and obligations. the commission has also proposed that the council should be able to adopt a decision on the matter. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a good example of the need to ensure that the safety of workers is not compromised by the introduction of new technologies. i therefore believe that the european commission must be clear, specific in its recommendations, in order to give meaning to this potential of the european semester to contribute to better governance and a more stable european union.
the commission is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: mr president, the actions for the danube macro-region do not serve to meet the needs of the people, such as employment with rights, education, housing, culture, flood control and water resources development projects. the macro-region does not mean industrial and rural development in these terms, but the deepening of the single capitalist market and the capitalist restructuring that has led the peoples to unemployment, mass poverty and bankruptcy. its actions serve the anti-people europe 2020 strategy and the energy giants, while taking away significant resources for the benefit of the project, connecting europe. the reorientation and use of cohesion policy funds will primarily benefit the profitability of european union enterprises operating on world markets, will intensify the crushing of labour and popular rights, will exacerbate uneven growth. we therefore believe that the big losers will be the workers who are called upon to make the hard sacrifices to support a development which does not address their needs.
on behalf of the efd group. mr president, i am particularly pleased that, as the representative of the last wing of this house, i can see full agreement in the recognition of mr diamantoure's work and his excellent cooperation with the committee on petitions. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use (aema) is a step in the right direction. according to the ombudsman's report, 2,510 complaints were registered in 2011 and the institution directly helped more than 22,000 citizens. that's a very good performance. i believe that this important institution should be made more widely known to the citizens of the european union, given that according to a eurobarometer report, 42% of european citizens are not satisfied with the level of transparency in the administration of the european union. the institution is necessary and the exercise of this institution has been successful so far. as is the case every year, the majority of inquiries carried out by the ombudsman concerned the european commission - some 58% - and it is particularly important to devise appropriate measures to strengthen cooperation with the commission in the interests of european citizens. in particular, at this difficult time of crisis in institutions and democratic values, i congratulate mr diamantouros on the excellent way in which he is carrying out his duties, and i also congratulate mrs mazzoni, chairman of the committee on petitions, who is contributing to the good cooperation between the two institutions. i take this opportunity to give a ton of optimism and to say that the crisis will pass. we must restore the citizens' confidence in the institutions and the european union.
madam president, greece has recently made a monumental effort, alongside economic efforts, to combat illegal immigration, and this in an environment of extremist and racist voices. however, we are already seeing tangible results with the efforts being made. the flows of illegal immigrants to evros have been greatly reduced and overall flows into our country have been curtailed because of better border controls, and i would remind you that this country is also under great pressure at the moment from events in syria. but it is the right time, and that is why i am mentioning it, to realise that europe can no longer bear the burden of illegal immigration on its own. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a good example of the need to ensure that the safety of workers is not compromised by the use of asbestos.
madam president, on 6 april 2009, a powerful earthquake struck the region of abruzzo in central italy. many dozens of people were killed, schools, public buildings, kindergartens, hospitals and other infrastructure collapsed. the european parliament, bypassing bureaucratic procedures and following a proposal from our italian colleagues and the mobilisation of the european solidarity fund, has approved eur 500 million for the relief of the inhabitants of the earthquake-stricken areas and for the repair of damage. three years later, just last monday, madam president, an italian court convicted six fellow seismologists for failing to predict the earthquake. this is an absurd, unprecedented and, in my view, dangerous decision. this decision does not condemn seismologists, it does not condemn scientists, it condemns science itself and all sciences. it is not my intention to ask the european parliament to intervene in the independent italian judiciary, but let me say, madam president, that the judges are also judged.

in writing. this proposal from the european parliament's own initiative examines the functioning and effectiveness of existing financial instruments (fi) and the scope for developing a range of new innovative instruments combining grants from the union budget and public and/or private funding to increase investment in europe. in any case, flexible measures in this direction are also in greece's interest in order to free up community funds for enterprises which will, on the one hand, provide liquidity to the market and, on the other hand, contribute to the development effort made by the country. i therefore voted in favour of this report. the commission will also assess the impact of the new multiannual financial framework on the budgetary position of greece, in particular in the light of the outcome of the 2014-2020 programming period. both in the european parliament and at the level of governments, the country is demanding that greece's current gdp be taken into account, rather than the gdp of the period 2009-2010 calculated by the commission, which is about 18% higher than today, depriving our country of valuable resources from the european structural funds.
in writing. the commission estimates that the implementation of the eu 2020 strategy requires a total investment of several billion euros by 2020 across the eu. however, the lack of resources in recent years has made such investments difficult. i voted in favour of the report because the ultimate objective of innovative financial instruments and their catalytic effect is to enable, on the basis of a contribution from the union budget, funds to be raised for projects in cases where the market proves to be inadequate or incomplete. in this way, public intervention can reduce the cost of potential risks or partially bear them, facilitating the implementation of these projects. as regards the design of new financial instruments, i agree with the importance of ex ante evaluation to identify situations of market failure or sub-optimal investments, as well as the need to provide for a number of simple quality indicators for ex ante and ex post evaluation. these instruments, in combination with project bonds, will make investments more attractive and will have a multiplier effect to make further investments. in this way, they will play a key role in the recovery efforts of the member states and in the strategy for growth.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the activities of the european ombudsman in 2011. the information provided to the european parliament shows that the role of the european ombudsman is not only gaining in visibility and serving our fellow citizens in europe, but also consolidating its position as a means of accountability in the union. it is significant that in 2011 the ombudsman registered 2,510 complaints, of which 53% were submitted via the electronic complaint form, which is available on the ombudsman's website in all official eu languages. issues of transparency, public and personal data access and control of contract and grant award are the main requests to the ombudsman. further promotion of electronic communication with the ombudsman is necessary and with the very good work being done by mr n. diamantorus from this crucial position it is a given that it will be achieved.
