on behalf of the efd group. mr president, i want to extol the work of the rapporteurs. i share most of the concerns and suggestions expressed in their reports. i also commend the work of commissioner damannakis, who has stood by the committee throughout its work. i am not going to go into the details of the commission's proposals, but i would like to say that the commission is very much aware of the problems facing the community in this area. the report is being discussed at a crucial moment, because we are now shaping the common fisheries policy which will lay the foundations of fisheries for the next ten years. it is necessary to give special attention to small-scale fishing and its management, both in the context of the common fisheries policy and in terms of adequate funding from the new maritime and fisheries fund. small-scale fishing accounts for 80% of the fishing fleet in the european union. the traditional fisheries sector, because of its structural weaknesses, is more exposed to external shocks. the economic crisis, the increase in oil prices, market failures and bottlenecks in the supply of the product are some of the problems affecting the sector and need to be addressed by best practices. the rapporteur's proposals are sufficiently positive. the most important problem, however, as highlighted by the industry, is that of overfishing of stocks. 80% of the mediterranean fish stocks are overfished without adequate recovery, and the european regulation on fisheries in the mediterranean is violated every day. i think the issue of monitoring the application and compliance of member states with existing legislation is a key one. the future of fisheries in europe lies in coastal fishing activities which are sustainable, have a low impact on the marine environment and are a driver of growth and jobs in europe. the small-scale fisheries sector has enormous advantages which must be exploited.
mr president, i would first like to refer to the issue of the external dimension in ms lövin’s report. i am very sad at what is happening in parliament concerning this issue. the report was adopted in committee with 24 votes in favour and 1 against. nevertheless, i am afraid that the ppe group has decided that no other political group should be given reports of this kind. i cannot otherwise understand why, in order to delete three lines of this report, it is tabling an alternative report instead of just asking for a split vote. mr mato adrover is a good chair of the committee on fisheries, and i would ask him to have a rethink, because a chair cannot go against a 24-1 majority in his committee. on the issue of shark finning, i am confident that tomorrow a great majority within this parliament will vote in favour of sharks being landed with their fins naturally attached. we will close the loopholes in the legislation. i am confident that the majority in parliament tomorrow will honestly ban shark finning across the eu.

madam president, first of all i would like to thank president caldeira for presenting the 2011 annual report of the court of auditors. in the context of the current economic and financial crisis, the pressure on public finances is now even greater and therefore the efficient use of public money is more important than ever. according to the court's report, the combined error rate for regional policy in transport, energy, employment and social affairs decreased significantly compared to 2010 from 7,7% to 5,1%. we must ensure that european union money is spent more efficiently, which does not require spending cuts, but better control. the national authorities must commit themselves to better managing eu funds, since according to the court of auditors more than 60% of the errors found in the implementation of regional policy could have been detected by the member states if the appropriate checks had been carried out.
in writing. i voted in favour of the resolution on the doha climate change conference because it recognises that climate change is an urgent and irreversible threat to human societies and the planet, it proposes a legally binding global agreement based on the principle of 'shared but differentiated responsibilities' and it recognises that the scientific evidence leaves little room for delay. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment is a very important one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area. the european parliament has already expressed its concern about the situation in the middle east, and the european union has also expressed its concern about the situation in the middle east. the doha conference, however, must not refer the matter to the next international conference as is the custom, it must record commitments with a specific timetable for reducing emissions and not be limited to vague policies on the need to reduce them. after all, ecology and economics can coexist. let's not give the winner an extension of rights for some catastrophe.
in writing. the kke voted against the report enlargement: eu policies, criteria and strategic interests (a7 0274/2012) because it dangerously reinforces the reactionary character and aggressiveness of the eu against the peoples of its member states, the acceding states, but also the states of the wider region. the eu claims to impose its own imperialist 'law' on international relations and cross-border disputes. international disputes will be turned into bilateral disputes under the auspices of the eu, which will undertake to resolve them in its own interests. the eu should intervene to all parties to disputes ..... to resort to the international court of justice or to a binding arbitration mechanism ... by asking the commission and the council to set up an arbitration mechanism aimed at resolving bilateral and multilateral disputes. the accession process is used by the eu to enforce the eu strategy europe 2020 in order to serve the interests of the monopolies. the eu 'transforms the political, socioeconomic and cultural data of the countries' that wish to join the eu by funding mechanisms of the bourgeois state, ngos and the labour aristocracy of the trade unions to manipulate the labour movement.
in writing. i voted in favour of mrs coppa's report because it confirms the european parliament's commitment to enlargement policy and introduces innovative approaches to improve it. the new realities, such as the economic crisis and global challenges, call for a new approach to enlargement.
in writing. the european parliament's committee on foreign affairs and security has called for the eu to take action to prevent the use of force by the palestinian authority against palestinian civilians. the european parliament has been asked to take a decision on the situation in the middle east, and the european parliament has been asked to take a decision on the situation in the middle east. it says nothing about israel's murderous bombing of palestinian civilians. it is not asking for an end to the criminal orgy of the israeli occupation forces. instead, it defends israel's "right to protect its population", but calls for it to "act in a proportionate manner". so he defends israel's crimes, as long as they are "proportional"! we support the struggle of the palestinian people against the israeli occupation, for an independent, viable, sovereign palestinian state on the 1967 borders with east jerusalem as its capital. the lifting of all blockades of palestinians in the west bank and the gaza strip. the immediate release of all palestinian and other political prisoners in israeli prisons. the withdrawal of the israeli army from all the territories occupied in 1967. we demand that the greek government immediately cancel its military cooperation with israel. shut down the soda base now.
in writing. the decision to call a ceasefire in the gaza strip last night is the first step towards de-escalating the hostilities. in this resolution, parliament reiterates its firm position on a two-state solution, the state of israel and an independent, democratic and viable state of palestine coexisting in peace and security. i voted in favour of this resolution which recognises that the long-running conflict which is continually taking the lives of civilians and children cannot be resolved by complaints and appeals. the whole international community and the eu, which has a special interest in the security and peaceful coexistence of the peoples of the region, should play a more active political role in efforts to achieve a just and lasting peace between israelis and palestinians. it is also important to note that the situation in syria is not improving and that a reversal of the situation in the whole region is a threat to international peace and security.
in writing. the kke voted against the report on the common security and defence policy (csdp) because it signifies the strengthening of the reactionary characteristics and the expansion of the imperialist wars of the eu throughout the world, being based on the new strategic doctrine of nato. the report leaves the community institutions because they have not made full use of the full range of eu military means. the eu-us-nato imperialist war in libya is being exploited to promote the 'concentration and sharing' of arms for even more murderous operations against the people. the report calls for an increase in military operations and their financing by the stability and development funds, the creation of a permanent eu army general staff, the immediate use of euro-army units (battlegroups) and the upgrading of the european defence agency. it also called for the acceleration of enhanced military cooperation (the baimar initiative), interventions by 'alliances of interest' and joint armaments programmes leading to the centralisation of the defence industry. the eu is stepping up its intervention in syria and stepping up israel's attacks against the palestinian people - a bridgehead for imperialist plans to intervene in iran to control the resources and exploit the people. the eu is an imperialist alliance of monopolies and an enemy of the people in its member states and throughout the world.
in writing. the report fully confirms the positions of the kke, which warned that the clauses of solidarity and mutual defence in the anti-people lisbon treaty deepen the militarisation and reactionary characteristics of the eu and strengthen the mechanisms for shielding the dictatorship of the monopolies. the clauses promote eu military and political interventions in member states under the pretext of 'terrorism', 'natural or man-made disasters', 'cyber attacks' or even 'pandemics' or 'energy shortages'. the report calls the actions and mobilizations of the popular movement a 'threat to democratic institutions'. the report calls for the eu and its member states to commit themselves to a 'all-out' response in close cooperation with nato, and calls for the eu to adopt the new strategic approach of nato, so that together the eu and nato act as the world's policeman and terrorist of the people. the report calls for a 'permanent operational headquarters' and a 'permanent eu crisis management response system', 'crisis management readiness exercises' and the deployment of military capabilities and 'rapid reaction forces'. the kke votes against this reactionary and anti-people report and thus contributes to strengthening the popular alliance and the anti-monopoly struggle of the working class and its allies for secession from the eu and for popular power.
in writing. the role of small-scale fisheries is very important. i voted in favour of mr ferreira's report because i believe that small-scale fishing needs to be given special attention and management, both in the context of the common fisheries policy and in terms of adequate funding from the new emff.
mr president, i am among those who oppose the ratification of this agreement because it is a free trade agreement between the european union and the two countries which will do nothing but further strengthen the position of the west european monopolies. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. and let no one in this chamber tell me that there is any step in the european union's foreign policy that is not being taken primarily to serve the interests of the union, the interests of multinational capital, the interests of those who have caused the crisis in europe and are now trying to export it to these latin american countries. it will cause enormous problems in those countries. it is precisely because we read garcía márquez, mr salafranca, that we are opposed to this agreement which will lead to the exploitation of the colombian people. and in any case, because so much has been said, i would like to stress that it is not possible for the farc, which represents a very large part of the colombian people, to be included in the european union's list of terrorists and no one is interested in being removed from that list. the farc must be removed from the terrorist list.
mr president, greece is entering its sixth year of recession. 2013 is predicted to be, by analogy, the equivalent of 1942, in world war ii. today, citizens are lining up in lines for food. many children faint in schools because of lack of adequate food, and over 500,000 children are malnourished in greece. winter has arrived, and most residents cannot afford heating oil, so they live without heating. unemployment among young people aged 18 to 35 is over 55%. greece is experiencing nightmares that we all thought were in the past. my country has recently taken the necessary decisions to get back on its feet. in the name of solidarity, the european union is called upon to do its duty, understanding that behind the figures are people who have seen their lives radically changed. i would therefore ask that a programme be set up within the framework of the structural funds, and more specifically the social fund, to deal with hunger among the citizens who are suffering.
mr president, failure was avoided in doha but it is nevertheless true that, while climate negotiations are progressing, the speed of progress is not such as to allow us to hope for a climate agreement by 2015. we have no time to lose and, as the world bank report has told us, a 4°c warmer world is not only a threatening prospect, but is closing in on us very fast. the european parliament has to push more actively for progress in negotiations, not only with a resolution before next december’s climate change conference – cop 19 – in poland but in the intermediate meetings taking place in bonn and elsewhere. i call also on european citizens to speak up for climate action and progress in negotiations. if it is not an urgent social demand, it will not happen.
madam president, in the case of microbial resistance to antibiotics, which comes from their misuse, we are as weak as the weakest link. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the future of the european union's action in the field of drugs. we have voted unanimously in the committee on the environment, public health and food safety that we must preserve the effectiveness of the antibiotics we have because it is difficult to develop new ones. at the same time, we must focus on direct funding of research to develop new antibiotics because the incentives to create new antibiotics that we use as little as possible are obviously not sufficient. finally, the increasing number of hospital cases and their considerable cost are a reality. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the costs of abuse, both direct and indirect, are far too high.
madam president, the aim of the eu patent system is to further strengthen the monopoly groups in the european union's capitalist single market and, of course, in world markets by cheaper and more efficient control of intellectual property. this will increase the concentration of patents in the hands of economically powerful groups and will strengthen the monopoly of knowledge, as demanded by the most powerful multinationals in the european union. it will make it even more difficult for workers to access products and services in critical sectors such as health, medicine, energy, food, etc., which will be subject to even greater capitalist speculation. the system will establish a new patent court system based on a collection of even more reactionary legal provisions that take precedence over national law. this circumvents a number of limitations on the scope of patents currently provided for by national law. but then public social goods and scientific achievements will increasingly be commodified and inaccessible.
the debate is closed. the vote on the three reports on the european patent will take place today, tuesday 11 december, at 12 noon. in writing. although the most comprehensive form of eu cooperation is in the area of the common market, there are still many obstacles to its full implementation. the digital revolution is one of them, as new forms of trade require additional regulation and reforms in order for both businesses and consumers to benefit from the new digital opportunities. it is characteristic that the internet industry today is the most productive in western economies and even in a turbulent economic period it is constantly creating new jobs. but while in some economies, such as the us, the market represents 8% of gdp, europe has not yet fully developed and exploited this sector. the main reason is the fragmentation of the 27 national laws governing this market, which to some extent limits its further development. the present report, which i voted in favour of, attempts to identify the actions necessary to harmonize national policies and to unlock the potential and investment in the digital sector, an approach which greece should also take seriously in its efforts to recover its economy.
in writing. − europe faces today times of big challenges. the economic crisis is severely affecting our economy, our countries, our societies, our families. we also live in a time of big and unprecedented social, economic and technological developments. the so-called digital revolution has modified considerably our lives in several aspects. i voted in favour of this report because it clearly recognises that the completion of the digital single market will offer, amongst other things, possibilities for economic growth, the creation of employment, consumer opportunities, variety of products, and affordable prices to the citizens of the european union.
in writing. just yesterday, monday, the release of €750 million from the european investment bank to greece was announced. this money, together with some 700 million in loans to smes until 2015, will provide the liquidity needed by 99% of smes and enable healthy businesses to continue operating while preserving or creating new jobs. as the biggest problem facing smes at the moment is access to capital, this report, which i voted in favour of, focuses on the importance of releasing funds from both the etf and the european investment fund in order to support the operation of smes in a way that will, however, push them towards a more productive model. for greece, these initiatives are crucial and certainly in this difficult period they should be fully exploited.
in writing. − eu smes account for 75% of jobs in the private sector and, over the past five years, have contributed to the creation of around 80% of all new jobs. surprisingly enough, only 25% of smes operate beyond the national borders of the member state of their establishment and as few as 13% are active in markets outside the eu through exports. the number of smes involved in foreign direct investment is also limited. i voted in favour of this report because it understands that the eu can gain from supporting smes which can in turn provide the so-called needed growth in the hard times the eu is facing now. the report addresses key issues for smes such as access to information, access to capital, and a range of services that can be provided by the european union in assisting smes’ growth and prosperity.
in writing. the european maritime safety agency was launched immediately after the polluting sinking of the oil tanker erika, with the aim of ensuring safety and protection of the sea and preventing marine pollution. the successful operation of the agency to date suggests that its remit should be extended to include the prevention and response to environmental disasters such as oil spills. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption, and the council will be consulted on it. moreover, the foreseeable important role of the seafo in improving the training of seafarers with the long-term objective of increasing employment in the shipping sector and the exchange of best practices in the field of education and training of seafarers are elements favourable to supporting one of the strongest sectors in greece where the national economy has a comparative advantage.
in writing. the provision of a single patent protection regime, although the product of a long-standing effort in the eu, has not yet been fully achieved, although it is recognised by all member states that it can play a crucial role in the eu's economic growth and competitiveness. the economic impact of not completing it means that the validation of a european patent is now 13 times more expensive than the corresponding validation in the united states and 11 times more expensive than the corresponding validation in japan. this financial burden affects first and foremost small and mediumsized enterprises, which form the backbone of the european economy (99%) and of the greek economy. with this report, which i supported, the european parliament is putting forward proposals to overcome the obstacles to the establishment of a single patent protection regime in the eu by giving a boost to a globalised environment and more opportunities to european innovative ideas and patents.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because there is a need of an efficient patent system in europe that can make a major contribution to achieving growth through innovation. european businesses, in particular smes, need such tools to face the current economic crisis and remain globally competitive.
in writing. i voted in favour of the resolution on the growing threat of microbial resistance. without a doubt, antibiotics are essential in the treatment of many microbial infections in humans and animals. however, their unwise and excessive use in medicine and veterinary medicine has led to numerous negative consequences, such as the colonization of hospital environments with antibiotic-resistant microbes and thus an increase in hospital infections, the detection of antibiotic residues in food, and soil and water pollution. if we want to continue to use antibiotics as the main treatment for bacterial infections in both humans and animals, a new policy is needed with the following key objectives: - the correct and prudent use of antibiotics in both humans and animals on prescription only basis; - the emphasis on the prevention of microbial infections and their spread; - the development of alternative treatment methods; - the careful information and awareness-raising not only of patients but also of professionals in the field; - the control of infections and the use of antibiotics in livestock; - the prohibition of the illegal sale of antibiotics via the internet; and - the monitoring and surveillance of medicines intended for human and veterinary use.
mr president, mr commissioner, after extremely difficult and complex negotiations with the council, both in the conciliation committee and in the trialogue of 29 november, the european union now has a budget for 2013; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the declaration signed by the three presidents makes us cautiously optimistic, although this optimism will have to be demonstrated in practice. the european parliament has maintained a responsible attitude from the start of the negotiations, stressing the need to respect the european union's commitments to its citizens. however, we cannot hide our concern that the agreed level of payment appropriations for 2013 may be insufficient to cover the actual payment needs for the following year. the european parliament therefore makes clear that its positive vote on the 2013 budget is inextricably linked to the joint statements at the highest political level of the three institutions on the financing of the outstanding payments of eur 2,9 billion for 2012. we, as the european parliament, will monitor closely the implementation of the budget at all levels so that, if additional resources are needed, the member states can provide them. it is imperative, mr president, that the european union be able to pay its bills in order to maintain its credibility and solvency.
mr president, the monopoly business groups that have been established and dominate air transport in the european union are leading the way in inflating passenger and freight fares, reducing the frequency of air services, in mass layoffs, in intensifying work and in drastically reducing the wages of airline crews and ground staff, and in disrupting industrial relations, thus degrading air services as a whole. in greece, the business group that has a monopoly on the market, after the sale of olympiacos, abolished its ground services division and transferred all its staff, 850 people, to goldair handling, the largest company in the sector today, cutting wages, mass layoffs and intensifying work. the workers are fighting against this anti-people policy which erodes workers' rights and gains, makes air transport too expensive for the people, degrades the quality of services and endangers air transport safety. in the anti-people policy of the european union and governments, workers are proposing their own solution for modern, cheap air transport for the people, with full rights for workers in the industry and environmental protection.

the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial instruments are used in a way that is compatible with the common market. and yes, we would certainly prefer to introduce the tax in all the member states of the european union, but, insofar as this is not possible, the solution is not inaction, nor the hostage-taking of the many of the few. and yes, the internal market certainly has nothing to lose but to gain from moving to a first level of harmonisation of indirect taxation in the financial sector from the totally fragmented landscape that exists today. as regards the argument that jobs are being lost in a europe of 25 million unemployed because of the crisis, which is largely caused by the financial sector, to speak of a risk of unemployment because of taxation is, as the ancient greeks would have said, a 'hybride', that is to say, an insult. unless we mean the golden boys of the industry who amass mythical fortunes by betting or designing intricate and complex financial products, like the ones that brought the financial industry to the brink of collapse. if we are talking about these brilliant minds, which are being identified and absorbed by the financial sector before they have even had time to graduate from universities, these brilliant minds, dear colleagues, we must try by all means to keep them in the service of the real economy, of innovation, because only in this way will we ensure europe's competitiveness in today's globalised environment.
member of the commission. − mr president, honourable members, i would like to thank you for your strong support for a common system of ftt. this is an important signal for all member states: those participating, those considering joining the group, and those others who prefer not to participate. i have no doubt that the european parliament will give its consent tomorrow to enhanced cooperation in the area of ftt. i would like to take this opportunity to urge the council to work just as swiftly in authorising enhanced cooperation early next year. for our part, we are already working on a proposal for the ftt to be implemented under enhanced cooperation so that a new discussion on the substance can be started as soon as possible. i will, of course, keep the european parliament informed of any progress on this matter. as your rapporteur was already told, we are prepared to continue our dialogue with your economic and monetary affairs committee on possible changes to be made to our initial proposal. many of you spoke today about the substance of the proposal, which will come soon. i have to say that we do not foresee any surprises in the forthcoming proposal, because the participating member states requested that we present the substantive proposal based on our initial proposal. therefore, you will not encounter many differences between the proposals. of course, we need to adapt the proposal to the limited scope of the participating member states and take into account the debates that were held when the initial proposal was discussed. what we need now is to speed up the procedural steps so that these member states can start discussing the substance. this is crucial if the ftt is to be implemented in due course, as requested by the eu heads of state and government.

member of the commission. − mr president, in my introductory speech i was very clear that i was not happy with the level of enforcement. i did not evade that issue. indeed, i informed parliament of the measures which i think have to be implemented. to be fair, there have been infringement proceedings – 18, of which three remain open – and 14 communications to member states, of which two remain open. but i share the view of those who say that if we are not even able to enforce the rules we have today, how much more difficult it will be to enforce new legislation. i was very clear in my presentation that the commission’s report does not propose new legislation, but better enforcement. i shall promise only that which i think the commission can deliver and i can deliver. i have indicated which areas will be strengthened as regards enforcement. first of all, there will be an implementing decision next year to harmonise the member states’ reporting on controls, because we do not even have harmonisation of the controls which are carried out by the member states, which are primarily responsible for the enforcement of the 2005 regulation. there will also be a conference in dublin next year on the specific issue of rules on animal welfare and transport, and in 2013 we shall be launching a new series of study visits. these will involve not only the member states but also an auditor from the food and veterinary office (fvo), who will act as guide and accompany four experts from member states on certain traditional trade routes on visits to another member state. we will also improve the control of animal transport by making better use of navigation systems. there have been some legal problems in this respect which we will try to solve, but since we have these methods of modern technology — which do not cost much — i think we should use them. we are not using them enough because of a lack of harmonisation and the lack of will, on the part of those who are involved in the transport of animals, to use these navigation systems. i shall consider with the legal services whether there is any possibility of introducing a mandatory requirement for their use. however, my parting shot today will be this. improving enforcement should not be the final step, but the beginning of an approach which could be both reasonable and ambitious. because if everything fails, and enforcement does not work — though i think it will — we will have to reconsider our position.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow, wednesday 12 december, at 12.30 p.m.
the next item is the debate on the oral question to the commission on mediation in civil and commercial matters by mr klaus heiner lehne, deputy editor, on behalf of the committee on legal affairs (o-000169/2012 - b7-0370/2012)
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, ‘justice delayed is justice denied’, and we have a very big backlog in many of our member states. mediation is one possible means of getting rid of the backlog, especially in smaller cases where you do not necessarily go through a lengthy court procedure but, of course, access to justice is a fundamental right. i do not believe that mediation can simply replace a court procedure; it is an alternative, but in order to become a real alternative it has to be known and, as has been rightly said, it has to become a cultural choice. for this we need time. we also need to see how article 9 is being implemented. article 9 of the directive requires member states to encourage, by any means they consider appropriate, the availability to the general public of the relevant information. there we have to go in the same direction. there is also something for the commission in the text — article 10 requires us to make publicly available information on the competent courts or authorities communicated by the member states. the member states and the commission need to work hand in hand to make sure more information about mediation is available. of course, in order to be successful this approach will involve training for mediators and also for lawyers, to ensure that they do not necessarily see mediation as opposed to their professional interests. that brings me back to the need for a change of culture. this is also something which will not happen overnight. so we have to persevere, to ‘go for it’. that is why i will issue this communication in 2013 on the basis of the information we have received from the member states. publishing this information, discussing the subject again and analysing the various problems in the different member states should generate the necessary peer pressure to gradually make it clear that mediation is a valid alternative, particularly in the case of small claims, neighbourhood disputes, labour disputes and family disputes, so that people understand that this can solve their problems. to that end, of course, we have to work altogether, and i thank parliament for its very constructive position.
the debate is closed.
the next item is the report by mrs monika flašíková beňová, on behalf of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs, on the situation of fundamental rights in the european union (2010-2011) (2011/2069 (ini)) (a7-0383/2012)
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, i would like to say to mr cofferati that there is a city of human rights. graz considers itself to have become the city of human rights out of the european city of culture. it is very interesting to see how they have developed their institutions from being the european city of culture into the european city of human rights. it is not an official title, but it is a title which they care about and one which they are developing in their policy in the city. it is very interesting to look at that and to take from it good ideas about how human rights can function at a local level. i just wanted to say this as a reaction to the proposal which the honourable member made. the debate – and i do not want to enter into a party-political discussion – made it very obvious that there is a kind of frustration with the way the charter is applied. it is not applied horizontally and not to everything, and it is not applicable in situations which have no link with eu law. that can be very frustrating, and i agree with those who have underlined their frustrations. but the charter does not replace national constitutions and national systems of fundamental rights, it complements them. i believe we have at our disposal a wonderful instrument, and we have to try – even if it cannot always be applied perfectly – to apply it as much as we can and to extend its scope of application. i believe this is vital for the credibility of the legally-binding charter and for the rule of law in the european legal order. we also have the convention on human rights, which is not a european union instrument but an instrument of the council of europe. as you know, the treaty foresees the european union becoming a party to the convention on human rights. it is important that we make rapid progress towards a speedy accession. we have made it on a technical level, and it has resulted in the elaboration of a draft accession agreement. there has been very intensive work done on this. the amendments which have been brought forward are limited and do not jeopardise the overall architecture and balance of the draft accession agreement. i am convinced that, with political will and determination, it will be possible to solve the outstanding issues rapidly. i make an appeal to all member states to help us to go in this direction, because this will be an important complement to the charter of fundamental rights. to all those who are not satisfied with the limited scope of application of the charter, i would say that we should be patient and go step by step. look at the united states. it needed a hundred years and a very bloody civil war before the bill of rights was applied not only at federal level but also to individual states. this does not mean that we will need a civil war and a hundred years to get there, but we should also have the courage to go step by step and address future priorities. as i have already underlined, i believe that very slowly we are installing this culture of fundamental rights in all our institutions. it will come step by step – more quickly here, more slowly there – but we have always to control how it works. i also know – because we have already had this debate in this chamber – how frustrated many of you are that the only real strong instrument when something goes wrong is article 7 of the treaty of the european union, which is the atomic bomb. instead of going to the root of the problem in order to solve it, it is very difficult to do it in another way. in his state of the union speech the president of the commission addressed the threats to the legal and democratic fabric in some of our european states in recent months and expressed his worries. at the same time he also underlined the limits of our institutional arrangements. believe me, i would also prefer, instead of having only soft power or the nuclear option, to have something in between. those who have underlined the copenhagen deal dilemma are absolutely right, because we do have instruments before a state becomes a member of the union, but once a state is a member of the union, we no longer have these instruments. this is why i have taken the first step in developing such an instrument. this is the justice scoreboard, which is one of the tools better to help address the copenhagen dilemma but, mrs sargentini, it is only the first step. you should be very happy that i managed to put that in the system. as a first step, it will be linked to economic and social development – for civil law application – but i hope that later we will take a second and a third step and that it will become a justice scoreboard for all the applications of our justice systems. this is one of the ways in which i am trying to take a step-by-step approach in order to create, with everybody’s agreement, an instrument which might help us cope with the deficiencies of the institutional possibilities of applying the charter to its full extent. i would like to thank all those members of parliament who, for many years – for some i would say decades – have been working and fighting in order to make fundamental rights a reality, and a reality – as has been said – not only in a text but for human beings, for our citizens. so let us unite our forces and go in this direction, because i think it is an important direction. unfortunately – and i say this very clearly to mr moraes – we take a lot of very important new initiatives in this house, but until citizens understand that we have done that, we need a lot of time and a lot of energy. i hope that in the european year of citizens – the year in which we should go out in order to explain to our citizens, also about our instruments and our initiatives – that this will be of help. i hope we will all go out in order to explain what europe is about. it is about the rights of the individual. i do not think that we should act as though this europe was a space without rights. fortunately this is not the case. it is not perfect, but it is perfectible.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow, wednesday 12 december, at 12.30 p.m.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) for italy to support 512 redundancies in 10 enterprises active in the manufacture of other transport equipment. the continuing economic and financial crisis in italy has further highlighted the added value of the egf as a tool for the european union's social policy. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. the support provided is also a practical expression of the european union's solidarity. the italian government is making efforts to rationalize the budget and to cut expenditure drastically. the eib's support will be able to contribute to the fight against unemployment without significantly burdening the national budget's fiscal consolidation efforts.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) for sweden to support the redundancies at saab automobile ab and its subsidiary saab automobile powertrain ab. it is clear that the negative effects of globalisation are further highlighting the added value of the egf as a tool for the social policy of the european union. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. moreover, the support provided is the practical expression of the union's solidarity. finally, the case of sweden is the best illustration of the fact that this financial instrument does not limit its activity only to countries facing economic and budgetary challenges but can be equally useful in helping the reintegration of the displaced into the labour market even in economically strong countries. this in turn underlines the importance of this fund and demonstrates the need to maintain it during the coming multiannual financial framework 2014-2020.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report which contains some important proposals for improving statistics in the eu. taking the independence of the statisticians as a given and non-negotiable, member states should formally commit themselves to the strict application of the code of good practice for european statistics. eurostat's role in the new european statistical programme is clear and remains a primary one, but as it also draws information from the national statistical institutes, the commission should assist the statistical institutes of member states which face difficulties or are unable to produce high quality statistics due to methodological obstacles or limited resources in order to ensure that the data they provide are reliable and as accurate as possible. at the same time, in the area of transparency and accountability, eurostat should ensure that citizens can have full access to its data through easy and user-friendly procedures.
in writing. the kke voted against the european parliament's recommendation to introduce a financial transaction tax. at a time when wages and pensions are being cut to an unprecedented degree, workers' incomes are being taxed wildly and capital is being taxed less, the political staff of capital in the eu and its member states are engaged in a blatant deception of the people, presenting such a tax as 'equal sharing of the burdens of the crisis'. and this, they know very well, is not a tax on the profits of financial capital, but another indirect tax on transactions, which banking capital will pass on to the workers. the revenue from this tax will go directly to the eu funds to re-finance business groups with hot money from the eu budget. the committee on budgets has therefore rejected the kke's proposal for a 45% tax on capital. the profits of banking capital, of monopolies as a whole, for the eu and for the city governments are "sacred", inviolable and of course tax-free. it is once again proved that there is no popular way out of the capitalist crisis without a clash with the domination of the monopolies, the politics of the eu and its very structure.

on behalf of the efd group. mr president, we are talking about human rights. human rights is a moving and beautiful title of a great book with many chapters, which continues to be written every day. this book is not finished. but i fear that we are talking more than we are talking, because on the one hand there are no adequate mechanisms for the defence of human rights, and on the other hand the adverse economic and social conditions of the last four years throughout europe and the world have made it more difficult than ever to apply human rights rules and have led us to a policy of global austerity and austerity, which in turn, indirectly or immediately, has created human rights violations by depressing wages, reducing social benefits and state funds, driving large groups into poverty and social exclusion. poverty is easy to describe, but hard to live in. in 2012, the number of unemployed worldwide reached 200 million, an increase of 27 million. work is a human right that, in addition to providing a livelihood, also creates self-respect in people. the financial crisis disproportionately affects young people as new entrants to the labour market, with the result that they are the last to be hired and the first to be fired. it is particularly important to safeguard the rights of the most vulnerable groups in the population, such as children, women, the elderly, the disabled and those living in conditions of poverty and economic deprivation. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done.

president-in-office of the council. − madam president, i welcome the opportunity to participate in this debate this evening. albania is an important country in the region. we cooperate widely with albania in a range of areas and conduct extensive political dialogue. our relations are gaining in importance, reflecting the significant and growing role of albania in the region. it is fully involved, in particular, in regional initiatives such as the regional cooperation council and the south-east european cooperation process. furthermore, albania chaired the council of ministers of the council of europe during 2012. i would like to thank the parliament for its valuable contribution to this debate, and, in particular, extend my appreciation to the rapporteur, nikolaos chountis, for his valuable work. i think there is a very great deal on which the council and the parliament can agree. in its conclusions adopted last night, the council welcomed the progress made by albania to meet the 12 key priorities that were outlined in the commission’s opinion of 2010. dialogue between the government and the opposition has improved significantly, paving the way for the progress needed to fulfil the political criteria for membership of the european union. albania has met four of the key priorities and is well on the way to meeting two more. these cover, in particular, the areas of the functioning of the parliament, electoral reform and public administration reform. progress has also been achieved in other areas which remain priorities, for example: the lifting of immunities for high-level public officials and judges, and an increase in seizures of criminal assets. the council has taken note of the commission’s recommendation to grant candidate status, subject to completion of key measures in the areas of judicial and public administration reform, and revision of the parliamentary rules of procedure. with a view to deciding whether to grant candidate status, the commission has been asked to report as soon as the necessary progress has been achieved, while also taking into account further action taken by albania to fight corruption and organised crime. there are other areas where we need to see further efforts from albania. these include rule of law issues, strengthening the independence of the judiciary, as well as its efficiency and accountability, and the protection of minorities. these are all central to the country’s path towards the european union. allow me to close by referring to next year’s parliamentary elections. we all know that elections in albania have often been contested. the successful conduct of the elections in june next year will be essential for demonstrating the commitment of all of albania’s political parties to electoral reform. this will be a crucial test for the smooth functioning of the country’s democratic institutions. sustained political dialogue and continuing efforts in all the areas covered by the key priorities will remain critical to the reform process and to albania’s future progress towards the european union.
member of the commission. − madam president, i would like to thank parliament, and in particular the rapporteur, mr chountis, for his excellent report on albania. i welcome the report’s support for albania’s european perspective and the acknowledgement of the potential of the european integration process as a major driving force for reforms and cross-party political cooperation in the country. the report is largely in line with the assessment and recommendations made by the commission in its enlargement package, and it strikes the right balance between the recognition of the achievements made by albania and the emphasis on continuing the implementation of reforms in all areas under the 12 key priorities identified in the commission’s 2010 opinion on albania’s application for membership of the european union. let me turn to the 2012 commission progress report on albania. this year’s report reflects a new situation in the country which stems from the implementation of the november 2011 political agreement between government and opposition. this agreement marked the end of the political stalemate and paved the way for stronger cross-party cooperation which made a number of important reforms possible in the areas under the key priorities of the opinion. taking this progress into account, the commission recommended granting candidate country status to albania, subject to completion of key measures in the areas of judicial and public administration reform and the finalisation of the revision of the rules of procedure of parliament. we have been clear on what albania needs to do to continue progressing on its european integration path. unfortunately, the country’s political leaders have not always been able to consistently place european union integration at the top of their political agenda and have not always been willing to make the necessary political compromises that cross-party support for fundamental reforms evidently requires. the necessary conditions are still outstanding so we need to continue encouraging our albanian friends in government and opposition to focus on the european union integration process. beyond the key short-term measures i have just mentioned, albania faces many challenges, particularly in the area of the rule of law, which, as underlined in your resolution, is at the centre of the commission’s approach to enlargement. ownership of the reform process and political maturity are essential in this respect. albania will need to build on what it has already achieved and consolidate and sustain a constructive political dialogue. this is essential if it is to continue delivering on reforms. albania will also need to provide further evidence and demonstrate concrete results in fighting corruption, money laundering and organised crime. the successful conduct of parliamentary elections in june 2013 will be an essential element for albania’s european union integration perspective. we share your view on albania’s good neighbourly relations and constructive contribution to regional stability. these are essential elements of the european integration process. we need to send a strong message encouraging albania to continue with its constructive regional stance, emphasising to its political leaders the importance of clear messages promoting cooperation and good neighbourly relations. let me conclude by thanking you, honourable members, for your continued support, which is essential to the enlargement process in general and to albanian efforts. i am looking forward to the debate.
the commission is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: the european parliament's report on albania's accession prospects is being discussed almost simultaneously with the council, which is also taking the decisions mentioned by the minister, but i think that the european parliament's report is important. the report was voted in favour by the committee on foreign affairs by all the political groups. and so i want to thank all my colleagues and especially the shadow rapporteurs for their contribution and support. the report on albania's accession prospects once again underlines the fact that, since albania's accession to the european union is the wish of the albanian people, we must respect this wish and must create the incentive to tackle the problems facing this country: problems with democracy, the rule of law, corruption, minority and labour rights. in this sense, the question of integration is a matter for albanian society, not just for the albanian forces. progress has been made, but more still needs to be done. the report highlights some of these. the european union has a duty to help the people of albania, and i am sure that the european union will do so. these statements were made after the committee adopted the resolution and created tension and undermined the climate and good neighbourly relations in the region. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. they are not justified by any expediency; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject. in this sense, the european parliament and the other european institutions should stand firmly and decisively in such views in order to demand that albania's good neighbourly relations with other countries be strengthened, not only to keep alive albania's accession prospects but for the security, stability and peace in the region.
the european union is not only a political union, but also a political union of the people. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, mr houdini, on his very balanced report. it is clear that the council will rather consider that albania does not meet the conditions, that is to say, it does not meet in particular the 12 criteria set out by the european commission for albania to be able to move forward and to have a european perspective. however, this can be a catalyst for a more stable policy in albania, for a consensus between the albanian political forces and, above all, for the regulation of certain outstanding issues, such as the corruption that still exists, organised crime, money laundering and corruption in the judiciary. there are also problems in human rights issues, especially in relation to property issues, since there are internationally recognized minorities, such as the greek minority. the european parliament has also expressed its concern at the situation in albania, which is suffering from a serious economic crisis, and has called on the commission to take action to help the country's economy. today an attempt was made to change that comment and it made things worse. the european union has a duty to ensure that the albanian people are not excluded from the european union, and that the albanian people are not excluded from the european union. it is obvious that the albanian prime minister made this move of a nationalist nature in anticipation of the council's negative decision for his country. he needs to be given a message that albania will not lose the game on the outside if it continues like this, but it has a good chance of losing it on the inside.
the council and the european parliament's resolution shall assess albania's accession process against the criteria of the maastricht, copenhagen and lisbon treaties, the four freedoms of capital, the europe 2020 strategy of the european union; the european union has been trying to impose a new economic policy on albania, which is based on the principle of the free movement of capital, and the economic reforms that are necessary to ensure the penetration of european monopolies in albania, the brutal exploitation of workers and of the resources of production - oil, gas and minerals - and at the same time the strengthening of the bourgeois political system, the repressive mechanisms for the manipulation of the workers' movement. the nationalist and chauvinist preachings and policies - the 'golden rule' - of the bourgeoisie are being exploited to exploit the peoples of the wider balkans. we express the radical opposition of the communist party of greece to albania's accession to the european union. the people are opposed to the integration of new states into the european union. the struggle for disengagement from the european union is inextricably linked to the overthrow of the power of the monopolies of popular power and economy, to the development of relations of friendship and cooperation between peoples on the basis of mutual benefit.
