answer "blue card". it is clear that the albanian people's opinion is the dominant one in the choice they will make. but we all, as political parties, have a responsibility to stand up for albania and for other countries. the same reasons we have for opposing greece's accession to the european union are the same reasons we have for opposing albania.
president-in-office of the council. − madam president, i am grateful to parliament for raising this important issue this evening. the council shares the concerns that many of you have about the seriousness of the situation in syria and in particular its impact on the civilian population. we continue to monitor developments as we have been doing since the beginning of the crisis, focusing in particular on the issue of humanitarian assistance to syrian nationals who are victims of the crisis. many of them have been obliged to leave their homes and are currently displaced in other parts of syria or across the border in neighbouring countries such as turkey, jordan, lebanon and iraq. this issue has been addressed both at political and technical level and some of these discussions have involved representatives of international organisations such as the unhcr and the iom which are the most active in syria. in october the council confirmed the continuing commitment of the european union and its member states to provide humanitarian assistance to the affected population, but it also emphasised the need for further funds and called for all donors to increase their contributions to the latest un appeals for funding humanitarian assistance and refugee assistance. the european union and its member states are together providing the largest contribution to the regional response plan for syrian refugees launched by the unhcr and other organisations in september. to date, this assistance has reached a total of about eur 222 million, amounting to 53% of the overall international response. the council has also been aware of the potential challenges to the member states of increasing migratory pressure from nationals fleeing from syria. the issue has been discussed by ministers on several occasions, and at the most recent meeting of the justice and home affairs council last week the council had the opportunity to hear a presentation from the united nations high commissioner for refugees setting out his latest assessment of the situation. while the overall numbers of syrian nationals who have fled to member states has so far remained relatively low, some member states have begun to see a noticeable increase in the number of asylum applications. this is a trend which is likely to continue. given that there are no signs that the internal crisis in syria is likely to be resolved in the near future, the council is also taking action to prepare for the medium- and long-term challenges which could result from a further increase in migratory pressure. in october the council endorsed a proposal from the commission to establish a regional protection programme. this programme is designed to cope with the specific situation in syria, drawing on the experiences of similar programmes elsewhere. it will help enhance the capabilities of the authorities and of the organisations dealing with international protection and refugee issues and will provide lasting solutions as regards the plight of those who have been forced to leave the country. plans for the establishment of a regional protection programme are under way, with these scheduled to become fully operational in spring 2013. the council is concerned both at the deterioration of the political situation in syria and at the effects that this is having on civilians. we will continue to do what we can to try to bring an end to the crisis, and to address the specific problems encountered by those fleeing from it. this must involve further humanitarian assistance as well as the establishment of an effective regional protection programme. we stand ready to take any further action that might be needed to cope with the problems of migratory pressures as and when developments in syria so require.
member of the commission. − madam president, this is indeed a very important debate. as you know, nearly half a million people have sought refuge in the neighbouring countries from the atrocities in syria. turkey, lebanon, jordan and iraq have made formidable efforts to give those people shelter and more people are likely to flee. the red crescent reports that some 2.5 million people are internally displaced. in addition to syrian refugees there are also a large number of people who used to live, or had sought refuge, in syria, such as palestinians and iraqis, and have now become displaced or been forced to flee. as the president of the council said, the eu’s response has been very strong from a humanitarian point of view. we have mobilised more than eur 230 million so far, which is being used to provide healthcare, food, shelter, water, sanitation, heating and registration to the refugees and support to the communities hosting them. we will continue to provide this support to the host communities as long as the crisis continues. normal funds are being, and will be, mobilised to address needs as they appear. regarding the question of a regional protection programme, the exact modalities, which have still to be decided, are being prepared with the relevant partners, such as unhcr. that will play an important role in the eu’s response. a regional protection programme is, as you know, a specific tool of long-term capacity building in the area of international protection that aims at institution-building as well as supporting sustainable solutions for refugees. a resettlement commitment, whereby eu member states undertake on a voluntary basis to offer resettlement places in their countries, accompanies each regional protection programme. the european refugee fund will provide co-financing for pledged resettlement cases. the current rules under the eu resettlement programme allow member states to choose to allocate a number of places under their planned pledging for the resettlement of refugees from a country in a region designated for a regional protection programme or for emergency resettlement. it is for each member state to decide individually whether they wish to make use of this possibility, but i called on member states at the last ga (general affairs) council to do their utmost to respond to this. the commission is urging member states to show generosity and solidarity. not very many syrian refugees have come to europe so far – around 16 000, which is not a very large number considering the huge number of people who have left the country. but nevertheless these people are asking for shelter in the european union, and that number could grow. we have taken action to prepare eu member states to deal with the consequences. these issues have been discussed in several meetings with ga ministers. we have also had meetings organised by the european asylum support office (easo) in recent months. this has allowed member states to exchange views on the goal of aligning as far as possible their policies in response to asylum requests from syrian nationals. eu member states have confirmed their intention to provide adequate protection, in particular by not returning syrian nationals staying in the eu to syria. there has been a wide consensus among member states that the majority of syrian applicants could be entitled to receive international protection, either as a refugee as defined in the geneva convention, or as a beneficiary of a subsidiary form of protection. this is confirmed in practice by the recognition rate for syrian nationals throughout the eu of approximately 92% in the second quarter of 2012. it is important for this to be standard in the european union. in order to prepare for the possibility of a rising influx, a constant monitoring exercise is being carried out by dg home affairs, including the different relevant agencies and entities – easo, frontex, europol, other commission departments, the member states of course, unhcr and other organisations in the area. this is an ongoing monitoring process; it has ensured that we have constant updates on the situation so that we can react very swiftly if the situation deteriorates to the point of placing additional pressure on our asylum system. easo has offered to stand by to be ready to provide assistance to member states facing particular pressure. we have also asked those potential member states to identify their needs and communicate them to easo. we have received in this connection a letter from cyprus as a pre-warning of potential crisis. at the request of the member states affected, up to eur 9.8 million will be available under the european refugee fund in 2013 to assist in addressing situations of particular pressure created by the sudden arrival of a number of persons who might be in need of international protection and thereby place exceptionally heavy and urgent demands on the reception facilities and asylum systems of the member states concerned. in the event of a massive influx of displaced persons into the eu, the commission is prepared to propose using the temporary protection directive mechanism where appropriate. this would be the last resort. the numbers fall far short of that at present, and we will not use the mechanism until it is really necessary. for the moment, while some countries are under pressure, they are able to deal with applications from syrians. a proposal to activate the temporary protection mechanism would, of course, have to be approved by member states. the commission is already active in providing help to syria’s neighbours in their efforts to cope with the refugees who have fled the in-fighting in that country. we will also continue to be vigilant regarding any possible influx of refugees into the eu, and stand ready to support and assist member states and the refugees through the relevant agencies and other forms of cooperation.
i am speaking on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, i thank the president-in-office of the council and the commissioner for informing us of the initiatives taken so far. the truth is that we are talking about the preparation of europe. but it's important to look at -- and we actually heard today in the introductory presentation what's been done so far -- what's been done so far and how we're continuing the effort because the crisis is ongoing, the flows are ongoing and of course even as we speak syrian citizens are still leaving syria. i would like to add to the figures provided by the president-in-office of the council and the commissioner that, according to the un high commissioner, every day there are 3,000 more syrians leaving the country, while 40% of the 500,000 people who have left syria so far are not in the camps, and it is a given that in the coming period, over time and with the heavy winter, we will have flows south and west of syria. i would like to make two main points: the first is that we must consider how we can support all the efforts in the region, how we can best support both the organizations there and the third neighbouring countries, such as turkey and lebanon and the other countries mentioned. and we must of course consider whether this effort is sufficient - we have heard that more funding is needed - and ensure that the money that has been given so far is used in the best possible way. we also need to map the pathways that they're developing. we have been reading in recent days that turkey is now preventing the flows. turkey is no longer accepting any more refugees from syria because it has no other options. we must see how the syrians move next and in what direction they continue their march. and the second very important issue is how do we continue our own strategy. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the european union's citizens are not excluded from the european union's external borders. one of those countries is greece, which is already receiving flows from syria. the numbers aren't very big. from august to october there were about 600, as far as i know, and many of them say they don't want to apply for asylum, that they want to return once the crisis is over to their home country, where their livelihood is, their family. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. but one country, or two or three, from the south, which are, because of their geographical position, in the front line, cannot respond on a european level. the european union must take the initiative in this period. which he didn't do during the arab spring. in the arab spring, we were fragmented. the european union has a duty to ensure that the schengen area is not used as a barrier to entry for citizens of third countries. let's not make the same mistakes. let us ask all the countries, all 27, since we have a massive influx of refugees from syria, to take all their responsibilities. we have the shining example of the eurema pilot project in malta. it is a very good example to persuade all member states at this time to take responsibility and to host and provide the humanitarian aid that is needed for the people who will arrive from syria until the crisis is over. it's the last night we'll talk this year. may 2013 bring better days for the syrians as well.
the question is "blue card". in her final report, the honourable member highlighted the hard work of the greek government, in cooperation with frontex, the european commission and easo, to deal with the flows of nationals from third countries to the greek border. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the european parliament is able to take part in the negotiations on the accession of the new member states to the european union. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter.
madam president, minister, commissioner, it is a fact that the european union has been present in the syrian crisis from the very beginning by supporting human rights and international law, by imposing sanctions on the regime, by preventing barbarity, by humanitarian aid, by communicating with the opposition and encouraging a coordinated peaceful and effective role, and most recently by recognising the alliance of opposition groups as the legitimate representative of the syrian people. but where are we today? fear of tomorrow, insecurity, is growing; the violence and the waves of flight too. the hundreds of syrian refugees in the neighbouring countries have already been mentioned by my colleagues, and apart from the problems of living conditions and the humanitarian problems of the syrian refugees, which concern us, there is also the difficulty of absorbing and providing for the basic needs of the countries that are receiving them, plus the political tensions and the difficulty of religious balance that is created in the host countries. the european union is not only a political union, but also a political union of the peoples of europe. i wanted to address the european commission and the council because i do not feel that there is a strategy at the moment, in view of the wave of displacement that is already taking place, and on the other hand there are countries that are particularly affected, such as my country greece, and are expected to be hit even more, in view of the possible developments. we all know that a nato intervention is possible, and the use of chemical weapons by the syrian regime is possible. i think that the events in our region are serious, tragic, with political and humanitarian implications. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the situation in the region is not affected by the crisis. i believe that a coherent and multifaceted strategy is needed, commensurate with the circumstances and the urgency of the situation.
madam president, we have a moral and legal obligation as the european union to welcome these people who are suffering from the developments in syria and who need us now without the twists and turns and the asterisks. i think that's clear. but let me mention my country, greece. we are asking a vulnerable member of our family, greece, to take full responsibility and to clarify: vulnerable member not because of the economic crisis facing greece but because it already bears a huge part of the burden of the migratory flow to europe. i am not ignoring the considerable financial and technical assistance which the commission is giving us. i should like to thank the commissioner on this point, because we feel that she is our ally in the european parliament and your proposal to create the asylum and migration fund is an injection of solidarity, if i may say so, and the council must move quickly and not hesitate to accept this proposal. the solution is simple, i think, if there is the political will. a refugee coming to europe must face a single european asylum system. in such a system, let me say, there is no place for the dublin regulation, which is absurd, which increases tensions between member states and, with all due respect, let me say, which also gives rise to a kind of hypocrisy on the part of some partners on the issues of asylum and illegal immigration. we must therefore give refugees who come to europe the possibility of going to any country they wish and not being trapped in greece because it happens to be on the external borders of our union. this is unfair to the refugees, it is unfair to greece.
madam president, it is a fact that the european union must show greater solidarity with the people fleeing their country, syria, because of the barbaric attacks and bombings of civilians. the countries neighbouring syria have taken a big share of the responsibility and we have to think that they may not be able to afford to take in larger waves of refugees; they're already at the tipping point, but winter is coming. it is likely that many people will be forced to leave the places where they are today, in tents, so i think we should not wait until spring to plan for a mass influx of refugees. i've personally visited some islands in greece and i see that there are people coming from syria who are not registered as refugees at the moment, because they are looking for other ways to reunite with their families. we must therefore consider that the question is not only how much money there is but also whether there is adequate preparation by, among others, the countries in the periphery of europe and greater participation in the shared responsibility by all european countries. greece and other countries will not be able to cope with the management of refugees on their own and we must be ready well before spring; i would say, in the next few weeks, in the next few months, we need to have plans for perhaps a massive influx of refugees into europe.
in writing. following the events in syria, the number of illegal migrants to the north aegean islands has increased significantly in recent times, and there is also an increase in the number of illegal migrants crossing the bulgarian-turkish land border. according to official figures from the greek ministry of foreign affairs, there is a phenomenon of buying visas and using forged syrian documents, which has increased in recent times. we are afraid of a repetition of the situations we have seen in the past with impoverished migrants arriving in europe from its southern borders. in greece there is no adequate infrastructure to accommodate them. the protection of the human rights of migrants must be the result of eu policies and practices, there is a need for solidarity and responsibility-sharing. the current negotiation for dublin 3 is essential to remove the element of responsibility for the country of first entry of illegal immigrants. the transformation of the current conflicts into a general civil war should be addressed as a possibility by the eu member states jointly, through the activation of the european directive on displaced persons.
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, fundamental values and rights are indivisible. they are the constitutional foundations on which our societies and our europe base themselves. they should be the compass for all our actions at regional, national and european levels. they should be the compass for respecting the rights of all our citizens in all policy domains – social, economic and security. the discussions in the council have shown that certain member states oppose the inclusion of the former third pillar areas, which no longer exist, in the fra’s multiannual framework as a matter of principle. their position would be the same if we had put forward a proposal to modify the fra regulation, and that is why i am convinced that it is unnecessary and even counterproductive. i would like, as a principle, and as a message to those member states and to some members of this house, to underline the following. we cannot ignore the entry into force of the lisbon treaty and of the binding charter of fundamental rights. perhaps, during the christmas break, reading our treaties and fundamental texts would help some ministers and some members of parliament to understand the context and the significance of the basic rules on which our common fate is based.

the commission is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: mr president, commissioner, let us now talk about growth in europe, since that is what the questions have been about. the commission has already proposed a number of measures to help the regions of the community to develop their economies in response to the recession, a recession of unprecedented proportions in the whole of europe since the second world war. the images of certain countries in the south in particular, with high unemployment, with malnourished children, with families losing their homes, are familiar to you. the answer, of course, is growth. but the policies that are being followed interpret the crisis differently. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment is a very important one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area. the situation has become more general in greece, which was used as a test bed for the implementation of these policies to overcome the crisis. one country after another is moving towards memorandum policies, austerity policies. the results for the whole of europe are known. i do not mention them; the commission is not prepared to accept this. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. point one: the multiannual financial agreement - i am referring to greece and other countries obviously - in a country which has lost 25% of its gdp due to the recession, which has 26% unemployment, the esdp is cut in half. the commission contends that the regulations do not allow it to do otherwise. but those regulations you could change. how can such a country and, obviously, other countries expect a growth boost? point two: the honourable member referred to the reforms being implemented in the countries of the south. what does he mean by that ? the policy of fiscal consolidation and competitiveness has a direct impact on growth. the example is very specific: in greece, despite internal devaluation, despite wage cuts, firms are leaving because of limited demand and because of a european policy that increases risk. so these policies are ineffective. we must pursue different policies; we are not going to achieve growth with them.

commission but also to the european council which will be meeting shortly in brussels. the issue of growth is certainly not the subject of the european council, but we must not forget that growth is in writing. the agency, which was set up in 2007, has not yet fulfilled its role and developed its potential, as the european parliament points out. the report, which i voted in favour of, finds that the next multiannual financial framework ensures adequate funding for the agency to further develop its scope of action in important areas such as social rights, in particular with regard to the independent monitoring of the implementation of the un convention on the rights of persons with disabilities (crpd). however, its operation should be in the spirit of the provisions of the treaty of lisbon on complementary actions in these areas.
